# MPFree pad player: layout spec

**Status:** APPROVED by Jay on 2026-11-11 at 08:15Z ("Approve, Synth before Looper"). Every thread works to this. Any change to it needs Jay's approval again.
**Owner:** the app thread ("Build app rounds and own the layout"; it was the "Hello MPC" thread until Jay renamed the app mpfree, 22:36Z). Send layout proposals there.
**Shared layout means:** the top bar, the tab set and its order, the Play page's structure, the right-hand column, the pad grid, the hint line, Prefs, and the global buttons (MENU, SHIFT + MENU, STOP, SHIFT + STOP, the Console's chord, NOTE REPEAT, the mode buttons).

## 1. The screen

- **Size:** 1280 × 800, landscape.
- **Every page, top to bottom:**
  - the top bar, 60 px;
  - the time engine's transport bar, on pages that use it;
  - the page's own content;
  - the hint line, 26 px, which says what the buttons do on that page. When a red System status problem starts, it says so once, in red, for 4 s: the problem's title and "Tap the status chip to see why." (Jay's approval of the System status plan, 22 Nov 2026 08:35Z). A red problem that keeps happening says it again only after a minute; a yellow one never does. Nothing else about the hint line changes. **While a setting waits for the XY pad** (section 4o's banner; Jay's approval of the XY mockups, 23 Nov 2026 10:34Z ("Approve, start phase 2"; canvas https://claude.ai/artifact/X2A8nsHiwCf6S6PuZQDq4n, pictures in mpfree-claude `xy-controller/mockups/`), board 4), a 48 px banner takes the hint line's place on every page, reaching up into the margin above it, so no page loses room.
- **Full-screen pages** (Loading, Touch lab, Calibrate touch) have no top bar. Any page change or MENU closes them.
- **Touch targets** (Jay after live test 3, 13 Nov): anything tapped answers over its whole visible button, and packed rows (pattern lanes, list rows) answer over the row's full pitch with no dead gap between them. A mute is at least the width of the name it belongs to, never a bare 32 px icon. Where a control can't be that big, the wheel and – / + reach it.
- **Typing a number** (Jay after live test 3: "Warp tempo (and other numeric fields with large range) should have ability to enter a number, instead of only being able to use dial"): a field that can be typed has a dotted line under its value, and a tap on the value opens the number pad over the page, under the top bar (keys 0 to 9, the point and the minus where the field takes them, Delete, Cancel and a two-row Enter, every key at least 64 px). It shows the range and the value now; Enter out of range keeps the pad open and says the range in red; Cancel or a change of page leaves the field as it was. The wheel and – / + step the number, and the wheel's push is Enter. Fields so far: the Sample tab's Warp tempo (the Warp tool) and the tempo (hold Play's BPM). A typed value is one undo step.
- **Typing a name** (the sampler review's R1: "No names. There is no way to type one"; Jay, 17 Nov 15:20Z, on the `sample-name` screen: "Your text input box looks good. It would be nice to be able to input more symbols though"): a name that can be typed has a dotted line under it, and a tap on it opens the text keyboard over the page, under the top bar, as the number pad does (the `Type a name` page, `mpfree_ui::textpad`). Its head says what is named, the name now and how long a name may be (32 characters; 24 for a port), with a switch at its right when there is more than one name to type (Pad and Sound, 168 × 64 px each, the one showing outlined). Under it the name, large, with the cursor; then five rows of ten places: the digits; Q to P; A to L and Delete; Shift, Z to M, the hyphen and the apostrophe; Cancel (two places), Clear, #+=, Space (three), the point and Enter (two, in the accent). #+= turns the letter keys into symbols (! @ # $ % ^ & * ( ) ~ ` = + [ ] { } ; : " < > ? / |), outlined, until it is pressed again (it reads ABC then); a new keyboard starts with letters. Every key is at least 64 px. The name starts as it is, with the cursor at its end, so Delete takes off its last letter and Clear the lot. Shift is for one letter (on by itself in an empty name, outlined while on) and shows the digits as ! @ # $ % & ( ) _ + and the point as a comma; the keys show what they type. A key that can't type (the name is full, nothing before the cursor) is drawn as a button that can't be used. The wheel and – / + move the cursor, and the wheel's push is Enter. Enter with a name left empty keeps the keyboard open and says so in red; Cancel or a change of page changes nothing. BROWSE has its own keyboard for its names (Rename, New folder, Save as, Search), the same size; its Sets place types a set's name there (32 characters) and a set song's **Notes** (240 characters, the field showing the last three lines and the count; section 4o). Names so far: the Sample tab's pad and its sound (a tap on the pad bar's title, on Wave, with any tool, and on Edit pad; the names typed are one undo step), and a USB MIDI device (Name on its port page in the I/O view, 24 characters at most so it fits Routing's columns; the 5-pin jacks keep the panel's MIDI In and MIDI Out; a rig setting, as the rest of the port page, so not on UNDO: typing its own name takes the given one off).
- **Button labels** (Jay after live test 3: "light gray on a medium gray button is no bueno"): a label on a `raised` fill is `text`, or `text_dim` while the button can't be used (`Theme::button_ink`); `text_faint` is not for labels. `Theme::check` holds both inks at 4.5:1 on `raised` in every theme, and the built-in themes are tested against it.

## 2. The top bar

- **Tabs** start at the left edge. There is no MPFree wordmark: that belongs to the startup and loading screens only (Jay, 08:02Z).
- **The status chip** (System status: Jay's message of 22 Nov 2026 08:17Z, "CPU / RAM on one fixed size status chip that is green/yellow/red depending on resource usage and error status", his approval of the plan, 08:35Z, and of this text, 09:55Z; doc https://claude.ai/artifact/Re2JmUJFbmrg74hj8wCEyR; [status.md](status.md)) sits at the right end of the chips, beside the folder icon, where the CPU chip was. It replaces the CPU, RAM and session chips.
  - It always reads the same two numbers, "CPU 24 %  RAM 38 %", and never a problem's name: problems are read on System status (section 2a). It is as wide as "CPU 100 %  RAM 100 %" (about 200 px), so a number that changes width never nudges the chips beside it (Jay, 18 Nov 2026 07:28Z).
  - Its colour is the worst of three things: the CPU (yellow once the sound has been near its limit for 1 s, red only when the sound dropped out), the RAM (yellow over 70 %, red over 85 %) and the problems on the System status board (a yellow or red problem happening now). A number past its line takes that colour too, so plain numbers on a coloured chip mean a problem on System status. Grey, with "CPU -", until the sound is running.
  - A problem that has ended keeps the chip in its colour as a ring until System status is opened; one still going keeps it filled after it is seen. It never flashes, never opens anything by itself, and is the same in Make and Perform.
  - A tap opens System status; the chip is outlined in the accent while it shows, as the folder icon is lit while the browser shows. A tap again, MENU or any tab closes it.
- **Mode chips** sit on its left: level, tempo, and the arp chip. They say how the pads play, not whether anything is wrong. The session chip's states are System status problems now: No reply and Stopped are red ("The pads aren't making sound"), and No session, playing without Akai's setup, is yellow ("The pads started without Akai's setup"). There is **no kit chip** (the Play, Pads and Sequencer redesign, step 4, Jay's approval 18 Nov 00:30Z): what the pads play is on Play's transport (section 4), and the list of kits opens from there and from the Pads tab's **Kit** (section 4e). It was the kit's name and bank, "Classic House · Bank A", from live test 3 until then.
  - Chips never push tabs off the bar, and tabs never push chips off.
  - **Where the bar has no room for every chip** (the phrase player's pull request, 23 Nov 2026; approved by Jay, 23 Nov 2026 19:22Z: "Yes, tempo off when crowded" on the pull request's card for the top bar): the arp chip leaves its tempo off first, since the tempo is on the transport bar ("Phrase: Night drive, latched · 120 BPM" reads "Phrase: Night drive, latched"). If it still has no room, the end of its name is cut with "…"; it keeps its place and its tap. Any other chip with no room is left out, from the left end. Until this, a chip with no room was left out whole with every chip after it, so a long arp chip vanished, or stayed and pushed MAKE or PERFORM out.
  - **The list of kits** (Jay's card on the UI audit thread, 13 Nov 14:57Z), from what the pads play on Play's transport or the Pads tab's Kit, opens over the page: every kit, then the synth (as the wheel steps them), then **New kit** while one of the empty user kits is left, which puts it on the armed track (sampler R3 part 3, Jay's decision 1, 16 Nov 2026; a user kit is listed once a track plays it or it has sounds), beside a card with **Back**. A tap on a kit loads it and closes the list; Back or any tab closes it and keeps the kit. The hint line says so while it is open. **A tap on the Full level or Half level chip** turns that level off, as FULL LEVEL again would (the same card).
  - The time-left chip only shows: a tap says on the hint line what it shows.
  - **The "Pads: steps" chip** (sequencer decision 2, Jay's card "Pads become steps, as designed", 16 Nov 2026 20:00Z; mockup I, `step-pads.png` in the sequencer design notes): while STEP SEQ has the pads it reads "Pads: steps · Kick" (the sound's name, then "· Follow" while Follow is on), warn, where the 16 Levels chip goes; while STEP SEQ is held it reads "Pads: pick a sound". A tap gives the pads back, as STEP SEQ again would.
  - **The "Pads: Poly" chip** (step 3 of the doc "Instruments, pads and tracks: the redesign", https://claude.ai/artifact/A2iE1bDqPxk2uSJABCgTJg, rule 2 and the step's "\"Pads: Poly\" on screen with Follow track", Jay's yes of 20 Nov 2026 11:16Z; *proposed, waits for Jay's approval with the pull request*): while a synth is chosen for the pads (the Synth tab's Play Poly on the pads, section 4g), on every page, it reads "Pads: Poly" (the synth's name), warn, among the pad chips; a tap opens **What the pads play** (below; sampler step 15e, question 1 of the canvas "Pads and kits: step 15e", approved by Jay 23 Nov 2026 16:11Z; before it, a tap was Follow track), whose first row holds Follow track. Play's transport line reads "Pads: Poly · C3 to D#4" in the warn colour while it shows, whichever track Play's rows show.
  - **What the pads play** (sampler step 15e; the canvas "Pads and kits: step 15e", https://claude.ai/artifact/VDrYTMxR5ACoeJpUjUYZbH, board 3; approved by Jay 23 Nov 2026 16:11Z, "Build as drawn, with your picks"): the Pads chip's tap shows the Synth tab with this panel in its middle, where the list of synths opens; the chip again, **Done** (120 px, at the 64 px heading's right, under "What the pads play" over "Only a pick, Follow, UNDO or opening a project changes it") or another tab closes it. Two columns. Left: **BASE · EVERY BANK**, a 50 px row with what it plays ("Follow track" over "Track 1's kit: Boom Bap"; a kit in its colour or a synth, "A whole kit · Track 1 keeps what it plays") and its buttons, each sized to its words, **Follow track** (while it isn't) and **Pick** (the list, for every bank); then **BANKS**, a row each for A to H, its letter in a 26 px box: "The base" faint with nothing of its own; its own kit or synth ("Big Kit" over "From its bank C · you are on B") with **Follow base**; a missing one in the warn colour ("Missing: instrument 9" over "Plays the base meanwhile") with **Replace** and **Remove**. The bank the pads are on is raised and outlined in the accent, its letter box in the accent. A tap on a bank's row opens the list for that bank alone, whichever bank the pads are on. Right: **LAYERS · 2 OF 4**, a row each with where it plays as its first button (**Every bank**, or **Bank D**; a tap moves it between every bank and the bank the pads are on) and **Remove**, then **+ Add a layer** (the list with Layer on) while there is room; under them **UNDO**, a box with "Last change here: bank B got Big Kit, from its bank C." over "UNDO takes it back. Each pick, Follow and Remove is one step.", and, when an entry is missing, a box outlined in the warn colour, "1 missing" over what the project names and what plays meanwhile. **UNDO** with the panel open takes back the last change to the pad map (it has no redo). The list opened from the panel comes back to it, with Keep or Cancel. The hint line reads "Tap a row: pick its sound · Follow base: the bank plays the base again · UNDO: the last change here · Done or a tab: close".
  - **The Controller chip** (MIDI powerhouse 3.10; Jay's card, 18 Nov 2026 01:21Z, "A Controller switch in I/O": "the top bar says so"): while controller mode is on (section 4d) it shows among the status chips (where the kit chip was until the Play, Pads and Sequencer redesign), since the pads don't play the kit then. Warn: "Controller → Juno-X · ch 3 · A" (the port, the channel and the pads' bank), or why nothing goes ("Controller: pick its port on I/O", "Controller → Juno-X: not plugged in", "Controller: MIDI out is off in Prefs"). A tap turns controller mode off. Built; Jay confirmed its place (card, 18 Nov 2026 07:19Z, "Keep all three as built").
- **The MAKE / PERFORM chip** (sequencer decisions 4 and 5, Jay 16 Nov 2026: Make and Perform as designed; the sequencer design's "Make and Perform" section, mockup K): at the left of the status chips, it reads MAKE, or PERFORM with the second accent colour's dot. A tap switches, as SHIFT + MAIN does, and the hint line says what changed. The project keeps it (a new project is in Make), and switching never stops or changes anything playing. What Perform switches is listed with MAIN (section 6), TRACK MUTE (section 6) and REC's arm. **Rec song with REC** (S4's fourth part): in Perform the take REC records (the notes, their count-in, or a loop's pass) turns Rec song on for its length, unless it is on already; the REC chip adds "+ Rec song" ("REC → Loops C + Rec song"), and the target list's switch reads **Rec song with REC** and takes it off for the next take, or for the take recording (it is back for the take after). **Perform's guard** (S4's fourth part): on-screen buttons that delete or clear (the Song view's Delete and an audio region's Erase, the note editor's Delete, the Looper tab's Clear, a layer's × and ½, Play's loop strip's Clear, the step inspector's Clear, the song list's Remove) need a half-second hold in Perform: a red fill crosses the button from the left, and when it is across the button acts; let go early, or slide off, and nothing happens. The hint line says "Hold to delete". In Make they take one tap, and UNDO brings them back; ERASE + a tap and the wheel's push are as before. Perform's grid and NEXT: section 4f.
- **Width:** up to 6 tabs are drawn at full width (112 px). More tabs share that room, each as wide as its name (about 11 px a letter and 22 px more, at least 72 px), all narrowed alike to fit: the 8 music tabs run from 68 px (Play, Pads, Mix) to 116 px (Sequencer), and with the Dev tab from 61 to 104 px (the Play, Pads and Sequencer redesign, 18 Nov; before it the 7 tabs were 96 px each).
- **Active tab:** the page's own tab is highlighted; the Mix tab stays lit while an effect chain is open. Prefs, menus, the macros panel and the guide highlight none.
- **No mixer icon:** the Mix tab took the small sliders icon's job (Jay's card, 14 Nov 07:15Z; before that the icon opened the mixer, Jay 09:17Z).
- **The folder icon:** a small folder at the bar's right edge, after the chips, in the I/O icon's place (the Play, Pads and Sequencer redesign, approved by Jay 18 Nov 00:30Z; doc https://claude.ai/artifact/KHBG7mNbDFU4uZCFqNp9dW). A tap opens the browser over the page, as BROWSE does (section 3, Not tabs), and the icon is lit while it shows; the icon again, BROWSE or a tab closes it.
- **The I/O view** is the Mix tab's: the project's routing (audio ins and outs, MIDI ports, clock and sync, CV), saved with the project. The Mix tab's I/O chip opens it, and a tap on the tempo chip opens it at Sync. (Jay's picks on the MIDI thread's cards, 12 Nov 08:38Z and 08:39Z; MIDI and sync are project settings, not Prefs, 08:26Z. The FX thread owns the mixer and its frame; the MIDI thread builds the MIDI, sync and CV sections.)

## 2a. System status

System status (the plan's page; mockups in /mnt/project-files/status-monitor/mockups/) opens over the page, under the top bar, as Prefs and the browser do. It is not a tab, and none is highlighted while it shows. The status chip and the Prefs row System status (section 5) open it; the chip again, MENU or any tab closes it. Opening it marks every problem seen.

- **The head**, one line in the worst problem's colour: "Everything is working", or the worst problem's title, with how many problems are happening now and earlier. On its right, four meters with their numbers: CPU, RAM, the stick's free space and the MPC's temperature (each shown once MPFree can read it).
- **The systems, on the left:** always all nine, in this order: Sound, Memory, Storage, Pads and buttons, MIDI and sync, Project, Screen, Plugins, The MPC. Each row has a dot in its colour (a grey ring for a system not in use), a line of plain words ("41 % used · 1.1 GB free for sounds", "Juno-X isn't plugged in") and a badge with its problems' count. The worst system is picked when the page opens.
- **The picked system, on the right:** its problems as cards, worst and newest first, then the system's own numbers. A card happening now shows in full: its title, what happened, what to try, a button where MPFree can do it (one undo step where it changes the project) and one small line of numbers. One that has ended folds to a line (title, when, the start of what happened) and opens with a tap. One card per kind of problem, with how many times and when ("3 times · last 12 s ago · first at 21:14").
- **Now | This session**, a switch at the top right: only what is happening, or everything since MPFree started.
- **Save a report**, a button at the foot of the cards panel, on the right, on every system (Jay's pick, 23 Nov 2026 11:43Z): it saves one plain text file of this session's problems, the page's numbers and the last status lines to the stick (`files/reports/`), and the hint line says where it went. The cards and the numbers stop above it.
- **Controls:** a tap on a system picks it; the wheel steps the systems and its push opens the picked card; – / + do the same; a card's button does its job, and Save a report saves one. The hint line says so.

## 3. The tabs

The music tabs, in this order:

| Tab | What it is | Owner thread |
|---|---|---|
| Play | Kits, level, timing, the pattern, the loop, throws; Note Repeat / arp | App (arp inside it) |
| Sequencer | The armed track's clip as **Steps** (the pattern, the Your timing strip and the step inspector, which were Play's Beats view) or **Notes** (the note editor, which was Arrange's); STEP SEQ opens it (section 4m; the Play, Pads and Sequencer redesign, Jay 18 Nov 00:30Z) | App (the sequencer's engines: transport / time) |
| Looper | The loops' editor and setup (mockup J): the loops, each layer's mix, recording and input, using a loop | App (the looper engine: transport / time) |
| Sample | The sound editor: one page, Wave, with Send to pad and Chop to pads (Jay, 07:12Z; its Pads sub-tab moves to the Pads tab, 12 Nov 15:46Z; its FX sub-tab goes and Wave gains the pad's Sound section, the one Sample page, Jay 18 Nov 11:46Z; its Grain sub-tab goes, Grain being a tool on Wave, the one Sample page's step 5; so does its Warp sub-tab, Warp being a tool on Wave, step 4; its Rec sub-tab goes, Wave's recorder row recording, step 2f; so does its Chop sub-tab, Slice being a tool on Wave, step 3; and its Play sub-tab, which moves to the Pads tab as Edit pad, step 6b) | Sampler |
| Synth | The synth's pages (Poly, later FM) | Synth |
| Arrange | The time engine's launch grid, record settings, takes (the Clips tab until Jay renamed it, 12:55Z); its Edit row opens the note editor on the Sequencer tab | Transport / time |
| Pads | The pad bank beside the picked pad's settings and the kit's sounds, with Browse for the pad (section 4e; Jay, 12 Nov 15:46Z and 18 Nov 00:30Z) | App (the sampler's pad screens inside it) |
| Mix | The mixer (its strips as Faders or as Effects, the I/O view) and every strip's effect chain (section 3a; Jay's card, 14 Nov 07:15Z) | App (the mixer panel is the FX thread's crate) |
| Dev | Only when Prefs > "Show developer tools" is On | App |

- **Dev's pages,** on a strip under the top bar: MIDI log | Performance | CPU by engine | Processes | Map controls | Touch | Status | Console. CPU by engine and Processes were asked for by Jay at 08:19Z (the profiling thread builds them). Touch is the graphical touch calibration and testing tool with its tuning (Jay, 22:23Z; the touchscreen thread builds it, with a "Touch targets" page): the wheel and + / - step the tuning, SHIFT + wheel still changes Dev page, and MENU there offers Calibrate touch and Touch lab. Status is the System status board raw, for developers (Jay approved the System status plan, 22 Nov 08:35Z; [status.md](status.md)); the musician's status chip is in section 2 and the System status page in section 2a.
- **Not tabs:**
  - **Prefs.** SHIFT + MENU opens it.
  - **Each page's options.** MENU opens them. **Approved by Jay, 22 Nov 2026 22:40Z: "OK all four as written" on step 11's layout card (the instrument redesign's step 11d):** on the Synth tab's options, with the Instrument row picked, the help card starts with the track's line in the normal text colour, then a gap, then the row's help: "Track 3 plays Track 2's Poly", "In the mixer: Poly · Track 2".
    - **The tuning screens. Approved by Jay, 23 Nov 2026 16:14Z: "Approve as drawn" on the tunings plan's T4 card** (canvas https://claude.ai/artifact/E6YDpu1bomkRJ917xUJimi; boards in mpfree-claude `tunings/mockups/`; design: [tunings.md](tunings.md#t6-as-built-the-screens)). The Synth tab's options' **Tuning** row is the tab's instrument's: "Song's: Just (5-limit) on D" first, then the built-in tunings and tuning files as its own. With its own, an **OWN** tag (the second accent, outlined) follows the row's name and that instrument's **Tuning root** and **A4** rows come under it; on Song's there are none. A plugin that can't follow shows **12-TET (can't follow)** in amber with no arrows, and can't be stepped. With a Tuning row picked, the help card starts with what the instrument and the song play in (amber for a plugin that can't follow), then a full-width **Tunings** button ("The song's tuning and every instrument's", a chevron at the right), then the help.
    - **The Tunings page** (the same approval, board 3): an options page, opened from the Tuning row's Tunings button; Back or MENU goes back to the Tuning row. Under a **THE SONG'S TUNING** heading, the song's Tuning, Tuning root and A4; under **INSTRUMENTS · WHAT EACH PLAYS IN**, a row for each instrument ("Classic House · kit", "Poly · Track 2", "FM · Tracks 3 and 4"), with a line under its name ("its own", "Track 1 · keygroups, Notes and 16 Levels", "plugin, takes MIDI only") and its value: Song's, its own tuning, or 12-TET (can't follow) as above. A kit's own Tuning root and A4 come under its row; a synth's help has an **Open its options** button to its Synth options. A line under the help says gear on MIDI out plays 12-TET for now. The wheel passes over the headings.
  - **The Arp panel.** NOTE REPEAT + MENU opens it; it belongs to Play.
  - **The browser.** BROWSE, the folder icon (section 2) or the Pads tab's Browse for (section 4e) opens it over the current page, under the top bar, as the mixer does; BROWSE again, the folder icon or a tab closes it (controls decision 13; Jay after live test 3, 13 Nov 11:46Z; built by the project thread). **Load on and Next pad hit** (the Play, Pads and Sequencer redesign, Jay 18 Nov 00:30Z): for a sound, Load names the pad it goes on, **Load on A5** (the pad picked: a pad hit picks another, and the button follows), and Load to pad reads **Next pad hit** (the sound goes on the next pad hit). A kit's Load names the track it goes on, **Load on Drums** (the armed track's name, whose kit it replaces; board 8), and reads Load with no track armed or while the pads play an instrument. Beside it, **New track with this kit** (board 8) adds a track as + Track does, armed, and loads the kit into an empty kit for that track alone, so the kit the pads played stays as it was; with no room for another track or no empty kit left it says so and adds nothing. One UNDO on Arrange takes back the track and its kit, and REDO brings both back; while UNDO has them, the kit is out of the list of kits, and once REDO can't bring them back it is listed again, played by no track, so nothing loaded is lost (the redesign's step-1 deferral, built after step 6; the same for + Kit track and Chop to new kit). **The pad map** (the redesign's board 8, a step-1 deferral): under a sound, with a kit on the pads and only at the full width, the Selected column shows a bank of that kit's pads between the waveform and the buttons: a heading (the kit's name · bank B · tap a pad) with ‹ and › (56 px, round from H to A), then 16 pads four by four, pad 13 top left as on the MPC, each 54 px high with its label (B13), its sound's name (or empty) and its sound's colour as a stripe, the pad picked outlined in the accent. A finger on a pad picks it as a hit on the MPC's pad does (Load on follows) and plays it until it lifts. To make room, the sound's facts go on one line under its name (WAV audio · 0:00.40 · 44.1 kHz · 16-bit · Stereo), the waveform takes what is left, and the buttons go three across. The map shows the picked pad's bank until its arrows turn it. Its Projects place has New project, Save as, Save and Export. **Export** (Jay's pick on the missing-features thread's card, 14 Nov 01:39Z: "Yes, in BROWSE") opens the Export panel over the browser: Mixdown, Stems and MIDI file to tick, Stems by (Track, the default, or Channel, with how many times the range plays; Jay's pick "Yes, a Stems by row" on the missing-features thread's card, 14 Nov 15:20Z), the range (Arrangement, or a number of bars with - / +), the File row (24-bit, 16-bit or 32-bit float, and 44.1 or 48 kHz; it starts as Prefs > Export file and Export rate have it and changes the format for that export only, Jay's pick "Both" on the missing-features thread's card, 14 Nov 07:51Z), how it will play and the folder, then Start; while it runs, the time done and Cancel; when it is over, what it made and Show in BROWSE. While the panel shows, the wheel sets the bars, its push is Start (Close once it is over) and - closes it. BROWSE still closes the browser, and an export keeps running behind it. **Rows and kits** (Jay, 16 Nov 05:36Z, PR #410): each row has a star at its right end that toggles Favourites, and there are no Open or Favourite buttons; one tap opens a folder, and on anything else the first tap picks it and the second opens or loads it, as the wheel's push does. A kit is a folder holding `kit.txt` and its sounds; it loads whole, + goes inside it, and Kits has Save kit and New folder at the top. **Plugins** (Jay, 15 Nov 23:36Z, card on the Open MPC plugins thread: "Yes, build it as drawn"; mockups in /mnt/project-files/mpc-plugins/browser/) is a place in the Library column after Recordings. Its list has Catalog | Installed | On the stick at the top: Catalog is the Open MPC catalog's plugins that run on the MPC One, fetched over Ethernet; On the stick is the release zips copied to the stick's `files/plugins/`; each row is the plugin's name, its maker and its state (Installed, Update, Zip on the stick) or its kind. The chips under it are All, Instruments and Effects, with Search and a sort. The Selected column shows the plugin's own screen (Pictures steps through its tabs and the catalog's picture), its maker, kind, version and size, licence, the catalog's CPU figure and summary, and Install (or Install from zip, or Update), Pictures, Favourite and Remove (or Delete zip). **Sets** (live shows B1) is a place after Projects: a set is a list of songs to play in order, and the place and the Set editor are drawn in section 4p. The places column's rows close up to fit it (44 px apart on the MPC's screen, from 48). **SD card** (Jay, 23 Nov 2026 19:43Z, card on the disk streaming thread: "Yes, build it as drawn"; mockups: the browser preview's screens 24 to 28 and the manual's `browser-sd-card`) is a place in the Library column after Factory and before the Trash, with an SD card icon: the card in the MPC's slot, read only. A tap opens the card, and the list says "Opening the SD card, read only…" meanwhile. Open, the path reads "SD card · exFAT, 63.9 GB · read only" and the card lists as a place; the Selected column keeps Load on A5, Next pad hit, Audition, New track with this kit and Copy, with a "Read only: Copy, then Paste in a place" line, and the rows have no stars. Rename, Cut, Delete, New folder and Paste are not offered there. A card not safely removed last time says so in red under the list; an empty slot says so in the list, and a tap tries again. The sound picker offers it beside Samples and Recordings. With it the Library column holds 13 places above New project, so its rows close up to 36 px (from 40 px with the 12 places before).
  - **The pattern workspace.**
  - **The button guide.**
  - **System info, Touch lab and Calibrate touch.** These open from Prefs.
- **Adding, removing, renaming or reordering a tab** is a layout change, so it needs Jay's approval. The "Steps, Clips, Looper, Arrange, Synth, Mix" bar in the synth previews is a design mock, not this list.
- **The order is Play, Sequencer, Looper, Sample, Synth, Arrange, Pads, Mix, then Dev** (Jay, 18 Nov 2026 07:28Z: "I want the order to be: Play, Sequencer, Looper, Sample, Synth, Arrange, Pads, Mix"). Before that he chose an order at 08:15Z, renamed Clips to Arrange at 12:55Z, put Pads after Play (12 Nov 15:46Z), Mix last (14 Nov 07:15Z) and the Sequencer after Pads (the Play, Pads and Sequencer redesign, 18 Nov 00:30Z, design doc https://claude.ai/artifact/KHBG7mNbDFU4uZCFqNp9dW).

## 3a. The Mix tab

Approved by Jay on 14 Nov at 07:15Z ("Approve as drawn" on the Mix tab mockups: https://claude.ai/artifact/9w1vtAhfGcKmWj81JZdCUG). It answers his ask 7 after live test 3: a place for the mixer and effects, and a clear way to add an effect to a pad, a track or an audio channel.

- **The tab** holds the mixer and the effect chains. **TRACK MIX** opens it on Track Mix and **SHIFT + TRACK MIX** on Pad Mix, both on Faders; pressed on the tab (the mixer or a chain), either goes back to the page you came from. A tap on the tab opens the mixer on the view and the look it showed last.
- **Three looks of the same strips**, switched by a **Faders | Effects | EQ** toggle (246 px) left of the page arrows, where the mixer's CPU chip was (the cost, while profiling, moves to the master's name row, and on Effects and EQ to the title line). The strip views (Tracks, Pads, Inputs, Submixes) and the arrows work in all three; the I/O view is its own. When the view chips don't fit beside the toggle at their 124 px least (the tab beside the Q-Link column), they drop their counts and take 110, 90, 96 and 120 px (EQ look approved by Jay, 15 Nov 21:56Z, "Approve as drawn" on https://claude.ai/artifact/TXEcz7FkcTHqMdDNTFPHVp).
- **Faders** is the mixer as section 4b's strips have it.
- **Effects** puts one panel over the strips and the master: a title line ("TRACKS 1-8 · EACH CHAIN IN ORDER, LEFT TO RIGHT"), the slot numbers, then a 52 px row a strip (a page is the rows that fit, 8 on the MPC), and the master's row pinned at the bottom under a rule. A row is the strip's name over its colour stripe, with which strip it is and how many of its 8 slots are used, then its 8 slots: each effect (its name, a dot: green on, blue listening to another track's sound with "SC from ..." under it, red "Nothing picked" when it is set to listen and no track is picked, grey bypassed with "Off"), **+ FX** in the first free slot, the rest outlined. A strip has 8 insert slots.
- **On Effects:** a tap on + FX opens the effect picker for that strip; the effect lands in that slot and opens in the chain editor. A tap on an effect picks it (outlined; the title line names it) and the Q-Link column turns its first four knobs, as on the chain editor; a tap on the picked effect opens it in the chain editor. A drag along the row moves the effect (the slot it would land in is outlined). A tap on a strip's name selects the strip and gives the Q-Links back to it, as does Faders. A tap on an effect's "SC from ..." or "Nothing picked" line opens it in the chain editor with its sidechain list up (section 4b).
- **EQ** puts the same panel over the strips: a title line ("TRACKS 1-8 · EACH STRIP'S EQ · TAP A CURVE TO SHAPE IT", and on the right what is picked, "BASS · EQ · Band 2 picked"), the frequencies over the curves (20 Hz, 100, 1k, 10k, 20 kHz) and "Where it sits", then a 52 px row a strip and the master's row pinned under a rule. A row is the strip's name cell as on Effects, a box with the strip's first parametric EQ drawn as its response curve (±18 dB, the strip's colour, a dot a band in the band's colour, grey while bypassed; the Channel strip's EQ stage doesn't count), and two lines: its slot and how many bands ("Slot 2 · 3 bands"), and its cuts ("Low cut 35 Hz, 12 dB", "Cuts off", or "Bypassed"). A strip without one shows **+ EQ** in a dashed box with where it goes ("it goes in slot 3, the first free slot"; "the chain is full" and no button when it is).
- **On EQ:** a tap on + EQ adds a parametric EQ at the end of the strip's chain, in place. A tap on a curve picks that EQ (its box outlined) and the band under the finger (its dot ringed); the Q-Link column turns the band's Freq, Gain and Q and the EQ's Output, as on the EQ's page; a tap on what is picked opens the EQ in the chain editor. A dot dragged past 12 px moves its band, sideways its frequency and up and down its gain (a cut: only its frequency); a drag is one UNDO step. A tap on a strip's name selects the strip and gives the Q-Links back to it.
- **The EQ's page in the chain editor:** in place of sections, a graph over 63 % of the page (20 Hz to 20 kHz, ±18 dB, the curve in white over each band's shape in its colour, a dot a band: 1 to 8, LC and HC for the cuts) and the band panel under it: a chip a band in use and + Band, the picked band's type (Low cut, Low shelf, Bell, Notch, High shelf, High cut), its Freq, Gain (a cut: Slope) and Q, ON, Solo band and Remove. A dot dragged past 12 px moves its band; the wheel or a second finger sets its Q; two taps on the graph add a bell there; while a dot is held a bubble reads its values. Solo band plays the picked band alone and stops when the page closes (named Listen until the owner's rename, 16 Nov). The Q-Links turn the picked band's Freq, Gain and Q and the Output.
- **The chain editor's head:** a 44 px **‹** at the top left goes back to where the editor opened (the Mix tab as it was, or the Pads tab), then the **strip button**: the strip's colour, its name and which strip it is ("Pad 3"). A tap on it opens the **strip list** in the editor's place: group buttons (Tracks, Pads, Inputs, Returns, Submixes, Master), < > to page a long group, and the group's strips; a tap on one opens its chain (on its first effect, or with the picker up when it has none), and Back keeps the strip.
- **The effect picker's head** says where the effect goes: "ADD TO KEYS · IT GOES IN SLOT 4 OF 8" (the strip and the slot at the end of its chain), "PICK A PRESET OR DEFAULTS · IT GOES IN SLOT 4 OF KEYS" on the presets, and "KEYS IS FULL" when there is no room; holding a tile for presets is on the hint line.
- **Audio tracks** (the audio tracks rethink's phase 1, approved 22 Nov 07:28Z, section 4c): **Tracks** shows a strip per audio track, in track order with the other tracks, named after the track and in its colour, with a wave mark and its input under the name. Its M and S are the track's mute and solo. The Tracks count includes audio tracks. The one shared Audio strip goes.
- **No Loop strip** (the audio tracks rethink's phase 4, board 6; the phase 4 screens approved as drawn by Jay, 23 Nov 2026 19:24Z, https://claude.ai/artifact/KJDBaGjVb6orw27Zsvb1ff; from 4b behind Dev's "Loops on audio tracks", for everyone at 4e when Loop tracks go): each loop plays through its own track's channel. An audio strip's head shows what its track plays now under its input ("∿ Line L"), in the track's colour: "▶ loop · row 2" and the loop's name, or "▶ take · row 3" and the clip's. Its M and S are the only mute and solo a loop has; a muted track's loop runs on in time, silently.
- **The Pads tab's Effects row:** the pad's settings end with **Effects**, a button (no arrows) reading "+ FX" or the effects on the pad's mixer channel ("Compressor, EQ", or "Compressor +2" when they don't fit). A tap, or the wheel's push on the row, opens the pad's chain in the Mix tab (the picker up on an empty chain), and ‹ comes back to the Pads tab. Pads past the 16 with a channel show "—".

## 4. The Play page

Built in round 8 (PR #106) to Jay's approved mockups F to I (10:05Z), with real data and working controls (13:56Z on; side-by-side sheets in /mnt/project-files/hello-mpc/round8-play/compare/). The old big pads are gone, and since 12 Nov the mini pads too: Jay saw no benefit in them on the home screen (08:11Z) and picked "the pattern fills the room" (15:45Z, mockups in /mnt/project-files/hello-mpc/pads-mockups/). The pads live on the Pads tab (section 4e).

- **The frame, in every view:**
  - the transport bar: the state, the position, the tempo, the REC chip, the bar and step dots, and **what the pads play** at its right end (Play has one view since the looper redesign's step 5, so no view switch); on the Sequencer tab, whose Steps share this frame, the dots stand where what the pads play is, and the switch Steps | Notes is at the right end (section 4m). **What the pads play** (the Play, Pads and Sequencer redesign, step 4; board 1's "PADS PLAY"): "PADS PLAY" over a dot in the track's colour and "Drums · Boom Bap · A", the track Play's rows show, its kit as the list of kits names it and the bank on the pads (the PAD BANK lights can't show E to H), or the synth's line ("Keys · Poly · C3 to D#4"); 132 to 224 px, as wide as its text. Too long, the track goes first, then the kit's name is cut, but the bank stays. A tap opens the list of kits (section 2). While controller mode is on (section 4d) the pads play outside gear, so it says what the Controller chip says ("Controller → Juno-X · ch 3 · A", or why nothing goes, "Controller: pick its port on I/O"), its dot in warn, and a tap says on the hint line that the Controller chip gives the pads back to the kit (step 3b's follow-up, agreed with the MIDI thread 18 Nov 07:13Z). It took the kit chip's place; the dots sit beside it (board 1; the looper redesign's step 5 took the view switch away). **The REC chip** (sequencer decision 1, mockups F and G) names what REC records: "REC → Drums · Clip 1" in grey with a red dot while armed, "READY · PLAY starts → Loop 1" outlined in red while ready, solid red "● REC" or "● OVERDUB" while recording; "REC · PINNED" while pinned, "Track 1 + Loop 1" with both armed (SHIFT + Arm), and with several tracks armed (below) "REC → Drums + Bass + Keys", or "Drums + 2 tracks" past 24 characters ("+ Loop 1" after them with the loop too); **Approved by Jay, 22 Nov 2026 22:40Z: "OK all four as written" on step 11's layout card (the instrument redesign's step 11c):** while the pads play layers, before you press, the chip names where REC will record the first synth and counts the layers that record on tracks of their own, "REC → Track 2 · FM + 1 more"; with a synth a MIDI route names (step 11e) it counts that too, and over a kit names the armed track alone, "REC → Track 1 + 1 more"; Arrange's transport bar shows it as a chip at its right end. A tap on it opens **the target list** (mockup H) in place of the view: a row for each track's notes in track order (not the Loop tracks; "Drums · Clip 1", under it "plays the pads" for the pads' track, "records its MIDI input too" for another armed one, "SHIFT + tap: record it too" for the rest) and the loop's row last, the armed ones red with their pills (a tap arms it, pinned or not; on a track's row that track alone, as picking it does). **SHIFT + a tap on a track's row** records that track too, and again lets it go: several tracks in one take, each from its keyboard's route (the MPC 3.10.1 guide's Rec Arm Behavior, p. 62; [rec-arm.md](rec-arm.md#several-tracks)), up to 16, refused while a take records. More rows than fit in one column go in two, shorter (down to 40 px, a row's settings at its right left out), and past what two hold the first tracks and the loop show. Then the **Rec song** and **Pin the arm** switches, Close, and on the right what moves the arm (with "SHIFT + a track here: record it too") and the chip's three looks; the chip again, Close or another tab picked closes it. While REC is ready or recording, the pages without a transport bar show a short chip in the header ("READY → Track 1", "● REC → Loop 1"). The step dots are 8 px a 16th to make room for it;
  - **what the keys play** (live shows C1, section 4q): while the song has a patch, KEYS PLAY sits between the REC chip and what the pads play, in the place of the bar and step dots, which stay on the Sequencer tab and Arrange. A song with no patches keeps the dots;
  - the view under it;
  - at the right edge, from under the top bar to the hint line: the master bar (66 px: meter with peak hold, level handle, level, LIM when the limiter works) and the Q-Link column (128 px: four cards, knob 1 at the top, each with the knob's number and its target's name centred over the ring with the value in it). Where the target lives (the page, the track, the pad) is not drawn, so the column is narrow and every page beside it wider (Jay, 17 Nov 15:20Z, on the Sample tab: "you could save some horizontal space (and thus make the actual sample waveform display wider) by editing the Q-link display so that it doesn't say "wave" in the top right and then centering "Q1/Start", "Q2/End", etc over the top of the knob's value display"; on every page, by his pick at 15:22Z; it was 168 px). A drag on a card turns that knob, and a finger on a knob lights its card.
- **The views:**
  - All (home): **the loop row** (150 px), **Play's rows** across the whole width, then the throws beside the XY pad (280 px). **Play's rows** (the Play, Pads and Sequencer redesign's board 1; Jay 18 Nov 00:21Z: Play is a simple step sequencer of the armed track's pads, as on a Digitakt): the pads of the bank on the pads, one row each in pad order (A1 to A16), empty pads keeping a dashed "empty" row with no speaker, as many rows as fit (six with the loop row) and a rail at the right; a swipe up or down moves them through the 16 (a finger that moves 12 px swipes, so a tap on a step or a name acts when the finger lifts). A row: the sound's colour and name, the pad's name ("A1") and the speaker (the whole name is the mute, as before), then the 16 steps of the page. A pad of another type than a sample (section 4e) has its type under its name in small capitals, as STEP SEQ's lane has ON THE PADS: **LOOP**, **SYNTH** or **MIDI** (the redesign's step 6b, board 2; the Sequencer's lanes show it too). A tap on an empty step puts a note there; a tap on a lit step **takes it out**, with its locks, as a hit on a lit step pad does (Jay's device run, 20 Nov 2026: a tap that showed "off" instead of taking the step out was unclear; this replaces the redesign's step 3b, his default of 18 Nov 00:30Z); UNDO brings it back as it was. A step switched off by the Sequencer's step inspector (the sequencer's `Note::on`) keeps its note, velocity, length, settings and locks, the player leaves it out, and the cell is the empty step's fill with an outline and "off" in the lane's colour (its lock dots stay; board 1); a tap brings it back on as it was. ERASE + a tap also takes a step out (section 6). **The head**, after the Arm pill: the track (a dot in its colour, its name over "Track 1 · armed") with ‹ and › at its ends, each half arming the track before or after round the ends (Loop tracks have no pads, so it skips them; with no other it says so); the banks **A** to **H**, the one on the pads lit (a tap is PAD BANK's, and PAD BANK moves the rows; a new bank or track starts at its first row); the steps shown, "1–16 of 32", with ‹ and ›; and **Follow** (lit while on: the page turns with the playhead; a page picked by hand turns it off). Its **Sequencer tab ›** button went on 18 Nov (Jay, 07:28Z: the Sequencer tab is in the top bar); the room it had goes to the page's ‹ and › (60 px, a fingertip), Follow (96 px) and the banks (48 px), as far as it reaches. There is no length's – and + here: the Sequencer's Steps have them. A synth track shows its scale rows under the same head, its banks faint, and a swipe moves the rows' window. The hint line: "Tap a step: in or out · a name: mute · A–H: the bank · swipe: the 16 pads · ‹ Drums ›: next track · a loop's ring: its next step".
  - Beats, now the Sequencer tab's **Steps** (section 4m): the pattern (six lanes; up to eight with the strip off), then the Your timing strip (72 px: the average offset, the spread, the velocity and the spread of offsets over the last two bars against the 1/16 grid), then all eight throws across the width. A tap puts a step in or takes it out, as on Play's rows (a step's tap acts when the finger lifts); **a finger held on a lit step for 0.4 s picks it** for the inspector and the Q-Links (velocity, length, nudge, chance; Q-LINK steps to its condition, ratchet, nudge in ticks and the ratchet's ramp), as holding a step pad does, and its lift then taps nothing; held on an empty step, nothing is picked and the lift puts a note there. A tap on the step picked takes it out and closes the inspector; a tap on a step that is off (the inspector's Off) switches it back on (Jay's device run, 20 Nov 2026, replacing the redesign's step 3b's tap that picked first and then switched the step off). The tab's hint line: "Tap a step: in or out · hold a step: edit it · Notes: the clip's notes · STEP SEQ: steps on the pads · PAD BANK: bank · MENU: options · STOP …". Play's options have **Timing strip** (On by default, saved); Off gives the pattern the strip's room.
  - **The step inspector** (sequencer decision 2, Jay 16 Nov 2026 20:00Z, "as designed": the Beats mockup A): while a step is picked on Beats, or held on the pads with STEP SEQ, the inspector takes the throws' place (the same 196 px, so nothing else moves), and the throws come back when the step is picked off, cleared or let go. Its head names the step ("Step 7 · Kick", "Step 5 · Kick · held"); at its right, for a step picked, × picks it off and keeps it; before that, Q-LINK's banks: **Step**, **Trig** and **Sound**, the one the Q-Links show filled (Sound in the lock purple). A tap picks one, as Q-LINK does; Sound only while the step is held, since a turn there locks it. Eight chips give the step's velocity, length, nudge, chance, condition, ratchet, ramp (S6: the ratchet's velocity ramp, "flat", "+24", "−40") and **Tie / Slide** (S6's `Note::join`: off, tie or slide; a tap steps it, with a small › at its top right), the ones the bank's Q-Links turn outlined; a step held with no note says its locks play with no note. **LOCKS ON THIS STEP** lists its locks in the lock purple, each with its value and a × that takes it off (one take, so UNDO brings it back), then a dashed note on how to add one, then **On | Off** (two 76 px halves, the one the step is lit: On in the accent, Off in warn; Off switches the step off and keeps it, as a tap on Play does, every step held at once, and the head adds "· off"). **Copy**: the next step tapped on the same lane takes a copy, locks included (any other tap lets it go). **Clear**: the step comes out, locks too, as one take. Copy and Clear act on one step, so they are dimmed while several are held.
  - The Loop view is gone (the looper redesign's step 5; Jay 17 Nov 22:51Z): the loop row on All shows all eight loops, and the Looper tab (section 4a) is where a loop is shaped. Its Q-Links went with it: the Looper tab's turn the loops' levels, and the loop's delay send and filter and the line's level are the mixer's Loop and Line strips'.
  - The Drum view is gone (Jay, 13 Nov 13:09Z, "Fold timing into Beats, drop Drum"): its timing is Beats' strip. Its Decay and FX mix Q-Links went with it (Decay is on the Pads tab; the throws' mix stays at full).
- **The lane on the pads** (sequencer decision 2, Jay 16 Nov 20:00Z): while STEP SEQ has the pads, the pattern's first lane is the sound on the pads (on the synth, "Line": every note of each step, in the pen's colour; a tap on an empty step puts the pen there), outlined in the accent colour with "ON THE PADS" under its name; the pattern shows the pads' page, and its head reads "pads: page 2 · BANK B". ‹ and › on the head turn the pads' page too. The step pads held are outlined as a step picked is, and the Q-Link column's title names them and their bank ("Q-Links · Step 5 · Trig"). On the Sound bank a card whose held step has a lock shows it in the step pads' lock purple, its scope reading "STEP 5 · LOCK"; otherwise it shows the sound's own value.
- **The pattern** is the armed track's clip from the sequencer. Each lane's name is its mute button: a speaker in a box (red with a × while muted) at the name's right, and a tap anywhere on the name, over the lane's full height and half the gaps, mutes that pad. A tap on a step toggles it (or starts a pattern when there is none). **Its head** (Jay after live test 3: 32, 64 and 128 steps) has ‹ and › round the page number to turn its pages of 16, and – and + round its length: + doubles the steps, up to 128, the new ones repeating the old; – halves them, down to 16, and UNDO brings back what it took off. Each length change is one take. A button that can't act is dimmed, and a tap on it says why on the hint line. Each answers over the head's full height.
- **A step's cell** shows the step whole (mockup A): the cell dim in the lane's colour with its velocity as the height of the bright bar over its foot; a condition as a small label at its top left ("1:2", "FILL", "1ST"); a chance under 100 % as a dashed outline, with "70%" at the top left when there is no condition; a ratchet as a tick per repeat (up to four) beside that label; a nudge as a small triangle at its foot (at the left pointing left when early, at the right pointing right when late); a note lasting past its step as a bar into the next cell, and across the empty cells it lasts over; and a purple dot per lock at its top right, up to three, on an empty step too (a lock with no note). A step switched off but kept (step 3b) is the empty step's fill with a 2 px outline and "off" in the lane's colour, its lock dots kept, and no tie bar: its note doesn't play, so it lasts over nothing.
- **Scale rows** (S3, the sequencer design's mockup B, for synth tracks under decision 2): when the synth has the pads, the pattern's lanes are the pads' notes in pitch order, the highest on top, as many as fit. Each row's name is a key across the names column, its note (or a chord pad's chord) on the left, the key's root shaded in the pads' colour and tagged "root" on the right; a finger on it plays the note until it lifts, and there is no speaker (the kit's pad mutes are the drums'). ERASE held + a name clears that note's steps. The head adds **Fold** (lit while on: only the notes the clip uses) and **‹ Oct 3 ›**, which moves the rows an octave down or up, before the page's ‹ and ›; the head's details give way when they would crowd the title. With STEP SEQ the Line lane stays on top. The window starts from the root under the lowest note used.
- **The Arm pills** (sequencer decision 1, mockups F and G): the loop row's and the pattern's heading start with a pill, grey "Arm" on what REC doesn't record and red on what it does ("Armed", "Ready", "Rec"); the armed panel has a red edge, a dashed red outline while ready and a solid one while recording. A tap on a grey pill arms it, even when pinned; on the red one it says what REC records. **SHIFT + a tap** on a pill or a list row adds it, so both are red and REC records the notes and the loop in one pass; SHIFT + a tap on one of the two lets go of it, and a plain tap keeps only the one tapped.
- **The loop row** (the looper redesign's step 5; Jay 17 Nov 22:51Z, board 1, in the 150 px the loop strip had): every loop, since Play is where everything is played together. **Its heading:** the Arm pill, "LOOPS", then "Loop 1:" and the picked loop's layers as chips (a dot in the layer's colour, its name by what was played into it, and its speaker; a tap anywhere on the chip, from the row's top edge, mutes that layer, and a muted one is grey; "+2" when more don't fit), and at the right **Capture** (176 px) and **Pads: loop keys** (164 px, lit while the pads are the loop keys), as the Looper tab's deck has them. **Under it, a card a loop,** eight side by side (about 120 px each, 8 px apart): its name (a tap picks the loop, without starting anything; the picked card has an accent outline), its ring (the deck's: state colour, the bars and where it is; under 80 px across, only the middle's big line), and beside the ring its speaker and S, stacked, once it has sound; an empty loop says what it records from in their place ("Pads · free" when Sync is Free). A free loop has "free" after its name, and its ring rests as on the deck while the free loops are stopped (free loops, F2; Jay's OK of 23 Nov). A ring acts on the press as the deck's (record, cancel, end the first pass, overdub, end the overdub, unmute, play the stopped free loops), and held 1 s it clears the loop, the arc draining red. Only the cards that change are drawn again.
- **Throws and the XY pad** play Perform FX (`mpfree_fx::Perform`) on the master while held: each throw holds a pad of its default bank (Delay throw the 1/8 dotted echo throw, Reverb wash the wash throw, Filter dip the 1-bar low-pass sweep), entering on the next 1/16 while the transport plays; the XY pad plays its DJ filter across (low-pass left, off in the middle, high-pass right) and its echo up, and glides home when the finger lifts; Drop drums mutes the kick, snare and hat pads while held. **What the XY pad plays** (Jay, 18 Nov 2026 07:34Z: "I think we should just pick from a list using the chip"): its head is a chip, "XY · Filter + echo ▾", and a tap anywhere on the head opens **the XY pad's list** in place of the view, as the REC chip's target list does: "The XY pad plays", Close (112 × 44), then two groups (board 1 of the XY mockups, Jay's approval of the XY mockups, 23 Nov 2026 10:34Z ("Approve, start phase 2"; canvas https://claude.ai/artifact/X2A8nsHiwCf6S6PuZQDq4n, pictures in mpfree-claude `xy-controller/mockups/`); until then ten tiles two across, about 520 × 90). **READY-MADE · 10**, "on the master": ten tiles three across (each about 330 × 52, a fingertip and more), Filter + echo first, then the XY FX's nine effects (Filter, Echo, Dub echo, Wash, Crush, Flanger, Phaser, Ring mod, Gate), each with what across and up do ("across: Decay · up: Amount"). **YOURS · IN THIS SONG · N**: your layouts (section 4o), the same tiles, each saying what its directions are called, how many settings each moves and where they play ("across: Bite, 2 settings · up: Wet, 2 · Bass + Master", "follows the selected track", "Hold" when it starts with Hold on), then **+ New layout** (dashed: an empty layout of yours with Go back, opened in Setup) and **From the browser** (a layout saved in another song, through the browser). The one playing is lit in the accent. Under them two faint lines: a ready-made one changed in Setup becomes one of yours and the ready-made one stays as it was; a second finger on the pad does nothing until the first lifts. More than fit scroll with a drag or the wheel. The head's line reads "Tap one: the pad plays it. Yours change in Setup on the XY page." A tap picks one and closes the list; Close, the chip or a view closes it with nothing changed. With an XY FX effect picked the pad plays the master's XY FX (put after the Perform FX on the first pick): it opens while the finger is down and lets go when it lifts (an echo rings out), the dot stays where it lifted, the axes' words name its two settings and the label reads "Decay 2.4 s · Amount 40 %". Prefs keeps the pick of a ready-made one; your layouts, and which of them plays, are kept with the song. Sample screen `play-xy-list`.
- **No pads panel** (Jay, 12 Nov 15:45Z). The Chromatic | In key switch for the synth pads (Jay, 12:22Z) is in the heading over the Synth tab's presets, and SHIFT + 16 LEVEL works everywhere.
- **Q-Links on Play,** while controller mode is on (section 4d): the controller's four CCs, as on every page. Otherwise, on Play and the Sequencer's Steps: swing, tune (while 16 Levels shows Transpose: the armed track's transpose, two octaves either way; sequencer S4) and the drums' filter and delay send (a picked step on the Steps: its velocity, length, nudge and chance, or with Q-LINK or the inspector's Trig its condition, ratchet and nudge in ticks). The master bar sets the level. While NOTE REPEAT is held or the Arp panel is open, the cards show the arp's four (rate, gate, swing, octaves; on Phrase the first is the phrase's speed, as the Phrase tab's boards have it, below).
- **The header** keeps to the CPU and RAM chips (what the pads play is on the transport bar); the session chip shows only when something is wrong, the arp chip only while the arp, a repeat or a latch is on.
- **The hint line** starts where the panels do (left-aligned, as the mockups).
- **NOTE REPEAT's strip** takes the top of the view's first panel, so nothing else moves.
- **The Arp panel** takes the view under the transport bar.
  - **Its Modifier box** (Jay, 23 Nov 2026 16:08Z, "Approve and merge" on the arp controller tricks card; canvas https://claude.ai/artifact/THaamfTJUJ1T1xWKE8Mppi): its mode tabs share their row with the Modifier box (340 px, at the right): MODIFIER over the controller's name, and how far it is now as a percent over a meter in the second accent; with no controller, "Off · tap to pick a controller". A tap opens the arp options' Modifier section (Back, Controller, Pushes rate, Pushes octaves, Pushes gate, Pushes swing, Adds rolls, Adds accents); SHIFT + a touch on it arms MIDI Learn. While the Modifier pushes, each Q-Link slider shows a ring where it plays now, joined in the second accent, and its value reads "1/16 → 1/32"; the quick band's rate is the one playing, in the second accent; the step strip shows the octaves played. The sliders' knobs, the Q-Link cards and the arp chip stay as set.
  - **Its Pattern list** (Jay, 23 Nov 2026 16:08Z, "Approve, PR after #883" on the arp pattern library's card; canvas https://claude.ai/artifact/DyixxwWNCJtqf9fdxjM4ay): in Pattern mode the pattern list has the groups as tabs in its title line (Starters, Chords, Gates, Rolls, Riffs, Lines, Bass, Polymeter, Chance, then My patterns when there are any), the count at their right ("28 / 74"), then three rows. Each row is the pattern's name, a picture of its first bar's rhythm (the pictures lined up in a column), and its text, dim, at the right. A tap on a tab loads its group's first pattern; a tap on a row loads it; the wheel steps through every pattern and SHIFT + wheel jumps a group. Where the tabs would be narrower than 84 px, one group shows with ‹ and › arrows.
  - **The Phrase tab** (Jay's card on the five boards, "Yes, as drawn", 23 Nov 2026 15:48Z; boards https://claude.ai/artifact/2YUSRkN2Zhkf82y46RMaJ3, pictures in mpfree-claude `composing-tools/project-files/phrase-boards/`): the panel's fifth tab, after Pattern. Under the tabs, three panels 12 px apart. **The kinds of phrase** (166 px): Bass, Riff, Chords, Arp lines, then Yours a little under them, each with its count, the one shown lit. **That kind's phrases:** a row each (50 px) with the name, its length and a small picture of its notes, the track's pick lit and the phrases that suit the key first. **Plays** (397 px): the phrase's name and what it is, its notes large with a cursor while it plays, **Follows** (In key, Exact) and four sliders, Speed · Q1, Gate · Q2, Swing · Q3 and Octaves · Q4. A tap on a kind shows it and picks the phrase last picked there; a tap on a phrase picks it. The wheel moves the pick along the kind shown, and − / + step the kinds (− past Bass: the Pattern tab). While the transport plays, a new pick waits for the bar line: its row is outlined in amber and says "next bar", and the phrase still playing is marked in green. On a kit track the kinds are faint and one panel says that phrases play on a synth. On NOTE REPEAT's strip a phrase's − and + step its speed (½×, 1×, 2×). Sample screens `arp-phrase`, `arp-phrase-next-bar`, `arp-phrase-kit` and `arp-quick-band-phrase`.
  - **Making and improvising on the Phrase tab** (composing tools P2 and S5; Jay's cards, "Yes, as drawn", 24 Nov 2026: P2's boards A to H at 14:50Z, pictures in mpfree-claude `composing-tools/project-files/p2-boards/`; S5's boards A and B at 14:48Z, `composing-tools/project-files/s5-improvise-boards/`): **New idea** at the right of the list's heading and **More like this** at the right of Plays' heading open one panel in the list's place, the kinds still showing: a title and ×, the rows as ‹ value › steppers with the wheel's row marked, the status, and Try again, ‹ Try N, Back and Keep. Plays gains the **Plays** row (While I hold | When I stop) under what the phrase is. While I hold adds a line saying what it plays over and when it evolves, and the moves **Hold**, **Evolve**, **Return** and **Capture**, with the four sliders two by two. When I stop puts **Call and answer** in the list's place (the call, then the answer, the lanes over it and the turn light under it) and the **When I stop panel** in the detail's (Plays, Mode, Answers for, Key and Starts, Answer now and Capture both, the sliders), the shelves faint. The Pattern tab's second line of buttons ends with **Keep as a phrase**. Arrange's clip card ends with **Phrase** ("Make ›") and BROWSE has **Make a phrase** after Load on a MIDI file; both open the **Make a phrase** sheet, in the clip card's and the takes' place or over the browser's Selected panel. The details are in [arpeggiator.md](arpeggiator.md). The boards' Records row (You, mpfree, Both) is not built yet. Sample screens `arp-phrase-new-idea`, `arp-phrase-more-like`, `arp-phrase-evolve`, `arp-phrase-answer`, `clips-card-phrase`, `clips-make-phrase`, `browser-make-phrase` and `clips-add-part-phrase` (Add a part's phrase, on its S2.6 panel).
- **Macro banks:** Q-LINK steps the Q-Link column to Macros 1-4, then Macros 5-8, then back to the view's own knobs (with a step picked on the Steps it steps the step's banks instead: the inspector's × picks the step off); Play keeps its bank while other pages show. A finger held on a macro card opens the macros panel (section 4b).
- **Half length and double** stay on the Looper tab, where a loop is shaped (the loop strip's "still to come" went with it in the looper redesign's step 5).

## 4a. The Looper tab

**The Looper tab as an instrument** (Jay, 17 Nov 2026 21:54Z: the Sample tab's principles, not a copy of its panel; design and mockups approved 22:55Z, https://claude.ai/artifact/GjymkKBEUE6mESjyi3oHwV and https://claude.ai/artifact/VHmK33LYRcxB1LKNazwdSd). It replaces round 9's mockup J layout (the loop list at the left, the tools row, the Recording and input and Use the loop panels). Every loop is in sight with one touch to record, overdub or mute it; the picked loop is laid out under them; rare settings live in MENU. Built in five steps, each its own pull request; step 1 (the deck, the picked loop and the Q-Link banks) is this layout. **The recording rethink** (Jay's device notes, 20 Nov 2026; layout approved 08:31Z, "I like it, as long as you incorporate the changes we discussed with capturing audio, moving/resizing capture window"): Records from moved over the deck with mixes added, a countdown before a loop starts, Listen, Length: Pattern, and Capture's movable window on What you played.

- **The frame:** the deck of loops (240 px) across the top; the picked loop under it (the rest of the height); the Q-Link column (128 px) at the right of both; the hint line under them. Over the deck, the song's clock (a dot a beat, the bar and beat, the tempo; "Song stopped" in place of "Stopped" while a free loop holds audio), then, while a free loop holds audio, the free loops as a group: "Free" in bold, ▶ while they play or ■ while they are stopped, and the free clock's tempo ("· Free ▶ 93.4 BPM", without "BPM" when the song's bar and beat leave no room before Pads as loop keys; free loops, F2, Jay's OK of 23 Nov and his looper review's 5.1), **Pads as loop keys** (160 px), **Records from** (220 px, left of the memory: what every loop records, with its meter; a tap opens its choices in the lanes' place: **Pads**, **Line in**, **Loops**, and under them a row of mixes, **Master** first, then the tracks and submixes as the Mix tab names them, never the Loop track; while a take keeps recording another source than the one just picked, a small accent line over the name says THIS TAKE · NEXT: and the pick, and the name is what the take records, until the take ends; OK 20 Nov 2026 12:17Z) and the loop memory ("1:42 of 5 min"; Prefs > Loop memory, 1 to 20 minutes from the next start). Capture is no longer over the deck: it is the button on What you played (below) and SHIFT + REC.
- **The deck:** 8 identical loop channels side by side, each its name and length (a free loop: its name and "free"), a ring, a level bar beside the ring, and under it a speaker in a box and an **S** (solo, at most 44 px wide; step 3a). The ring is the loop: its wave runs round it (72 columns), an arc fills as it plays with a tick a bar and a hand at the playhead, and the middle says the bar ("2 of 4"); its colour is its state (the loop's colour playing, red recording, amber overdubbing, grey muted). A free loop's ring, while the free loops are stopped, rests where it will start again, faint, with ▶ in the middle ("stopped" under it, or "on the bar" while a Play waits for the song's bar line), and a tap plays the free loops (again while that Play waits: calls it off). An empty loop is a dashed circle with + and its source under it (Pads, Line in, Loops, or the mix's name; "· free" after it when Sync is Free); armed, it turns red and says the bar it starts on, and in the last bar before it counts the beats left ("3 beats to REC"), or REC on PLAY while stopped; capturing (step 4a), the same, saying "Capture on bar" or "Capturing", and a tap calls it off. **A tap on a ring is the loop's next step, on the press** (empty: record from the next bar line at least a beat away, so a start never comes without warning; waiting: cancel; first pass: end on the next bar line; playing: overdub; overdubbing: end it; muted: play), and picks the loop. A tap on the name picks it without starting anything. **A press and hold of 1 s clears the loop** (the arc drains in red; letting go sooner leaves it), and **UNDO brings a cleared loop back** (step 3a; the empty ring's foot says "Cleared · UNDO"). The speaker mutes or unmutes without picking. The S solos the loop without picking it (lit in the accent): while any loop is soloed the others play silent in their places, their rings grey with "by solo" in the middle. The level bar (-24 to +6 dB, with the loop's meter) drags, or takes the wheel while a finger rests on it (section 6). One loop records at a time.
- **The picked loop:** its header (colour, name, "Loops A · Verse · 4 bars · 6 layers", a free loop's "free · 4 bars · 6 layers"; **▶ Listen**, left of Send: with the music stopped, the loop alone round and round, lit "Stop" while it plays, faint while the music plays; on a free loop, **■ Stop free** in Listen's place while the free loops play (it stops them all, at their tops) and **▶ Play free** while they are stopped (named for the group they stop and start, Jay's looper review's 5.3) (it starts them: at once with the song stopped, else on the song's next bar line, lit while it waits, when a tap calls it off), and left of it **Make song** (132 px: every free loop becomes a song loop and the song takes their tempo, on the free loops' next bar line while they play, at once while they are stopped; lit while it waits for that bar line, faint while the song plays, when a tap says why on the hint line; Jay's go of 24 Nov); **Send**; **Clear** outlined in red), a ruler of the bars, and **a lane per layer, 60 px, the newest on top**, its head 196 px: **▶** plays that layer alone while the music is stopped, a tap on the name mutes it, S solos it, × on the picked layer deletes it (on the only layer it clears the loop), a tap on the wave picks it, and a finger resting on a lane gives the wheel its level (SHIFT: pan). Four lanes show; with more, a map down the right marks each layer and outlines the four in view, a label says how many are below, a swipe scrolls them (a finger that moves 8 px before the hold below) and a tap on the map jumps there. They stay where they were left until another layer is picked.
- **The controls the moment needs,** under the lanes. A loop that plays: **Feedback** (Fade old, 50 to 100 % kept a pass; drag or wheel), **Overdubs** (Add, Replace or Once, three segments; steps 3a and 3b; a Replace layer's lane tag says "replace"), **Reverse**, **Speed** (½×, 1×, 2×, three segments, like tape; step 3c), **½**, the length, **× 2**, **Undo**, the history as a row of chips (an overdub in its layer's colour, an edit grey, undone steps hollow; a tap opens the undo history in the lanes' place), **Redo**, and **Merge** with its progress. Dragging the ruler sideways nudges a 1/16 note at a time; a grip at each end of the ruler trims by 1/16 notes on release, the part cut off dimmed. Tapped while the loop records, each says why. An empty loop: **Sync** (Song, the default, or Free: the loop keeps its own time on the looper's free clock, plays on when the song stops and records with the song stopped; the choice holds for every empty loop until changed, as Starts' does; it shows the picked loop's own clock, so once the loop waits to record or records it is faint and fixed, and a tap says why on the hint line; free loops, F2, Jay's OK of 23 Nov and his looper review's 5.2), **Monitor**, **Length** (**Pattern**, the default: the length of the pattern playing in whole bars, Until tap with nothing playing; Until tap, 1, 2, 4, 8 bars), **Starts** (On the bar, or When I play: the first note starts the loop, and the song if it is stopped; step 4b; faint for Loops; an armed loop listening says "Play to start" in its ring; the choice holds for every empty loop until changed), **Then** (Play or Overdub), and in the lanes' place **What you played** (step 4a): the input over at least 8 bars (four patterns' worth, or the window's bars and one, up to 32 bars within the 60 s kept), with the song's bar lines numbered, the bars Capture would take shaded as a window with a grip at each end, and a **Capture bars 10 and 11** button (250 x 44 px) centred over them when Capture goes into this loop. Dragging the window moves it a bar at a time to earlier bars (it stays on them as the song plays on; dragged back to the newest bar line it keeps up again); dragging a grip grows or shrinks it a whole pattern at a time (with Length: Pattern and a pattern playing, else by the Length's bars, or a bar), a dashed line where each repeat of the pattern starts, the button saying "Capture bars 5 to 16 · 3 × pattern". A Capture, or a new Length, puts it back to the Length's bars. Records from is over the deck, not here. The line input, the latency, Loop level, Tempo from the first loop (step 4c: with the song stopped, a first loop on Until tap records free, its ring saying "Free" and the seconds, and closing it sets the tempo and starts the song) and Loop memory are in MENU.
- **Smart loop end** (the smart loop end plan's step 2 screens; Jay's OK on the drawings, 22 Nov 2026 18:37Z, "Approve as drawn", canvas https://claude.ai/artifact/3RuQ7c9MuffMkZcsNAWhA6). Nothing else on the tab moves for them.
  - **The chip:** on the picked loop's header, 10 px after its length, 32 px tall (radius 16, the accent's tint with a 1 px accent outline), while smart loop end has something to say about the picked loop: its words ("48 ms late", "Snapped 48 ms earlier", "Started on your first hit, 80 ms after REC", "Close call") and its action as a button inside it, 26 px tall at its right end: **Snap** (filled), **Use my tap**, **Use my press** or **Read as 2 bars at 158 BPM** (outlined). It ends before Listen, the words cut to fit. A tap on the button does it; a tap on the words opens the loop point view, and again closes it (the chip's tint deepens while it is open). It goes once a side is kept, and when the loop changes in another way.
  - **The loop point view,** in the ruler's, the lanes' and the map's place (1092 x 280 px, radius 10, the surface with an outline): a head (LOOP POINT; "Now: your tap, 1 bar at 96.00 BPM"; **×** 36 px at its right); the **Start** and **End** panes side by side, each a title (30 px, for Move start / Move end's ◀ ▶) and a note over a 108 px window of 300 ms (the start's from 100 ms before your press, the end's from 200 ms before your tap) with the audio's peaks in the accent, the part outside the loop shaded, your press (REC) or tap as a grey dashed line, and what was found as an accent line with its label and the gap ("−48 ms") over a light accent band between the two; a 22 px line saying what the other side makes of the loop ("Snap makes it 1 bar at 97.87 BPM, from the loop's next downbeat. Your tap stays one tap away."), or, when loop memory was full as the loop closed and it isn't snapped, why the end can't move later ("Loop memory was full when it closed, so nothing after your tap was kept: the end can move earlier, not later."), or during A/B what it does ("A/B: each time round, the end runs into the start as your tap has it, then as Snap would. The tempo stays 92.00 BPM."), the End pane's note then saying whose end comes next ("now: the snap's end, into the start"); and a 44 px row: **Snap** (filled) and **Keep my tap**, or **Use my tap** (or **Use my press**) and **Keep the snap**, or during A/B **Keep the snap** and **Keep my tap**; **A/B** (lit while it runs); a rule; READ AS and its choices in a segmented well (up to three, "1 bar · 97.87 BPM", the reading now lit). With nothing found (a close call alone), **Keep my tap** alone and no A/B. Records from, Send and the undo history open over it.
  - **Move start / Move end** (the looper refactor's R2; Jay's card on the looper refactor thread, 25 Nov 2026 00:08Z, "Build it with Q-Links too", page https://claude.ai/artifact/USvi9R5rN9a42fbgbks6uK). Nothing else on the tab moves for it. The loop point view's head gains STEP and its three choices in a segmented well left of × (**1/16**, **1 ms**, **Hit**; 60 px each, 28 px tall, the step lit; the first is 1/16, and it holds for the session); each pane's title gains **◀** and **▶** (40 x 30 px, 4 px apart, 52 px from the title's start), moving that edge earlier or later by the step (Hit: to the next or previous hit in that window, more than 2 ms away); the hits heard in each window are short ticks under the peaks; a finger on a window puts that edge there, on a hit within 10 px, and a drag follows it. A move not yet kept is a white line with a grip, labelled "your move" and its gap from your press or tap ("−65 ms"); the 22 px line says what it makes ("Your move makes it 4 bars at 92.58 BPM, from the loop's next downbeat.", or "Your move can't be made: ..."), and the 44 px row is **Move** (filled) and **Cancel** (Cancel alone when it can't be made); A/B then plays the loop as it is against the move ("A/B: each time round, the end runs into the start as the loop has it, then as your move would. The tempo stays 92.00 BPM until you Move (then 92.58)."; the End pane's note "now: your move's end, into the start" or "now: the loop's own end, into the start"). A kept move lands like a snap, on the loop's next downbeat with the tempo; the chip then says "Moved 65 ms earlier" ("Start moved ...", "Moved: start ..., end ...") with **Use my tap**, the head "Now: moved by hand, ...", each moved pane's note "moved by hand, 65 ms before your tap" with your move drawn where it is, and the row **Use my tap** (filled) and **Keep it**. Each window recentres on its edge when the edge leaves its middle 80 %. While the view shows, whatever the Q-Link bank, **Q1** moves the start and **Q2** the end a step a click (their cards Start and End, with where each is from your press or tap), Q3 and Q4 are free, and the hint line says "Q1: move the start · Q2: move the end · a step a click (STEP: 1/16, 1 ms, Hit) · drag a window: put an edge there · ×: close · STOP: ...".
  - **Snap anyway and Not now:** while a snap would change the tempo under the audio tracks' recordings, the hint line asks in the warning colour ("Loop 1: that changes the tempo under the audio tracks' recordings. Snap anyway?") and two 36 px buttons float at its right end, 20 px from the edge, their top 43 px from the bottom: **Snap anyway** (filled, 132 px; **Do it anyway** for Read as, an Undo or a Redo) and **Not now** (100 px), 8 px apart.
  - **Read as and Discard** (Jay's card on the looper refactor thread, 24 Nov 2026 02:28Z, "Ask first, as mocked up"): while the picked loop's first pass waits because Length's bars would be slower than 30 BPM, the hint line asks in the warning colour ("Loop 1: Length's 1 bar would be 24 BPM, slower than 30. Read it as 4 bars at 96 BPM?") and the same two buttons float in the same place: **Read as 4 bars** (filled, 132 px) and **Discard** (100 px). This question comes before a snap's.
- **Send** shows, in the lanes' place, what to do with the whole loop: **Show in Clips** (opens Arrange with the loop's cell in view; Jay's sequencer decision 3), and faint until the looper's bounce is wired, **To a pad**, **To an audio track** and **Save WAV**.
- **Q-Links:** three banks, which Q-LINK steps through and the hint line names: **Loops 1-4** (their levels, the first bank), **Loops 5-8**, and **the picked loop** (Feedback, the picked layer's level and pan, the seam fade). While the loop point view shows, whatever the bank, Q1 and Q2 move its start and end (Move start / Move end, above) and Q3 and Q4 are free.
- **Hardware:** REC records or overdubs the picked loop, OVERDUB overdubs, UNDO undoes (SHIFT: redo), and on an empty loop that was cleared brings it back. ERASE + a layer's lane deletes it; ERASE + a ring or a loop's name clears the loop (step 3a), and UNDO brings either back.
