- **A loop on a Loop track** is named by its place (Loop tracks, [loop-tracks.md](loop-tracks.md)): the header's "Loops B · Verse" (the row's scene name, or "row 2"). The REC chip says it.
- **Loops on audio tracks** (the audio tracks rethink's phase 4, board 5; the phase 4 screens approved as drawn by Jay, 23 Nov 2026 19:24Z, https://claude.ai/artifact/KJDBaGjVb6orw27Zsvb1ff; from 4b behind Dev's "Loops on audio tracks", for everyone at 4e when Loop tracks go):
  - **The deck's cards are named by place** ("Vocal · row 2", the loop's name under it); a slot with no loop says "Free" and "REC into an empty audio cell fills it". Over the deck, "Loops 6 of 8" replaces the dots' gauge. The picked loop's header reads "Vocal · row 2 · 4 bars · 3 layers".
  - **M and S on a card are its track's** (a small "VOCAL'S TRACK" over them): muting Guitar mutes both its loops' cards. The loop's own mute and solo go; layer mute and solo stay in the lanes.
  - **Records from goes:** a loop records from its track's input (section 4c, The input), so the bar over the deck says where the next loop goes instead: "Records into Vocal · row 3 · Line L". Listen plays a loop through its track's channel.
  - **A layer not yet on the stick** shows an amber dot on its card, gone once its file is written (section 4f, Loops in audio cells).
- **The pads as loop keys** (step 2): SHIFT + SAMPLER pressed again on the Looper tab, or the **Pads as loop keys** switch at the middle of the bar over the deck (lit while on), makes the pads the loop keys, on the Looper tab and Play: pads 13-16 are Loops 1-4's keys and 9-12 Loops 5-8's (a press is a tap on the ring; held 1 s, the ring's hold), 5-8 Loops 1-4's mutes and 1-4 Loops 5-8's (at once; SHIFT + a mute pad solos, step 3a). The keys light as the rings (dark empty, blinking red waiting, red recording, amber overdubbing, the loop's colour playing with its first beat brighter, faint muted or left out by a solo; a mute pad is red while its loop is muted, blue while it is soloed). On Play the loop row's **Pads: loop keys** lights as the deck's does, and a tap gives the pads back (the header chip it had went in the looper redesign's step 5); the hint line says the layout on both. Elsewhere the pads play the kit, and are keys again on return. One pad mode at a time: STEP SEQ, TRACK MUTE and 16 Levels take the pads and turn the keys off. The page is the first of the Perform pads, kept apart from the kits (Jay, 23:52Z). **The Perform set** (the Play, Pads and Sequencer redesign's step 6a, board 7): the loop keys are page A of eight (PAD BANK A-H), saved with the project. While the set is on the pads, **PAD BANK** picks its page (SHIFT or a second press for E-H, as a kit's banks) and the lit bank is the page; page B is Play's eight throws on pads 1-8 (held while held; a latched throw plays until its next hit), C-H start empty; a page can also hold a track's mute (waiting for the launch line in Perform, as TRACK MUTE's pads) or a scene (a hit launches the row). The deck's switch lights for page A only and a tap on another page brings page A back; SHIFT + SAMPLER brings the set back at the page it was left on. On another page Play's chip reads "Pads: Perform B" (the page's letter; warn) and its tap gives the pads back, and the hint line names the page on both ("Pads: Perform set, Page B · Throws · PAD BANK: its pages (A: the loop keys) · SHIFT + SAMPLER: the pads play"). Throw pads light dim in their tile's colour and full while they play; a track mute pad is red while its track is muted, dim white otherwise; a scene pad dim green. Any throw a pad plays lets go when the set leaves the pads.
- **The steps still to come** (approved 22:55Z; 3a Clear with undo, ERASE + a ring, Once and loop solo, 3b Replace, 3c Speed, 4a Capture, 4b When I play, 4c the tempo from the first loop and 5 Play's loop row (section 4) are built): the footswitch Learn card.


## 4b. The FX chain editor and macros

Approved by Jay at 22:34Z (the mod thread's card, "Approve as proposed"). The mod thread builds them (crate `mpfree-chain-ui`, PR #102 merged; pictures in /mnt/project-files/mod/chain-ui/).

- **The chain editor:** a track's effects in order (on/off, up, down, remove, "+ Add effect", up to 8), the selected effect's knobs, and a Modulate panel for the knob last touched (put it on macro 1 to 8, learn it to a Q-Link, add an LFO or a follower).
- **Dragging an effect** (Jay, 14 Nov 12:03Z: drag the FX up and down the chain besides Up and Down): a finger on an effect's row that moves more than a fingertip (12 px) lifts it, drawn under the finger with an accent rim while its place stays outlined; a 4 px accent line shows where it lands, and it moves when the finger lifts. Lifted where it started, or cancelled, nothing moves; a tap still picks it. Up and Down stay. The wheel moves it too (section 6).
- **Where it opens:** from a mixer strip's insert row, where it takes the mixer's place with a Back to the mixer; and from the Pads tab's Effects row, on the pad's own channel in the Mix tab with ‹ back to the Pads tab (section 3a). The Sample tab's FX sub-tab, with its Pad / Track switch, went with the one Sample page (Jay, 18 Nov 11:46Z). The insert row works as the mixer already sends it (FX thread).
- **Macros:** the Q-Link column can show "Macros 1-4" or "Macros 5-8" as a bank, on Play and while the chain editor is open. A long press on a macro card opens the full Macros and Morph panel (8 macros, up to 8 targets each, and a morph between two saved scenes A and B) over the page and under the top bar, as the mixer does. Play keeps its views and no tab is added.
- **Q-Links:** the column is the width the page gives it (128 px), not a wider one. While the chain editor is open they turn the first four knobs of the selected effect's Main page, whichever page is shown, for an effect with a Main page (Jay, 14 Nov: layout B), else the first four knobs of the page shown, with Macros 1-4 and 5-8 as banks. Their cards use the mixer's card shape. A Q-Link the user learns to a knob belongs to the page it was learned on; Macros 1-8 work on every page.
- **The Q-Link hook:** the app routes Q-Link turns per page in one place (main.rs, the `QLinkTurn` arm). A macro bank, when shown, takes the turns there first.
- **Built (round 10):** a strip's insert row opens the editor on that effect, and its "+" opens it with the effect picker up; it takes the mixer's place with the Mix tab lit, and Back opens the mixer again on the view it showed (Track Mix, Pad Mix and so on). Q-LINK steps the column through the effect's knobs, Macros 1-4 and Macros 5-8; a learn takes the next Q-Link turned. The wheel is Q-Link 1 until a tap puts the outline on a control (section 6). A finger held for half a second on a macro card (Play or the editor) opens the macros panel on that card's bank, and its Back goes back to the page it opened over. Still to come: Sample > FX (the sampler's sub-tab), the effect's own page from a tap on its name, and keeping the macro set-up and learned Q-Links in the store.
- **Mode row** (Jay, 14 Nov: effects with modes): under the effect's name, an effect in a family (`mpfree_fx::families`) shows a chip per mode, or < and > around the mode's name and its place ("Tube   3 of 10") when the chips don't fit. A tap switches the mode in place, keeping the slot (target `chain/mode`).
- **Pages** (Jay, 14 Nov: layout B): an effect with more than eight controls opens on its Main page, the eight a player reaches for (`mpfree_fx::main_page`), then its groups. One with eight or fewer, or a plugin, shows its groups, a group over eight split into pages of eight ("EQ 2"). The tabs have a row of their own under the Mode row; when they don't fit, < and > at its ends, the same arrows as the Mode row, step the page, and the row keeps the page shown in view. An effect with stages it switches (the Channel strip's gate, compressor and EQ) shows them on its Main page under the tabs, each with its on/off pill and a way to its page.
- **The effect page frame** (Jay, 14 Nov 13:41Z, "Build it as drawn" on the FX thread's effect page canvas, https://claude.ai/artifact/BbZs7t2hSJ2ZacDrMq5hqu, to the same rules as the Synth tab, section 4g; the FX thread builds it after the Mix tab, and until it lands the editor is as the lines above): the Synth tab's frame. At the left (220 px), the strip's effects: ‹ and the strip button, the inserts with their on pills, Add effect, then Up, Down and Remove. In the middle (884 px), the selected effect's page. At the right, the Q-Link column (128 px), turning the four the effect's plan picks on every page. The header holds the effect's name (its presets open from it), then its Mode chooser (chips when the modes fit; else a chip that opens the list of modes, with ‹ › beside it, as for Drive's ten), or page tabs, only for an effect that needs more than one page (the Channel strip, Perform FX). The page is labelled sections from the effect's plan, each drawing inside its section, as on the Synth tab; the Channel strip's stages are sections with their switches. The Modulate panel becomes one 54 px row under the page (56 on the canvas: the header 52, the page 540 and the row 54, with 10 between each, fill the 666 px), for the control last touched: macros 1 to 8, Learn, + LFO, + Follower, and a way to its matrix rows (shown over the page).
- **Sidechain** (Jay, 18 Nov 2026 11:04Z, "OK, sounds good. Merge and continue." on the FX thread's sidechain canvas, https://claude.ai/artifact/TVZ5ZkVornPfxxYCJNRKr5; on the screens the word is sidechain, never key, Jay 11:00Z): an effect that can listen to another track's sound (the Compressor, the Gate, the Ducker, the Vocoder on Input) has one row for it on its page, in place of its own switch. The Compressor's and the Gate's read **Sidechain: Off | Kick ›**, the Ducker's **Source: Kick › | Tempo**; the second button names the track it listens to ("Pick a track ›" when none is picked). A tap on that button while it is lit, or while nothing is picked, opens the **sidechain list**; a tap on it while Off is lit goes back to the track picked before, so the two can be compared. The Vocoder keeps Carrier (Saw, Noise, Input); on Input its Carrier heading reads "Voice: Mic ›" (or "Pick a voice ›") and a tap there opens the list. Picking a track turns the effect's switch on and Off turns it off; the track picked is saved with the project and left out of effect presets. Set to listen with nothing picked (a project whose track is gone, an automated switch), the section's note says so in red ("Nothing picked: it won't duck") and the effect's row in the chain column reads "Nothing picked"; nothing falls back quietly. Adding a Ducker (it starts on Sidechain) opens the list at once. **The sidechain list** is the strip list (above) over the editor: "Off" (listens to its own track) above the groups, only the groups that can feed this strip (a strip hears tracks earlier in the mix: inputs, pads and tracks feed anything after them, a strip of the same kind unless that makes a loop, a submix only lower-numbered submixes; a line under the groups says which can't and why), the tiles as the strip list's, and a strip the mixer would refuse greyed with why ("this strip", or "it listens to this one" when the two would loop). The head reads "SIDECHAIN FOR KEYS · COMPRESSOR · TAP WHAT IT LISTENS TO" with what it hears now ("Now: Kick") and Back. On the Gate the KEY section is TRIGGER, and the Gate's and the Ducker's Key HP and Key LP are SC low cut and SC high cut. Built, with these details for Jay to confirm: no level bar on the list's tiles (the strip meters own the peaks); no UNDO for the switch yet, as for the rest of the chain editor (section 6).
- **The Notes stage** (Jay, 17 Nov 19:19Z, "Approve as drawn" on board 7 of the MIDI screens canvas, https://claude.ai/artifact/9W8R9NDy4KJUcScN9DoTh6; the MIDI thread builds it, design in [note-fx.md](note-fx.md)): a note track's chain has two groups in the left column. NOTES, the track's MIDI effects and its arp in the order they run (on pills, "+ MIDI effect", up to 8 besides the arp; four rows show, then ‹ › on its caption scroll it), sits above SOUND, the effects on its sound as before (windowed the same way when both don't fit). Between them a Plays on row says where the notes go (its own sound, or a MIDI out port and channel) and opens the MIDI section's Tracks out. A MIDI effect's row opens its page in the effect page frame: its choices as chips (Chord's shapes, Scale's root), a keyboard with what goes in and what comes out, its settings in sections, a Modulate row, and the Q-Links on the four the effect's plan picks. The arp's row opens a page with what it plays and a way to its panel; it can move among the MIDI effects but not be removed. "+ MIDI effect" opens a picker of the MIDI effects as tiles over the page. Up, Down and Remove act on the row picked in either group. Returns, the master and Loop tracks have no Notes group.
- **The XY chips** (board 5 of the XY mockups, Jay's approval of the XY mockups, 23 Nov 2026 10:34Z; section 4o): the Modulate row gets **XY →** and **XY ↑** (each 60 × 44 px) between the control's name and the macro numbers, which narrow from 40 to 34 px to make room. A tap puts the control on that direction of the layout the XY pad plays, over its whole range, Even, Set; on a ready-made layout MPFree first makes a layout of yours from it, and the hint line says so. The chip is lit while the control is on that direction, and a tap on the lit chip takes it off; a control on the other direction moves. The hint line names what happened ("Tone (Drums) moves up on Squelch, 5.0 to 20 kHz · XY ↑ again: take it off · its range and curve: Setup on the XY page"). A control the pad can't move (a switch) shows the chips greyed, and a tap says why.
- **XY marks on knobs** (board 6, the same approval): a control on the layout the pad plays wears a badge at its dial's top left, **X→** or **Y↑**, in the theme's text colour with ink on it, so it never looks like a macro's coloured M1 to M8; under a macro's badge when it has one. A thin arc in the text colour outside its ring shows the stretch the pad covers, from a tick at the layout's from to an arrow at its to, so a backwards one's arrow runs the other way. A dot in the second accent on that arc shows where the setting really is now (for Add, its own value plus the pad's push), hollow while a recorded move plays it (the XY plan's phase 3). The chain editor, the effect pages and the Synth tab draw them.

## 4c. Audio tracks

**Approved by Jay on 12 Nov 2026 (05:33Z), as proposed** in mockups A to C (/mnt/project-files/audiotracks/mockup/). **Audio tracks as tracks** (the audio tracks rethink's phase 1, [plan](https://claude.ai/artifact/2rrcXW5CTeSmEuMA6EWwu7)) **approved by Jay on 22 Nov 2026 (07:28Z), as drawn** in https://claude.ai/artifact/WGpqj1pHAJT8MEixRPg6kT (boards A to D): the audio block under the MIDI lanes became audio tracks in the one track list. **Audio clips in the grid** (the rethink's phase 2) **approved by Jay on 22 Nov 2026 (23:20Z), as drawn** in https://claude.ai/artifact/A7K7GE4ZAxyjNiJgQJbncu (boards A to E; screens `arrange-clips-audio-cells`, `arrange-clips-audio-card`, `arrange-clips-audio-fitting`, `arrange-clips-audio-once` and `arrange-song-audio-blocks` in `mpfree --preview`): an audio track's cells hold audio clips (section 4f), made with To a cell (below) and Put in a cell (section 4i), set on the Clip tab (section 4j), and blocks of them go in the song (below). No tab is added: an audio track is recorded and edited in Arrange's Song view. The audio tracks thread owns the engine and the lanes' wiring; the time thread draws the lanes and take lanes.

- **One track list.** An audio track is a track like any other, in track order with note tracks and Loop tracks, and counts toward the 128. A new project still starts with 8 note tracks and no audio track. There is no audio block under the note lanes, no divider, no "+ Audio" and no limit of 8 audio tracks of its own (the engine plays 8 for now: a ninth is refused, and says so).
- **+ Track** (the Clips grid's title line and the lane after the Song view's last lane): a tap opens a chooser under it with **Note track** (what + Track added before), **Audio track** ("Audio N", input Left, armed, its lane or column in view) and **Loop track**. SHIFT + "+ Track" still adds a Loop track at once, until Loop tracks join audio tracks. UNDO takes the new track away.
- **The lane:**
  - It shares the ruler, the playhead, bar scrolling and the lane height (up to 56 px) with the other lanes. Every audio track has its lane, whether it holds anything or not.
  - Regions are blocks in the track's colour with the take's waveform. An older take has a "take N" tag, and fades show as slopes.
  - While recording, a red block grows from the punch in.
  - A tap selects a region. The selection has a kind (placement or audio region).
  - A sound from the Sample tab (the Slice tool's Put on Audio track, step 6) goes on the first empty audio track, else the first with nothing from that bar on, from the start of the bar the playhead is in, a region a slice; with none, it goes on a new audio track at the end of the track list. Undo takes it off in one go.
- **The header (the 96 px name area):**
  - It keeps the name, the arm dot (tap to arm), the input chip (L, R, L+R, ST) and the 3 px level bar along its bottom edge.
  - A tap on the name opens the track panel.
- **The track panel** (over the lanes, under the track's lane if it fits) keeps Arm, Mute, Solo, Takes, Input (Left by default) and Monitor (Auto, In, Off).
  - **Level and Pan are the track's mixer strip's:** the same fader and pan as in the Mix tab, moved from either place, and the strip's effects and sends follow. **Its strip in Mix ›** opens the Mix tab on that strip.
  - **Mute and Solo are the track's**, as on any track (TRACK MUTE, the grid): Solo plays this track alone among all the project's tracks.
  - **Mix** (the instrument redesign's step 9, approved 20 Nov): Input's fifth choice (the four line chips narrow to 44 px to make room, the monitor chips to 50 px). With Mix picked the header's chip reads "Mix", the panel grows one row under Input: **From** (‹, the source's name, ›: the instruments' channels, Drums, Synth, Loop, each submix, Master) and **Effects** (Before, After; After by default). The monitor modes give way to the words "Not played through" (a track recording from the mix never plays it through). An L, R, L+R or ST chip goes back to the line input.
- **The input** (the audio tracks rethink's phase 4, board 4; the phase 4 screens approved as drawn by Jay, 23 Nov 2026 19:24Z, https://claude.ai/artifact/KJDBaGjVb6orw27Zsvb1ff; from 4b behind Dev's "Loops on audio tracks", for everyone at 4e when Loop tracks go): a tap on an audio column's input line, or the lane's, opens **its input list**, for its loops and its takes alike: **Line** (L, R, L+R mono, Stereo); **Pads** ("Pads · Kit 1, with its clips": the instrument the pads play, after its effects and fader, as a Loop track's Pads is today); **Mix › a strip** (after its fader); **Mix › Master** (before the master's effects, all you hear but this track). An input the mixer refuses is dim, and the line under its group says why ("Vocal plays into Master through Bus A: pick another input, or take Vocal's send off Bus A"). A loop takes the input its track has when its first pass starts; each overdub takes the input it has then.
- **Q-Links: Play's Q-Link column, always on Arrange** (Jay's pick on 12 Nov 2026 at 06:01Z, mockup E in /mnt/project-files/audiotracks/mockup/qlinks-right/).
  - It's the same 128 px column with four vertical cards as on Play, at the right edge from under the top bar to the bottom. The Record and Takes panels narrow to about 250 px to make room, and the arp chip leaves the transport bar on Arrange. (The Record panel's Clip tab, the clip settings card, is in the same place: section 4j.)
  - The cards follow the selection. With an audio region selected, or the track panel open, they are Q1 Level, Q2 Pan (both the track's mixer strip's, as in the Mix tab), Q3 the region's gain and Q4 its fade.
  - With nothing selected, they are Play's knobs, the same cards as on Play. The mockup showed the armed track's level, pan and sends, but a sequencer track has no mixer strip of its own yet: every track plays the kit. Track knobs come when tracks get strips.
  - Q-LINK steps to Macros 1-4 and 5-8 as on Play, and Play and Arrange share the bank. The track panel no longer lists the Q-Links at its foot.
- **The bottom row with an audio region selected:** Split · Erase · Fades · Gain · Takes · **To a cell**, instead of the placement buttons. To a cell took Undo's place (phase 2); UNDO and SHIFT + UNDO undo and redo the region edits while they are what changed last (a take made since, such as To a cell's, takes UNDO back to the takes).
  - To a cell puts the region in its track's first empty cell, top down, as a clip as long as the region, named after the track and its bars ("Vocal 5–8"; Name on the Clip tab renames it).
  - The hint line says where it went ("Vocal, bars 5 to 8: in row 3 · UNDO takes it out").
  - A track with no empty cell is refused, with the reason ("Vocal has no empty cell").
  - The song doesn't change.
  - Dragging a region moves it, snapped to the grid (1/16 by default). SHIFT + drag moves it freely.
  - With nothing selected, the row is the Song view's own.
- **Blocks of audio clips** (phase 2, step 2d; board E): Rec song records an audio clip's launches as blocks on its track's lane. Recorded regions under such a block are replaced by it, as one step either UNDO takes back (Joshi's pick, 23 Nov 2026 04:38Z).
  - A block has a header strip in the track's colour with the clip's name ("Outro · once" for a once clip). The wave sits under the strip, drawn pass after pass with a faint line at each pass, so a block never looks like a region. On a lane under 24 px the strip goes and the name sits over the wave.
  - A block and a region never overlap. A block dropped, moved or stretched over a region is refused, with the reason ("Riff can't go over the region at bar 9: a block and a region never overlap on a lane"); while it is dragged, its outline is red where it would land over one.
  - A take recorded over a block cuts the block at the take's edges, and either UNDO takes back both. So does other sound put on the lane: a swipe on a take lane, a region dragged, a sound brought in (Joshi's pick, 23 Nov 2026 10:25Z).
  - A block stays on its lane, or goes to another audio track. A note clip's block never goes on an audio track's lane, nor an audio clip's on another lane.
- **Take lanes (comping):**
  - Takes opens the track's takes as thin lanes (about 28 px each) under its lane, newest first. They borrow height from the other lanes.
  - The Song view gains a vertical lane scroll: a drag on the name column. SHIFT + wheel keeps scrolling the bars, as it already did on the Song view (12 Nov 06:45Z, time thread and app thread).
  - Swiping across a take lane plays that stretch on the track, snapped to the grid (SHIFT + swipe is free). Tapping a whole take lane uses that take everywhere it has audio.
- **Buttons:**
  - REC records an armed audio track in song mode as the transport records. There is no new button.
  - An audio track plays its launched clip, and otherwise, in song mode, its song (phase 2). Rec song records audio clip launches as blocks. It still doesn't arm audio tracks.
- **What REC records** (the audio tracks rethink's phase 4, board 3; the phase 4 screens approved as drawn by Jay, 23 Nov 2026 19:24Z, https://claude.ai/artifact/KJDBaGjVb6orw27Zsvb1ff; from 4b behind Dev's "Loops on audio tracks", for everyone at 4e when Loop tracks go): song mode on, every armed audio track records a take on the song ("REC → Vocal · Line L", "REC → Vocal +2 · takes"). Song mode off, REC records into the armed cell: an empty cell gets a loop ("REC → Vocal · row 3 · Line L"), a loop cell an overdub ("REC → Vox loop · overdub"). One loop records at a time: REC on another cell while a loop records closes that loop on the launch line and starts the new one, and the chip says so first, in amber ("REC → Bass · row 2 · closes Vocal's loop"). With 8 loops in cells REC into an empty cell is refused: "REC: 8 loops · clear one", in red.
- **Memory:** "Audio 0:42 of 1:00 free" on the Song view's legend line while any audio track exists.
- **Prefs:** two rows come with it:
  - **Audio track memory:** 60 s stereo by default, applied at the next start.
  - **Line input latency:** in frames, shared with the looper.

  They go before System info when built, and Touch screen stays last.

## 4d. MIDI

**Agreed by Jay on 12 Nov 2026** (08:23Z "go for it" on the MIDI thread's mockups, https://claude.ai/artifact/5ygP92smCQVhu31qq2kfYZ, and the design doc's decisions, https://claude.ai/artifact/RxdZ76ve5hE5VptdkU3JMo; 08:26Z "I agree with all of your other recommendations"). The MIDI thread builds it.

- **The tempo chip** reads "120.0 from MIDI In", with a green dot, while the tempo follows an outside clock.
- **A track's settings on Arrange:** Instrument gains "External MIDI", with one "Plays on" field that holds the port and the channel together. Under the track name it says "MIDI Out · ch 1", or "Sends nothing: no port" in the warn colour. There is one MIDI output setting per track, never two look-alike settings (Jay, 12 Nov 07:00Z).
- **Dev's MIDI log page** gains port and kind filters, and Clock, Timing and Counts panels.
- **MIDI Learn:** SHIFT + a touch on a value. It takes values only: SHIFT + drag on an audio region and SHIFT + swipe on a take lane keep their jobs (section 4c). Built 17 Nov for the mixer's Faders look (a strip's fader, pan, sends, M and S), the chain editor, the macros panel, the Synth tab and Play's tempo: the hint line names what Learn waits for ("LEARNING Volume (Bass): move a knob, a key or a pedal on any controller"), then what the control moves; SHIFT + the same value again cancels. SHIFT's hint line on those pages starts "touch a value: MIDI Learn". **On Arrange's Clips grid** (sequencer S5, Jay's card of 18 Nov 00:10Z, "Both: grid taps and a Learn card") SHIFT + a tap learns a button: a cell (its tap, launching whatever clip that track and row hold), a row's number (its scene), Stop all, Back to song, Capture scene (in Perform, section 4f) or a track's name (its arm). It acts on the lift, so SHIFT + a drag still copies a clip or a row, and SHIFT + "+ Track" still adds a Loop track; SHIFT's hint line there reads "tap a clip, a scene or a track's name: MIDI Learn". The Learn section's Clips and scenes card adds a track's mute, solo and stop (built 18 Nov; see the Learn section below).
- **The MIDI, sync and CV settings** are the project's, in the mixer's I/O view (section 2, the Mix tab's I/O chip). Facts about the rig (CV tuning, port names) stay in Prefs.
- **A port's page** (board 6 of the MIDI powerhouse mockups, https://claude.ai/artifact/9W8R9NDy4KJUcScN9DoTh6, all eight boards approved by Jay on 16 Nov 18:35Z): each port's row in the I/O view's MIDI section gains a Page field, **Open ›**, which (or a tap on the port's name) opens the port's page over the section; **‹ MIDI** or Back closes it. Left: This port (what it is, Input), Ignore coming in (Clock, Program, Aftertouch, All notes off), What it does. Right: Now, Clock to this port (the Sync section's setting, never on the same screen; under it the board's Divide by and Nudge, built 17 Nov: on one line, "Divide" and "Nudge" with small − value + each, where the card is 350 px inside or more, as on the MPC's screen, else one under the other; then a line with the clock as it left, "Pulses leave every 20.8 ms, ±0.3 ms", or why it is only kept), This gear's delay (kept with the rig, so the delay is on the page rather than in Prefs), and Send to it (labelled SENDS MIDI: Test note, All notes off). On the MPC's screen the four Ignore chips sit in one row (the smaller letters below 96 px), so What it does keeps its lines. Q-Links 1 to 4: Input, Clock, the delay, Divide.
- **The Routing section** (board 5 of the same mockups, approved 16 Nov 18:35Z; Jay chose it as the home of keyboards on several tracks on 17 Nov 02:27Z): a section of the I/O view after MIDI, its blocks built 18 Nov. A grid: rows are the sources (each port with an input, The pads, then the tracks), columns are Armed track, Tracks by channel, Learn, each MIDI out port and CV out; the columns share the width after a 150 px name column and drop MIDI out ports that don't fit ("N more outputs: on the MIDI section"). A square shows its setting in two lines, a green bar for "plays in MPFree" and a blue one for "sends out"; "+" where a tap adds a route, "—" where nothing can go (the pads under an output say whether the armed track plays its kit there). Under the grid, a 214 px card for the picked square: its On, Off or + Route and Remove route, for Tracks by channel up to three route lines, each a channel stepper and a track stepper, and for thru (a port's square under a MIDI out port, board 5's "thru · ch 10 only"; built 17 Nov) up to three lines with a channel stepper each. Wheel: move across the squares; - and +: take a route away or add it; Q-Links 1 and 2: the picked route's channel and track. **Approved by Jay, 22 Nov 2026 22:40Z: "OK all four as written" on step 11's layout card (the instrument redesign's step 11e):** a route line's track stepper goes on past the last track to the synths no track has of its own, by their channel names ("Synth", "FM · Pads"), and the square, the card's title and Q-Link 2's card name the synth the same way; past the last synth the hint line says so. A port's Armed track and Learn squares are its Input (the same setting as on its row and page; both off is refused), a track's square under a port is its Plays on, Tracks by channel are the project's routes, and a port's square under a MIDI out port is its thru to that output (the project's; "—" for a USB device's own output). The MIDI section's Thru field on a port's row is the same setting, as one choice: Off or the output its every channel goes to. **Blocks** (board 5's tiles): a port's Armed track card shows its blocks at once, under its title "… → ARMED TRACK · ITS BLOCKS, IN ORDER"; a route's or thru's line ends in a Blocks › button that turns the card to that route's blocks, its main button then ‹ Routes. Up to four tiles (106 px tall, at most 150 wide) with a › between two (a tap swaps them) and a + Block tile, which turns the row into its five kinds and Cancel; the picked tile has an accent edge and a × at its corner; the Q-Links turn the picked block. Under the tiles, the last line: "Last through", the message, →, what it became, where it went and when. Built, with these details for Jay to confirm: a square counts its blocks with a small badge in its corner, not board 5's "1 block" line (a square's two lines stay its channel and state); the Velocity tile draws its curve, without board 5's dashed straight line; the Q-Links' jobs show in the Q-Link strip, not in a column beside the tiles.
- **The Learn section** (board 8 of the same mockups, approved 16 Nov 18:35Z; built 17 Nov, its MIDI Follow card the same evening): a section of the I/O view between Routing and Sync (the list reads Audio, MIDI, Routing, Learn, Sync, CV, Monitor). It replaces the MIDI section's "MIDI Learn" rows. While Learn waits for a control, a 48 px banner on top: a LEARNING chip, what it will take ("Volume (Bass): now move a knob, a key or a pedal on any controller") and Cancel. Then the heading "WHAT YOUR CONTROLLERS MOVE · N" with the key (RIG: every project · PROJECT: this song), and a table: Control, Moves, Now (a bar), Kept in (a RIG or PROJECT chip); rows 40 px, the picked one outlined in the second accent. Under it a 244 px card for the picked row: its title ("CC 74 → CUTOFF"), Learn again and Remove at the right, then four lines: Range (two steppers, low "to" high, in the parameter's units), Works as (a stepper), Takeover (Jump, Pick up, Scale; greyed unless Knob or fader) and Kept in (Rig, Project, then "every project" or "this song"), and a line saying what the takeover does. Wheel: pick a row; - and +: Works as; Q-Links 1 to 4: low, high, Works as, Takeover. A **Transport and tempo** button at the heading's right (the key moves to its left) swaps the card for the transport's eight (PLAY and STOP, PLAY, STOP, PLAY START, REC, OVERDUB, TAP TEMPO, Tempo; two rows of four, with Back); with nothing learned the card shows them on its own. Board 8 lists such rows (Tempo, PLAY and STOP) without saying where they are learned; this is that place. **Play** (Jay's card of 18 Nov 18:48Z, "A Play card in Learn"; built 18 Nov), a 64 px button left of Transport and tempo, swaps the card for Play's own controls that SHIFT + a touch on Play can't learn (those touches keep their jobs): PLAY with Back at the right, a line of words, then Swing, Filter (the one Play's Filter Q-Link turns: track 1's, which Play calls Drums, whatever the project names it; the rig stores only "play.1"), XY across and XY up on the first row of the transport card's grid, then **XY Hold**, **XY layout next** and **XY layout back** on its second row (board 10 of the XY mockups, Jay's approval of the XY mockups, 23 Nov 2026 10:34Z ("Approve, start phase 2"; canvas https://claude.ai/artifact/X2A8nsHiwCf6S6PuZQDq4n, pictures in mpfree-claude `xy-controller/mockups/`)), and faint lines at its foot (Swing and Filter turn as Play's Q-Links do; XY across and up move the pad as a finger does, its effect playing until both are back, across in the middle and up at the bottom, and on a layout of yours a knob holds the pad while it moves and lets go half a second after, as any learned knob does; XY Hold is the pad's Hold, a press switching it; the layout buttons step through this song's layouts of yours, or the ready-made ones while there are none, letting go of what the old one held first). **Next patch** takes the second row's fourth place and **Previous patch** starts a third row under it (live shows C1, Jay's approval of the patch screens, 23 Nov 2026 10:32Z: "Next patch and Previous patch on its grid"; the third row, and the faint lines moving beside Previous patch to make room for it, are details for Jay to confirm). Each is a press that steps the song's patches from any page while the song has patches, and a learned one's card reads **Switches** in Takeover's place: At once (the default) or Hands up, the line under the card saying "When your hands are up: the patch changes once every key and pedal is up." Play's own touches stay as they are. A tap arms Learn on one; the control is kept in the rig. **Clips and scenes** (built 18 Nov 2026; Jay's card of 00:10Z, "Both: grid taps and a Learn card"), a 136 px button left of Play, swaps the card for what SHIFT + a tap on the Clips grid can't learn: on the transport card's grid, a track's – name + stepper and its Mute, Solo and Stop, then Stop all and Back to song, with Back; the words say it is kept with this song. - and + step the track. The heading's buttons show whether or not anything is learned, each lit while its card shows; a tap on the lit one shows the picked row's card again. The key sits between the heading's words and the buttons where there is room for it. Built, with these details for Jay to confirm: the button's place beside Transport and tempo, and the card's two rows. **Looper** (the looper redesign's footswitch, approved by Jay 17 Nov 22:55Z with the redesign; built 18 Nov), a 76 px button left of Clips and scenes, swaps the card for the looper's on the transport card's two rows, five to a row (smart loop end step 2d, 22 Nov 2026): LOOPER with Back at the right, a line of words, a Loop stepper (− "Picked loop" or "Loop 1" to "Loop 8" +; - and + step it too) in the first place, 164 px wide so its words fit, that loop's Key and Mute, Next loop and Capture beside it, then Undo, Redo, Clear and smart loop end's Snap and Use my tap, and a faint line at its foot (a key held a second clears its loop; the MPC One has no footswitch jack). Where the heading's words don't fit before the buttons (the MPC's screen, where MIDI Follow's button shows too) they read "LEARNED · N". Built, with these details for Jay to confirm: the Looper button's place left of Clips and scenes, and the "LEARNED · N" heading where five buttons leave no room. **Show** (live shows B1; Jay approved it with the Show view, 22 Nov 2026 08:11Z, "Approve as drawn"; built 22 Nov), a 64 px button left of Looper, swaps the card for a set's controls on the transport card's grid: SHOW with Back at the right, the line "Tap one, then press a footswitch or a button on your controller. It works in every project while a set is open.", then Next song, Previous song, Go, Cancel next, Back to the set order, Panic (as SHIFT + STOP, plus notes off on every MIDI out) and Click, and a faint line at its foot (a press counts once: a second within 50 ms is the same press, and presses while a song changes are ignored). These are kept in the rig. A control learned to two jobs in the same place (on its port, or on any port) shows both rows in red with what else it does ("also Panic"). With Play and Show the heading's buttons are 64 (Show), 76 (Looper), 136, 64, 164 (Transport and tempo) and 104 px (MIDI Follow), 8 px apart. **MIDI Follow**, board 8's right card: 340 px wide beside the picked row's card (or the transport's), which keeps the rest of the width (at least the 554 px its Range line needs; 562 on the MPC's screen); MIDI FOLLOW with Learn knobs at the right (Done, lit, while it listens), then Controller (− name +), Follow (Off, On), Knobs (the CCs, with Clear for the controller's own) and up to three lines saying what it does or why it waits. Board 8's Send values back row (built 18 Nov) is Off and On segments: on the Follow line, after a 150 px label, where the card is wide enough (the MPC's screen swaps the card in at its full width), else a "Send back" line at the card's foot, under one line of what Follow does. In a section narrower than the two cards, a MIDI Follow button at the heading's right (the other buttons move to its left, and the key is left out) swaps the card for it.
- **The Controller section** (MIDI powerhouse 3.10; Jay's card, 18 Nov 2026 01:21Z, "A Controller switch in I/O": an I/O section with an On switch; the pads send notes and the Q-Links CCs out of one port; MPFree's sounds ignore the pads until it is off, and the top bar says so). A form section of the I/O view between Learn and Sync (the list reads Audio, MIDI, Routing, Learn, Controller, Sync, CV, Monitor), columns 230 px then four of 130. Under the heading "The MPC as a controller for your gear": Controller mode (Off, On, and a line saying what it does or why it sends nothing), Sends to (port, channel), Pads send (Kit notes or One by one, From; a line with what A1 and A16 send), Pad pressure (Off, Poly, Channel). Under "Q-Links: what each sends": a row per bank, Q-Links 1-4 to 13-16, four fields each showing the CC's name (the port's profile, else the common name, else "CC 20"), its line the numbers. While it is on, on every page: the pads light blue (bright while held) and play the gear; PAD BANK picks their bank and Q-LINK the Q-Links' (section 6); the Q-Link column's four cards show the bank's CCs (name, value sent last); the hint line's Q-Link part reads "Q-Links: your gear's CCs", and Play's hint says what the pads, PAD BANK and Q-LINK do; the chip in the top bar (section 2). Built; Jay confirmed the chip's place, this section's place and the Q-Link column on every page (card, 18 Nov 2026 07:19Z, "Keep all three as built").
- **The Sync section's "From other gear" rows** (MIDI powerhouse, 18 Nov 2026; Jay's 21:02Z go to finish the MIDI work after the 3.10.1 guide check): a heading **From other gear** (column "Does") between Clock arriving and Clock out, with two rows in the section's own form, each a choice field and a line under its name. **MMC in**: Off or On; the line reads "MIDI Machine Control does nothing" (faint) or "its MIDI Machine Control plays, stops, records and moves the playhead". **Program change in**: Off, **Launches a scene** or **Picks a patch** (live shows C1, Jay's approval of the patch screens, 23 Nov 2026 10:32Z); the line reads "does nothing here" (faint), "program 1 launches scene 1, 2 scene 2, and so on" or "program 1 picks patch 1, 2 patch 2, and so on" (past the song's last patch it does nothing, and the MIDI monitor says so: "nothing: the song has no patch 9"). While it picks a patch, a **Switches** row under it: At once (the default; "the patch changes as the program change comes in") or When your hands are up ("the patch changes once every key and pedal is up"). Both are the project's, as the rest of Sync is (the MPC keeps them in Preferences > Sync). The MIDI monitor says where each went: "MMC: the transport", "launched scene 3", "picked patch 2". Nothing is added elsewhere; MPFree sends no MMC.

## 4e. The Pads tab

**Picked by Jay on 12 Nov 2026** (15:46Z, "Pads + Browse / Pad switch"; his ask at 08:11Z: "the pads are the centerpiece of the MPC, and deserve their own top menu"). Mockups pads-1-browse and pads-2-pad in /mnt/project-files/hello-mpc/pads-mockups/. Built to them by the app thread on 12 Nov (screens `pads-browse` and `pads-pad` in `mpfree --preview`).

**Changed by the Play, Pads and Sequencer redesign** (Jay approved it 18 Nov 00:30Z: "Beautiful! Ok, let's build it!"; doc https://claude.ai/artifact/KHBG7mNbDFU4uZCFqNp9dW, mockups https://claude.ai/artifact/9UD77LP6xGPnMuHPMY9vhr): the Browse view is gone. The bar's **Browse for A5** opens the browser over the page (section 3, Not tabs), and the tab is the pads beside the pad's settings (screens `pads-pad` and `browser-samples`).

- **Its place:** a top-level tab, after Arrange and before Mix since Jay's order of 18 Nov 07:28Z (it was between Play and Arrange from 12 Nov).
- **The pad bank on the left** (400 to 440 px), as the Sample tab's Pads sub-page had it: the bank's 16 pads as on the MPC (pad 1 bottom left), each with its name, layers and play mode (another type: its type in the top right corner, LOOP, SYNTH or MIDI, and under the name what it plays, "2 bars", "Poly C4", "ch 10 · 49"); a tap picks a pad, a tap on the pad picked plays it; pads held on the hardware light up. Under it: Copy, Paste, Clear and Map to keys.
- **The kit's banks over the pads** (sampler R3 part 3, Jay's decision 1, 16 Nov; mockup 5 of the sampler review): a strip of the kit's banks A to H, each with a 4 × 4 dot for every pad of it that has a sound (pad 1 bottom left, in the pads' colours), the bank shown outlined. The strip answers over its whole width. A tap on a bank puts it on the pads, as PAD BANK does, and the Pads and Sample tabs go to the same pad of that bank; the bank on the pads always shows here. The panel's heading says the bank ("bank B").
- **The Pads of row** (sampler R3 part 3, mockup 5; wording agreed with the sequencer thread, 17 Nov; "Kit of" until the redesign's board 7): a 52 px row under the bar. **PADS OF**, then a chip for each track that plays a kit, with the kit's name and colour (the armed track outlined), and the tracks with clips that play the synth or an instrument of their own, faint ("synth, no kit"). Every track plays kit 1 until a pick, so a track is listed when it is armed, has a clip, or plays another kit than kit 1; Loop tracks have none. A tap on a track arms it, so the pads play its kit. **+ Kit track** after them adds a track at the end (Arrange's + Track, `TimeAction::AddTrack`, one UNDO) and, once it is armed, gives it the first empty kit; with none left it says so. At the right end, past a rule, a 300 px **Perform pads** chip ("one set for the project"; not "Perform", which the top bar's MAKE / PERFORM chip is, section 2) shows the Perform view (below) in the kit's place; while it shows, the Perform pads chip is the one outlined and a tap on any enabled track (the armed one too) goes back to the kit's pads. Tracks that don't fit are left out, the armed one kept.
- **The Perform view** (the redesign's step 6, board 7; screen `pads-perform`): the project's Perform set where the kit's pads were, the Pads of row kept. The bar: the pad's action colour, "Perform pads B2 · Reverb wash" over what it does ("Throw · while held, on the master · page B of the project's Perform set"), and on the right a 220 px **On the pads: off / on** switch (a light at its left, outlined while on) that puts the set on the pads at the page shown, as SHIFT + SAMPLER pressed again on the Looper tab does (section 4a). On the left (440 px), as the kit's: the page's 16 pads with their action's name, a line ("master", "Drums", "its key", "track mute": a throw's line names where it plays) and its kind in the corner (THROW, MACRO, LOOP, LOOP MUTE, MUTE, SCENE), the pages A to H over them (a dot per pad with an action; PAD BANK picks the page too), and under them Copy, Paste, Clear and **Name page** (the keyboard, for the page's name, up to 24 letters; shown over the pads and in the log when PAD BANK turns to it). The rest of the width, beside the Q-Link column (128 px, at the right as on Play): a 52 px row of six chips, **Throw** (an effect), **Macro** (knobs held), **Loop key**, **Loop mute**, **Track mute**, **Scene** (none lit on an empty pad); two 52 px rows of four chips, the eight choices of that kind (Play's throws with their lines, the eight macros with how many knobs each moves, Loops 1 to 8, the song's tracks or the scene rows, eight round the one picked); and the **Pad** list: the pad's own settings, what it does and its choice being the chips above (no Pad does row; Clear empties a pad): a throw's **On** (Master, then the Mix tab's tracks as it names them, a track's own channel only while in use, then Sub 1 to 8), Hold (While held, Latch, 1 bar), Starts (At once, Next 1/16, Next beat) and Let go (Ring out, Cut); a macro pad's Macro and Value (0 to 100 %); a loop pad's Loop, a mute's Track or a scene pad's Scene; with what a hit does under the rows (and how the set reaches the pads live, unless a throw's four rows leave room for only the first; in the warning colour when a throw's On is a track's channel that plays nothing); and under the list a 52 px row, **Edit the effect ›** (the Mix tab's chain editor on the Perform FX of the throw's strip, at the Slots page with the throw's own settings; ‹ comes back to the view) and **Hear it** (the throw for a bar on its strip, from its Starts, as a pad set to 1 bar plays), both faint on a pad that isn't a throw, then **Learn a key** (any pad, an empty one too; the redesign's step 6g): MIDI Learn waits for a key, pad or footswitch on outside gear to play the picked pad, as SHIFT + a touch hands a value to it (section 4d), and the hint line says LEARNING with the pad's name ("Perform pads B2"); tapped again while it waits, it cancels. SHIFT + a tap on a pad of the map does the same for that pad: it picks the pad and doesn't play it, and while SHIFT is held the hint says so. A learned key plays its pad on every page as a hit on it does, whatever the MPC's pads show, and is kept in the rig as the looper's footswitch is; its messages are taken by its pad, as any learned control's are, so the key doesn't also play or record a note on the armed track; the Learn section lists it as "Perform pads B2" (no card there: it is learned from this view). A throw on a track or a submix plays on a Perform FX at the end of that strip's chain, put there when On picks it, never by a hit (said in the hint and the log; the Mix tab shows it, it passes sound unchanged while nothing throws, and it is saved with the project). When no pad's throw plays on that strip any more (On moves on, the pad is cleared or changed, UNDO), a Perform FX that picking On put there this session comes off again (said too), so stepping On through the strips leaves nothing behind; one that was on the strip already, or that the project brought, stays. A hit on a strip whose Perform FX is gone plays nothing and says so. A hardware hit picks the pad (a throw plays while held, one set to 1 bar plays its bar, and a macro pad holds its macro while held); the kit's pads don't play. **The Q-Links** (the redesign's step 6f) turn the picked throw's own settings on the Perform FX of the strip its On picks: Q1 its length and Q2 its amount, the cards named for its effect (a Delay throw's Time and Feedback; Reverb wash's Decay alone, on Q2; Stutter, Beat repeat, Tape stop and Vinyl brake's Length and Mix; Filter dip's Sweep and Depth), SHIFT turning the amount finer. The cards name the knob alone (the scope isn't drawn, as on every column); the hint says what they turn: "Q-Links: Q1 Time, Q2 Feedback: every Delay throw on Drums", since every pad throwing that effect on that strip shares them. Q3 and Q4, and all four on a pad that isn't a throw or whose strip's Perform FX is gone, are faint; a turn of a faint card says why in the hint and the log (for a gone Perform FX: "Drums has no Perform FX for the throw: pick the pad's On again"). A turn records over a move lane while the transport records, as any knob does; a run of turns of one knob, until the finger lifts, is one UNDO step, and the lift says in the log where it left the setting. Q-LINK does nothing on the view.
- **Clip windows and scenes pages** (the Arrange refactor's 5.5, plan decision 5; Jay's card of 23 Nov 2026 23:24Z, "Yes, as drawn", mpfree-claude `arrange/spec-diffs/9-scene-banks-and-pad-pages.html`): on the Perform view, a 52 px row of three chips over the kind chips, **Own pads · Clip window · Scenes**, says what the page is; Own pads gives the page its own pads back as they were. **A clip window** is 4 tracks by 4 rows of the Clips grid, laid out as the grid (the top row of pads is the window's first row). A hit taps the cell: it launches the clip on its line, stops it while it plays, calls it off while it waits, and arms an empty Loop cell; an empty cell's pad does nothing and says so (the pads never make a clip). On a clip window the kind row is its tracks (**Tracks 1–4**, **Tracks 5–8** …), the two choice rows its rows (**Rows 1–4** … ), and the list is **Clip window** (Tracks, Rows; the wheel and − / + move it a track or a row at a time). The window is the page's own, set here, so several pages can be several windows; it doesn't follow the grid's scroll. **A scenes page** is a bank's 16 rows, row 1 top left and on along each row of pads as you read; a hit launches the row, as its number on the grid does. Its chips are **Bank A** to **Bank D** and its list **Scenes** (Bank). For all 64 scenes on the pads, four pages are scenes pages, one per bank, turned with PAD BANK. On both, Copy, Paste, Clear and the throw buttons are faint, with why in the hint; Name page and Learn a key work. Each change is one UNDO step. **The pads' lights:** a clip or scene pad lights as the grid shows its cell or row: dark with no clip, dim green with a clip, green while it plays, blinking green while it waits for its line, red while it records; an armed empty Loop cell blinks red. A Scene pad on a page of its own pads lights the same way. Sample screens `pads-perform-clips`, `pads-perform-scenes`.
- **A bar over everything:** the picked pad's colour, name and a line about its sound, then on the right **Kit** and **Browse for A5** (the pad picked). **Kit** (the redesign's step 4, board 6: "Kit Boom Bap" and a chevron, 220 px) opens the list of kits over the page (section 2), as the kit chip did. **Browse for A5** is a 220 px button with a folder that opens the browser over the page, where Load reads Load on A5 (section 3, Not tabs). Without a library (no `--data`), the browser opens empty and the log says so.
- **The pad's settings** beside the pads: the picked pad's settings (sound, play mode, voices, mute group, level, pan, tune, and its Effects: section 3a) and the kit's sounds with Put on, as the Sample tab's Pads sub-page had them. Q-Links 1 to 4 turn level, pan, tune and voices. **As a panel of controls** (Jay's device notes and his pick of mockup B, 20 Nov 2026 05:32Z; mockups in /mnt/project-files/pads-knobs/): one section titled "Pad A5" with the clip on its right; on a Sample pad, Sound (‹ ›) beside Effects (a button), Mode as five buttons (One shot, Gate, Toggle, Loop, Granular), then Level, Pan, Tune and Polyphony as knobs, then Mute group (– +) beside Edit pad (a button). A Loop pad has Tempo (– +) and Mute group where Mode was and no Polyphony; an Instrument pad has Engine (‹ ›) and a Note knob; a MIDI out pad has Port (‹ ›), then Channel and Note knobs. Each knob a Q-Link turns is lit, with its name, in that Q-Link's colour (Q1 accent, Q2 green, Q3 amber, Q4 purple, as the Q-Link cards). The line saying what a hit does stays along the panel's bottom. The wheel, – / + and its push work on the panel's controls as they did on the rows. The list's last row, **Edit pad** ("layers, keys, scenes, slices ›"), opens Edit pad (below) on the pad picked; on an Instrument or MIDI out pad it is faint and says why (they have no layers).
- **Edit pad** (the one Sample page's step 6b): the page that was the Sample tab's Play sub-page, under the Pads tab, which stays lit in the top bar. Its frame is the Sample tab's (section 4i): the pad bar with the pad's colour, name and line, and in the place of the Wave | Play switch a **‹ Pads** chip that goes back to the pads; the section chips (Layers, Amp, Filter, Pitch, Mod, Movement, Pad, Slices), the open section's picture and panel with Scenes · A to B, and the Q-Link column, as built (section 4i, "Built: Play"). A pad hit picks that pad for it, as on the Sample tab. The Pads tab button, ‹ Pads, or PROG EDIT go back to the pads.
  - **Edit pad in sections:** eight chips over the page (Layers, Amp, Filter, Pitch, Mod, Movement from sampler R5 part 4, Pad, and Slices from sampler R4 part 5), each with a line of what it holds; a tap opens the section's picture and controls, and Q-Links 1 to 4 turn its four main settings. Its cards carry the section's name.
  - **Built: Edit pad** (the Sample tab's Play sub-page until the one Sample page's step 6b). The section chips stay over the page; the open section's picture spans it (the layer map on Layers, Mod and Pad; the envelope over the sound on Amp and Pitch; the filter beside its envelope on Filter; the keyboard on Pitch and Pad while the pad is a keygroup or the pads play notes), and the panel holds the section's controls in two groups (Amp: Level, with Level and Pan; Envelope, with its five knobs), with Scenes · A to B (Store A, Store B, Return to A, the crossfader) on its right, 356 px, on every section. It has no wheel's card: its panel outlines the control picked. Add layer and Remove layer are buttons in the Layers section's panel. The Pad section's first line holds Polyphony, Mute group and Slice (sampler R4: the slice a hit plays, "3 of 16", faint on a pad that isn't sliced), and its chip's line ends "· 16 slices" on a sliced pad. **The kit's Tuning** (tunings step T6, board 4 of the tuning screens, Jay's approval 23 Nov 2026 16:14Z): The kit's pads section has a third line, **Tuning** (‹ ›, full width), what the kit's keygroups, Notes and 16 Levels play in: Song's first ("Song's: Just (5-limit) on D"), then the built-in tunings and the tuning files as the kit's own (the Tunings page's choices; its own root and A4 are on that page), and at the section's foot "keys, notes and 16 Levels follow; hits don't". The pad's line ends "tuning: song's" or "tuning: own" while the pad is a keygroup or the pads play notes or 16 Levels by Tune. While it is picked the hint line is "Tuning: what keys, Notes and 16 Levels play in · pad hits keep their own pitch · - / +, or push the wheel and turn". The Slices section (R4 part 5) shows the sliced sound across the page with a numbered line at each slice's start, the slice picked shaded (a tap picks one), and its panel holds Slice (200 px: the slice picked), Sound (260 px: Pitch and Level knobs, the Reverse switch) and Shape (300 px: Filter and Fade out knobs, Plays to: Its end or Sound end); Q-Links Slice, Pitch, Level, Filter. Faint, saying how to slice the pad, on a pad that isn't sliced.
  - **The Filter section** (the filter gap plan's F5; approved by Jay 24 Nov 2026 02:27Z, "Build it as drawn", on the canvas "Pad filter screen (F5)", https://claude.ai/artifact/FMMdD19g5nayXUSfjybEJM; screens `pads-edit-filter`, `pads-edit-filter-types` and `pads-edit-filter-moves`):
    - **The pictures.** Left, the filter's graph, titled with its type ("Filter · Low-pass"); while Env depth isn't 0, a dashed curve in Q3's colour shows where the envelope's peak takes the filter, the band between them shaded, with the target in Hz ("env +2.0 oct → 4.00 kHz"). Right, the filter envelope, labelled down its right edge (70 px) with the cutoff in Hz at its peak, sustain (when it sits apart from both) and rest.
    - **A row of 36 px buttons in the graph's title line:** **On** ("● On", lit; "○ Off" bypassed: every setting kept, reaching the next hit as a type change does; the section chip's line reads "Bypassed · LP 24"), **Compare** ("Comparing", lit: plays the filter as it was when the section opened, the changes drawn dashed, until tapped again, an edit is made, or the section is left, which keep the changes), **Copy** and **Paste** (the whole filter: type, slope, knobs, bypass, envelope and its moves; Paste onto the pad picked, one UNDO), **Reset** (the filter as a new pad has it, Off, one UNDO). Faint ones say why on a tap. The hint line says what each did.
    - **The Filter panel** (440 px of the band, the envelope's 300): **Type** (‹ ›: Off, Low-pass, High-pass, Band-pass, Notch, Peak, Ladder, Vowel, Comb; from Off, › is Low-pass at the pad's slope, 12 dB on a new pad) beside **Slope** (140 px, ‹ ›: the type's slopes in dB: low-pass and high-pass 6 to 48, band-pass 12 to 48, notch 12 and 24; faint "24 dB, fixed" on the Ladder, "12 dB, fixed" on the Peak, "–" on the Vowel and Comb) and **Moves** (100 px, a button: "3 moves ›", how many are set). A type change keeps the slope where the new type has it, else the nearest. Under them Cutoff and Resonance, then the type's own knobs (today only the Ladder's Drive, shown only on the Ladder), then Env depth. Knobs take the type's names (Comb: Pitch and Feedback; Vowel: Vowel and Sharpness), and the Peak shows its boost under Resonance ("+14.0 dB"). Off keeps the knobs, faint. The envelope's knobs have their names on two lines.
    - **The types:** a tap on the Type chip's name opens them over the pictures (the panel stays): "Pick a filter" with Done; nine tiles, each its curve, its name and a line of what it's for; a tap puts it on the pad at once while the pads keep playing; the slopes as six chips under them, those the type lacks faint (a tap says why). Done or the Filter chip closes it. The wheel steps the types.
    - **What moves the filter** (the panel's Moves button): it takes the pictures' and panels' place under the section chips, with Done. Left, the cutoff's moves: Envelope (Env depth), Velocity (Vel → cutoff), Key (Key track), Random, and the LFO and Pressure as the Movement and Mod sections set them, with a jump there (faint when they move something else); each a knob in its Q-Link's colour and a bar on one ±4 octave scale around the cutoff, showing how far it can take it. Right, Resonance's Random, and the envelope's Vel → depth and Vel → attack. At the foot, a line on where the hardest and softest hits go. Q-Links turn Key track, Vel → cutoff, Random and Env depth; the wheel turns the row picked. The Mod section keeps its Vel → cutoff row, now -100 to +100 %.
    - **Q-Links:** Cutoff, Resonance, Env depth, then the type's first own knob (Drive on the Ladder), else Env decay.
    - **The section chip's line** gives the slope in dB ("LP 24 dB · 1.00 kHz", was "LP4 · 1.00 kHz"), "Bypassed · LP 24", or "Comparing".
    - **Built otherwise than drawn:** no long press to open the moves (a held control already takes the wheel, section 6's hold and turn), so only the Moves button opens them; no read-only line of macros, locks and learned controls on the cutoff (the page doesn't know them yet); the Moves count is a button beside Slope, not in the panel's heading, which can't be tapped; the bypass reaches the next hit (no fade needed: a playing note keeps its filter); picking another pad while comparing leaves that pad as it was (UNDO brings the changes back); - / + don't step the slope over the types.
- **The pad's type** (the redesign's step 6b, board 6; the owner's card "Yes, per-pad port (board 6)", 18 Nov 09:26Z): a 64 px row of four chips over the settings, each a name over a short line: **Sample** (its sound), **Loop** (in time), **Instrument** (a synth voice), **MIDI out** (to your gear); the pad's type lit. A tap makes the picked pad that type (one UNDO); the hint line says what it did or why not ("A11 has no sound to loop: Browse for one"). The settings follow the type: a loop's sound, Tempo (its BPM and bars), mute group, level, pan, tune and Effects, Q-Links level, pan, tune and tempo; an instrument's Engine and Note, Q-Links note and engine; MIDI out's Port, Channel and Note, Q-Links note, channel and port. Under the rows a line says what a hit does ("A hit sends note 49 on channel 10 to Volca Kick"; an instrument's "A hit plays the Synth tab's Poly sound at C4, through this pad's channel on Mix"), in the warning colour when it would do nothing and why ("sends nothing: Volca Kick is not plugged in"), and for an instrument past the 16 that have a synth of their own, which plays through the Synth channel ("... through the Synth channel: no synth of its own (16 pads at most have one)"; the owner's card "Own pad channel, Synth tab's sound", 18 Nov 20:15Z). The panel keeps its title, "Pad A13". While a Loop pad's hit waits for the launch line (the transport playing, Launch not at once; sampler R5.3b), its pad blinks as a cued clip's cell does, on the MPC and on screen, until it sounds on the line.
- **The Sample tab** loses its Pads sub-tab and gains two small buttons beside its bar on every page (330 px, mockup sample-send-to-pad), **Send to pad** (the pad picked, sound and edits, onto the next pad hit; it reads "Hit a pad" while it waits, a second press calls it off) and **Chop to pads** (picks the Wave page's Slice tool; with Slice picked it is the tool's own Chop to pads). Both are faint while the pad picked has no sound.
- **PROG EDIT** opens the Pads tab (the Synth tab while the synth has the pads). On the Pads tab it opens Edit pad on the pad picked, and on Edit pad it goes back to the pads, as the MPC's PROG EDIT opens its Track Edit.
- While the synth has the pads, the bar's heading carries the Chromatic | In key switch, as the Synth tab does.

## 4f. Up to 128 tracks

**Jay picked "Up to 128, like Akai's MPC"** on 14 Nov 2026 (01:27Z card, over 16 or 32), asked for mockups first (02:25Z), and **approved the screens at 04:24Z** as drawn in https://claude.ai/artifact/NKJM7t9kNQm7jZc2NdHcvg (PNGs in /mnt/project-files/tracks-128/; screens `clips-tracks`, `clips-tracks-end` and `arrange-song-tracks` in `mpfree --preview`). New projects still start with 8 tracks. Removing a track comes later.

- **The Clips grid** stays 8 tracks across.
  - **+ Track** is a button on the title line, left of Edit. It adds "Track N", armed, with its page in view; UNDO takes it away. It is dim at 128. (The proposal had a column after the last track, but a full page of 8 has no free column.)
  - **The pager:** with more than 8 tracks, "‹ 9–16 of 24 ›" takes the colour legend's place. The arrows, or a swipe of 48 px or more along the track names, show the 8 tracks before or after.
  - **A track's name arms it on the lift**, so a swipe along the names pages without arming a track.
  - **The grid follows the armed track:** arming a track off the page (from the Armed row, for one) brings its page.
  - With 8 tracks or fewer there is no pager, and "+ Track" leaves the legend room for "red: recording" only.
  - **Audio tracks** (section 4c): columns in track order, with a wave mark and the input under the name. The name arms the track, as on any track.
  - **Audio cells** hold audio clips (the rethink's phase 2, approved 22 Nov 23:20Z as drawn on board A, section 4c). A cell shows the clip's name and a small wave across its length (24 columns, drawn as a Loop cell's).
    - Playing, the wave lights up to the playhead, with a line there.
    - A once clip's line follows its audio. It is drawn dimmed once the track has stopped and the sound rings on, so the grid never shows silence while the track sounds.
    - Queued, the cell is outlined amber, as any clip is.
  - **What the cell says.** In Make the word goes in the wave's place while the clip is stopped or queued. In Perform it goes in the foot under the wave.
    - Its length: "2 bars". A once clip says "once", or in Perform its time, "3:17 once".
    - "from 17.1" while queued.
    - When it matters: "made at 120" (amber), made at another tempo than the project's; "waits 18.1" (amber, streaming on), a launch moved on to the line where its audio is ready; "not ready" (red), cancelled after 2 lines; "missing" (red), its recording's file wasn't found, and a tap is refused.
    - A tap on a cell with a word says it in full on the hint line: "Riff was made at 120 BPM: at 92 it leaves 1.2 s of silence each pass. Fit to tempo on the Clip tab fixes it." · "Outro waits for its audio: it starts on 18.1." · "Amen's audio wasn't ready within 2 bars. Tap to try again." · "Solo's audio is missing: take 4's file wasn't found when the project opened."
  - **An empty audio cell** stays dashed. A tap on it says on the hint line: "An empty audio cell: put audio in with To a cell on the Song view, or Put in a cell on the Sample page."
  - **Moving and copying** work as for any cell. Drag a cell to another row or another audio track. SHIFT as it lifts makes a copy, which names the same recordings. A drop between an audio track and a note or Loop track is refused, and the hint line says why ("Drums plays notes: an audio clip goes on an audio track").
  - Scenes, row launchers, Stop, Stop all and follow actions work on audio cells as on any cell.
  - **Loop tracks** (Jay's sequencer decision 3, 16 Nov; [loop-tracks.md](loop-tracks.md)): columns after the tracks, named Loops A, Loops B and on, a wave mark before their input under the name ("Pads"). A loop cell shows its name, its loop's wave in the detail's place and its length under it; playing, the wave lights up to the playhead in place of the ring. With Loop tracks the title is the loops' gauge, "Loops 4 of 8 · memory 38 %", since the pager takes the legend's place past 8 tracks. **SHIFT + "+ Track"** adds an empty Loop track at the end, and a tap on a Loop track's sound line steps what it records (Pads, Line in, Resample). **A tap on an empty Loop cell arms it** for the next loop recorded (red edge and dot; "Armed", then "Ready" while the first pass waits and "REC" while it records), the REC chip naming it ("REC → Loops C · Drop"); a second tap lets go. A new project still starts with no Loop track. **In the Song view** a Loop track has a lane like any track, its input under its name; song mode plays its loops' blocks, each loop in and out on its block's start and end, and a loop's block stays on its own lane (a drag to another track says why). **SHIFT as a loop's dragged cell lifts** copies it: into another row of its own Loop track it is the same loop (a scene change between the rows keeps it playing), and onto an empty cell of another Loop track it is a new loop, a copy of its layers, its foot saying "copying in" until they are all in, silent until it is launched (sequencer decision 3, part 6).
  - **Loops in audio cells** (the audio tracks rethink's phase 4, board 2; the phase 4 screens approved as drawn by Jay, 23 Nov 2026 19:24Z, https://claude.ai/artifact/KJDBaGjVb6orw27Zsvb1ff; from 4b behind Dev's "Loops on audio tracks", for everyone at 4e when Loop tracks go); at 4e it replaces the Loop tracks bullet above): an audio column's cell holds a take clip (phase 2) or a loop. A loop cell shows ⟳ and its name, its length under it, and the deck's ring at its right in the track's colour, with no wave (a take clip keeps its wave); queued, it is outlined amber with "from 17.1" in its length's place; a layer still being written shows an amber dot and the word "writing". A tap on an empty cell of the armed audio track arms it for a loop (red edge, "● REC here", "a loop" under it). The title adds the loops' gauge, "Loops 6 of 8", while any cell holds a loop, and the Record panel says "Armed: Vocal · row 3" and "Makes: a loop". A drag between an audio column and a note column is refused as now; SHIFT as a loop cell lifts copies it: into another row of its track it is the same loop, onto another audio track's empty cell a new loop, a copy of its layers ("copying in" until they are all in).
- **The Song view:** "+ Track" is the lane after the last lane, opening the chooser of section 4c (there is no "+ Audio"). The lanes scroll as before, by a drag on the names.
- **The tempo lane** (sequencer roadmap S6, tempo ramps): a 24 px lane under the Song view's ruler, above the track lanes, "Tempo" in the name column. It draws the song's tempo across the bars: flat from each tempo point, sloping to the next where a point ramps, the project's tempo before the first point, with a dot on each point and its tempo beside it. A tap on the lane adds a point on the nearest bar line with the tempo there; a tap on a point picks it (ringed), and the bottom row's last two buttons become **Ramp** (lit while it glides to the next point) and **Delete**; the wheel sets its tempo; a point drags along the lane to a bar line. In song mode, turning the tempo sets the point in effect at the playhead, or adds one at that bar (Jay's card of 18 Nov 10:17Z). Built so far: the lane, its taps and drags (`mpfree-time-ui`); song mode playing the points, the wheel, Ramp and Delete come with the app's side. Details for Jay to confirm: the lane's height and place, points on bar lines only, and the tempo shown only on the points.
- **TRACK MUTE:** all 16 pads are tracks, and the PAD BANK buttons pick which 16, as on Akai's MPC: A is tracks 1-16, B 17-32, up to H, 113-128, with SHIFT or a second press for E-H as for a kit's banks (section 6). Audio tracks are on the pads like any track, in track order. The kit's bank on the pads stays, and TRACK MUTE's bank is kept for the next TRACK MUTE.
- **Mutes on the grid** (sequencer decision 5): a muted track's column is drawn muted, and so is a track silent while others are soloed; a soloed track's header is outlined in the accent colour. A track with a mute or solo waiting in the mute queue (section 6, TRACK MUTE) has its header hatched and outlined in amber, its second line saying what lands and how soon ("mutes in 2 beats", "plays in 1 bar", "solo in 3 beats"; where that doesn't fit the column, "mutes 2 beats", then just "mutes"). Sample screens `clips-mutes` and `clips-solo`. **The transport bar** then starts with a WAITING reading in amber, naming the tracks and where they land ("WAITING Hats, Vox at 18.1"; past two tracks, "Hats, Vox +2 at 18.1"), ahead of the tempo.
- **Make's rows and Perform's** (sequencer decision 5, the design's "8 rows with names and lengths" and "5 big rows with rings and countdowns", mockup C): in Make the grid shows 8 compact rows (44 px at least), each cell its name and under it its length, or "from 7.1" in amber while queued, or a loop's wave; a playing clip has a small ring at its right. In Perform it shows 5 big rows, as before, and a queued cell also counts down: an amber bar along its foot fills over the launch line's span before it starts (a bar, with Launch at 1 bar). The rows in view follow the switch. Sample screens `clips` (Make) and `clips-perform`.
- **Back to song** (sequencer S4, the review's "Song mode and live launches", mockup C): while song mode is on and a clip is launched over the song or waits to be, a "Back to song" button (124 px, tinted green) sits on the grid's title line, left of the pager or "+ Track"; the title gives up what follows its " · " where it would touch it. A tap stops every clip on the next launch line, and the song plays every track from there (at once while stopped; with song mode off it turns it on). Sample screen `clips-perform`.
- **Capture scene in Perform** (the Arrange refactor's 5.3, its second place; Jay's card of 23 Nov 2026 19:02Z, "Yes, as drawn", mpfree-claude `arrange/spec-diffs/10-capture-scene-in-perform.html`): in Perform, Capture scene (128 px) sits on the grid's title line left of Back to song, the pager or "+ Track"; Show, while a set is open, left of it. It is faint while nothing plays (no clip launched, the song not playing), and a tap then does nothing. It is left out where it and Show would not clear the view chip (more than 8 tracks with Back to song, or with Show): then the scene card's Capture is the way. The title's words give way first, then the bank chip, as they do for Back to song. A tap keeps the clips playing as copies in the first empty row under the scene playing, as the scene card's Capture does, and one UNDO takes it back. SHIFT + a tap learns it, lit or faint. Make's grid doesn't have it. Sample screens `clips-perform`, `clips-perform-capture`.
- **NEXT** (Perform, while the transport rolls): the transport bar says what comes next and how soon, in the accent colour, after WAITING: a scene launched ("NEXT Drop in 2 beats"), else the clips cued ("Drop bass in 1 bar", "Bass stops in 3 beats", "Drop bass +2 in 1 bar"), else a reload waiting ("Verse B reloads in 2 beats"), else the song list's next step ("Verse in 2 bars", "Stop all in 1 bar"; section 4k), else the scene playing's follow action ("Verse in 3 bars", "Verse again", "Any scene", "Drop or stay" with a chance), else in song mode the next marker ("Chorus in 4 bars"). Counts round up: beats under a bar, then bars. The bar's first reading is cut to fit rather than left out.
- **The view chip** (the Arrange refactor's phase 5; approved as drawn 23 Nov 2026 11:43Z, https://claude.ai/artifact/LmzxQ7tBW33RRgBxUpak93): a 174 px chip of three 58 px parts, **Clips | List | Song**, at the left of the title line of the grid, the Song view and the song list, the view shown lit. A tap on another part shows it. It takes the place of the view's name in the title, the grid's "Song ›", the Song view's List (title line) and Clips (bottom row), and the song list's Clips and Song. The grid's title reads "Slots 1–5 of 16"; the Song view's title line has only its legend ("20 bars · from bar 1"). MAIN in Perform still goes Clips, Song, List and round. Sample screens `clips`, `arrange-song`, `arrange-list`.
- **Scene banks A to D** (the Arrange refactor's 5.6; Jay's card of 23 Nov 2026 23:24Z, "Yes, as drawn", mpfree-claude `arrange/spec-diffs/9-scene-banks-and-pad-pages.html`): the grid keeps 64 rows, four banks of 16, A to D, and scrolls through all of them as one list (SHIFT + wheel, as before). The **bank chip** sits right of the view chip on the grid's title line: four 28 px parts, **A B C D**. The bank in the middle of the view is lit (and faint as a target, since a tap there changes nothing); a bank with a clip or a named scene is bright, an empty one dim. A tap on another bank scrolls the grid to its first row. Each bank's first row has a line across the grid above it and the bank's letter by the row's number. The grid's title says which rows show ("Rows 13–20"). Follow actions stay in their bank (Next from row 32 goes round to row 17). With more than eight tracks and Back to song showing there is no room on the title line, so the chip steps aside (SHIFT + wheel still scrolls). While a clip window is on the pads (section 4e), the grid outlines its 16 cells in the accent colour; the outline goes when PAD BANK turns to another page. Sample screens `clips-bank-b`, `clips-pad-window`.
- **What each track plays** (D3; phase 5.1, approved with the view chip): in song mode, a track the song has blocks for has a **SONG** tag at the right of its header's second line (of its name line when it has no second line, or while a mute waits there). It is filled green while the track plays the song, and outlined green while a clip launched on it plays over the song. A muted track keeps its tag, dimmed. There is no tag with song mode off, or on a track the song has nothing for. A clip waiting to start leaves the tag filled: the song plays on that track until the clip's line. Sample screen `clips-perform-song`.
- **Next on a playing cell** (phase 5.1): a stop in song mode gives the track the song again, so its cell says "to song 6.1" (not "stops 6.1"); with song mode off it still says "stops 6.1". Any change waiting on a playing cell ("until 6.1", "stops 6.1", "to song 6.1") is in amber. In Perform a stop or Back to song also counts down on the playing cell: the amber bar along its foot, left of the ring.
- **Empty cells** (decision 4; phase 5.4, approved with the view chip): an empty cell acts on the lift, so a drag or a scroll that starts on one does nothing. In Make one tap makes a clip there. In Perform a tap makes nothing, and the hint line says "Perform: hold an empty cell to make a clip there · a tap makes nothing, so a stray finger can't stop what plays"; held half a second, with Perform's red fill crossing it (section 2's guard), it makes the clip. An empty Loop cell stays one tap, since it only arms the next loop.
- **Clips over the song** on the Song view (D3; phase 5.1, approved with the view chip): in song mode a clip launched on a track is a band over its lane, in the track's colour with a green edge and its name, from where it started to the view's right edge, with the song's blocks hatched under it (they are silent until the track goes back). While a stop or Back to song waits the band ends on that line, and an amber dashed line across the lane marks where the song comes back. A clip waiting to start is a dashed amber outline from its line. Sample screen `arrange-song-over`.
- **The hint line under the grid** (the scene card, 5.2): "Tap a clip: it plays or stops on the next Launch line · drag it: move it (SHIFT as you lift: copy) · a row's number plays it, held: its card · + records · a track's name arms it".
- **The block tools** (the Arrange refactor's phase 6; Jay's yes on its spec diff, "Yes, as drawn", 23 Nov 2026 16:06Z, https://claude.ai/artifact/BAsR9AhHkmt1cWZdPRuBph): the Song view's bottom row is **Split** (76 px, its first place), Song on (104), Scenes → song and Song → scenes (128 each), **Duplicate** (104), Make unique (112) and Delete (80). Split cuts the picked block at the playhead and picks the part after; with none picked, every block across the playhead. Duplicate puts a linked copy right after the picked block and picks it; faint with none picked, and not shown while a section or tempo point is picked. The picked block has a **start grip** (two short bars inside its left end) that trims it from the left to the bar, what it plays staying in place, never past the block before it, its last bar kept, with a "from bar N" tag. The picked block held still 0.4 s and then dragged **slides** what it plays under it, to the 1/16, outlined in amber with a "slide +1 beat" tag. Sample screens `arrange-song-block`, `arrange-song-trim`.
