- **The zoom chip** (the Arrange refactor's 6.4; Jay's yes on its spec diff, "Yes, as drawn", 23 Nov 2026 16:05Z, https://claude.ai/artifact/N2JtC9UNpZ5RFBw891qRYn): right of the view chip on the Song view's title line, a 244 px chip: "Bars", then 4 · 8 · 16 · 32 · 64, 40 px each, the bars shown lit. A tap shows that many bars across the lanes from the same first bar. At 4 and 8 bars a faint line marks each beat and drags (a block, its end, its start) snap to the beat, the tag saying "bar 5 beat 3"; at 16 and more they snap to the bar. The ruler numbers every bar up to 16, every 4th at 32 and every 8th at 64, and a marker's name is cut short before the next marker. The wheel and a block held at the edge scroll a sixteenth of what shows (a bar at least). The zoom lasts while the app runs, whichever view or page shows; a new session starts at 16. The legend ("32 bars · from bar 1") stays right-aligned beside Rec song. Sample screens `arrange-song-zoom-4`, `arrange-song-zoom-64`.

## 4g. The Synth tab

**Picked by Jay on 14 Nov 2026** (13:25Z, the instruments thread's card, "Build it as drawn"; his ask at 12:40Z: the pages looked like "a wall of knobs", and for consistency the list on the left and the Q-Links on the right). Mockups: the "Synth page layouts" canvas (https://claude.ai/artifact/MSNu169wNrkV8Ag9mSRnD4); rules: the doc "Instrument and effect page design rules" (https://claude.ai/artifact/ABwAw3rxAj5Rgi8P1MdL4Q). Built by the instruments thread (`mpfree-synth-ui`, `view::LAYOUT`).

- **The frame,** as the Looper tab's: at the left (220 px), the instrument card (72 px: swatch, engine, the track and Native or Plugin), then the pads heading with the Chromatic | In key switch while the synth has the pads, then the presets; in the middle, the title bar (56 px: the preset and the pages as tabs) over the page; at the right, the shared Q-Link column (128 px); the hint line under them. The bottom Q-Link strip and the strip of pictures are gone; the page gets their room (884 x 598 px).
- **The page** is labelled sections, each with one job, coloured by it (sources cyan, filter and tone coral, envelopes and dynamics lime, movement pink, voice and output grey) and laid out per page. Pictures sit inside the section they draw. Knobs are sized by importance; choices are buttons or a chip with ‹ ›, whole numbers steppers, toggles pills, envelopes and mixes faders. "Open ›" by a section's name goes to its page. The inside of the page is the Synth tab's own (section 7).
- **The Q-Link column** names what Q-Links 1 to 4 turn on the page, as their sections name them (the section is the card's scope, not drawn since the column narrowed); the controls carry Q1 to Q4 badges in the knobs' colours. A drag on a card turns it.
- **Hardware** as before: the wheel picks and turns the controls (section 6), SHIFT + wheel steps the pages, Q-Links 1 to 4 turn the badged controls.
- **The gear** (Jay's device notes, 20 Nov 2026: the Synth options on screen, not only on MENU; mockups: the "Synth page: pick and play" canvas, https://claude.ai/artifact/WxERuieihAA7FsGwGuS8UA; *proposed, waits for Jay's approval with the pull request*): a 44 px raised button at the right end of the title bar, after the page tabs, opens the Synth options as MENU does.
- **Play on the pads** (Jay's device notes, 20 Nov 2026: hear a synth on the pads in one tap; Jay's pick of 20 Nov, "Gear and Back now, then redesign", step 1 of the doc "Instruments, pads and tracks: the redesign", https://claude.ai/artifact/A2iE1bDqPxk2uSJABCgTJg; *proposed, waits for Jay's approval with the pull request*): while the selected track's instrument is a kit, the pads heading under the instrument card (48 px tall, a fingertip's, rather than 30) is a button: the accent with a play triangle, "Play Poly on the pads" over "Track 1 keeps its kit". A tap chooses the synth the tab shows for the pads, on its own Synth channel, without changing the track; the button then reads "Follow track" over "The pads play Poly on every track", raised with the accent's outline and a stop square (*step 3 of the redesign, proposed with its pull request*: "Back to Track 1's kit" over "The pads play Poly now" in step 1). The synth stays on the pads whichever track is selected or armed, through REC's list and REC, and the header names it ("Pads: Poly", section 2); the button again, the chip, or a kit picked give the pads back to the track (Follow track). REC records the synth on a free track (one nobody set up; a new one when none is), never a pinned track that isn't free (the redesign's rule 3). While the synth is the track's own, the heading is the Chromatic | In key switch as before.
- **The list of synths** (Jay's device notes, 20 Nov 2026: "assign a synth ... in just 1 or 2 actions, or ... preview it on the pads"; mockups: the "Synth page: pick and play" canvas, https://claude.ai/artifact/WxERuieihAA7FsGwGuS8UA; step 6 of the doc "Instruments, pads and tracks: the redesign", https://claude.ai/artifact/A2iE1bDqPxk2uSJABCgTJg; *proposed, waits for Jay's approval with the pull request*): a tap on the instrument card opens it over the page (the middle, under the title bar): a 64 px heading, "Pick what the pads play" (sampler step 15e; "Pick a synth for the pads" before) over "A tap puts it on the pads at once: play them to hear it", with Cancel (120 px) and Keep (150 px, the accent: "Keep FM", or "Done" with nothing changed) at its right; under it a tile for each choice, four a row, 104 px tall (shorter when many plugin synths make more rows): the selected track's instrument first ("Track 1's", the pads follow the track again), then the twelve built-in synths, each with what it is good for, then the plugin synths. The one on the pads is outlined in the accent and tagged "ON THE PADS". A tap plays the synth on the pads at once, from a place no track has, on its own mixer strip ("FM · Pads"); no track changes. The card reads "Close the synths" while it is open; a second tap on it, Keep, or another page keeps the synth on the pads; Cancel gives them back what they had. While the pads play a synth picked there, the header names it ("Pads: FM", section 2) and the heading under the card is Follow track, as for Play on the pads below. On a kit's pages a 48 px row under the heading holds two chips, **All banks** (150 px) and **Bank B only** (170 px, the bank the pads are on), the one picked in the accent; the tiles start under it (step 6c, *proposed with the recommended answer to Jay's card on where a bank is given its instrument*). While the pads play a bank's own the header's chip reads "Pads B: FM". **Layer** (step 11 of the redesign, the plan's "Step 11", Joshi's "Build all three" of 22 Nov 2026; approved by Jay, 22 Nov 2026 22:40Z: "OK all four as written" on step 11's layout card): a 110 px switch in the heading before Cancel, raised and outlined when off, the accent when on. On, the heading's second line reads "A tap adds it to the pads or takes it off, up to 4", a tap adds the synth to what the pads play or takes it off (up to four), each one playing is outlined and tagged "ON THE PADS", and Keep reads "Keep layers"; Layer and Bank B only turn each other off. While the pads play layers the header's chip reads "Pads: FM + Pluck", and so do Play's transport line and the Follow track heading.
- **The list's Kits side** (sampler step 15e; the canvas "Pads and kits: step 15e", https://claude.ai/artifact/VDrYTMxR5ACoeJpUjUYZbH, boards 1 and 2; approved by Jay 23 Nov 2026 16:11Z, "Build as drawn, with your picks"): the row under the heading always shows, with **Synths** and **Kits** (110 px each) at its start and All banks and Bank B only at its end on a kit's pages; the list opens on Kits while a kit is what the bank plays. The Kits side has a tile for each kit, four a row, 84 px tall: first **Track 1's kit** ("The pads follow the track: Boom Bap"; **Follow track** while the track plays a synth), or with Bank B only **Follow base** (the bank plays what every bank plays); then each kit by place, "2 · Boom Bap" in the kit's colour over "16 pads · Track 1" ("48 pads in A to C, keygroups · no track"); the empty kits last, thin and faint, "9 · Empty" over "Start a kit here". A tap puts the kit on the pads at once, every bank or bank B alone, with no track changed; Keep reads "Keep Boom Bap", and the header's chip "Pads: Boom Bap". An empty kit goes on the pads too, playing nothing, and the hint says "Kit 9 is empty: load sounds on the Sample page" (question 3). With Layer on a tap adds the kit as a layer or takes it off. Between the row and the tiles, a 46 px row for the outlined kit: with Bank B only and more than one bank of the kit filled, "Bank B plays Big Kit's bank" and a 44 px letter for each filled bank, the one played picked (PAD BANK still picks the bank you play); for a kit that holds keygroups (question 2), "Big Kit's keys play" with **Pads**, **Keygroups** and **Both**, and a line on what the one picked does.
- **Presets** (Jay, 18 Nov 2026 20:47Z, pick A on the synth library thread's card "Which way should the Synth tab open presets and save?"; mockups: the "Synth presets: open and save" canvas, https://claude.ai/artifact/E8q1AymcsUQ7kNgmTapBPC). On MPFree's own engines the preset's name in the title bar is a button (raised, with ▾). A tap opens the browser over the page as the engine's **preset picker**: Factory (the engine's own presets, as the side list has them), Yours (the musician's, `Instruments/<engine>/` in the library), Recent and Favourites. The wheel tries each preset as it moves (the pads play it). Push or Load keeps it. Cancel or BROWSE puts back the sound from before the first try, edits and all, and goes back to the Synth tab. - and + step through the places. **Save preset** is the first button over the list. The browser's keyboard, starting on the preset's name, saves the sound in Yours; a name already there asks before it is replaced (the old one goes to the Trash), and factory presets are never changed. The title then names the saved preset, and Cancel keeps it (a save ends the try). A tap on the side list still loads a preset at once, and "edited" under the name says there is something to save. A plugin's name doesn't open the picker (its presets are in the side list). *Proposed (instrument redesign step 4c, 20 Nov 2026, for Jay to approve):* the preset picked has **New track** beside Load: the sound tried goes back, and a new track with a synth of its own playing that preset is added and selected.
- **An MPC plugin's skin** (Jay, 15 Nov 22:22Z, card on the Open MPC plugins thread: "Full width at MPC size"). When the armed track plays an MPC plugin that has a skin, the Synth tab shows the skin as its makers drew it: full width right under the top bar, at 1280 x 628, pixel for pixel as on an MPC (the `Synth skin` page, `mpfree-app/src/skin_page.rs`). Under it, the skin's tabs as the MPC lays them out, one button per function key (a tap steps through that key's sub-tabs), and "MPFree page" at the right; the hint line under them. The side list and the Q-Link column step aside while it shows; Q-Links 1 to 4 turn the knobs the skin's own Q-Links file gives the tab (a tap on a control picks its column, as on the MPC), and the wheel picks and turns the skin's controls (section 6). "MPFree page" shows the frame above for the plugin, with a "Plugin skin" button (52 px) under the instrument card to go back. A plugin with no skin, or a skin that doesn't read, gets the frame above, as the MPC shows its default pages.

## 4h. Drag and drop

**Jay asked** on 14 Nov 2026 at 12:03Z for dragging effects up and down the chain besides Up and Down, and for drag and drop on the Clips grid, the Song view and the Looper's layers. He picked mockups first (12:12Z) and **approved them as drawn at 12:41Z** (https://claude.ai/artifact/Qj2hoe5JPWGFxgmPShJn4Z).

- **On every screen:** a finger that moves more than a fingertip (12 px) picks a thing up; there is no hold. On a thing that can be dragged, a tap acts on the lift. Its own place stays outlined, and where it would land is outlined (or a line between rows). Lifted where it started, or cancelled, nothing changes. SHIFT held as the finger lifts drops a copy. UNDO takes back any drop. Dragged to an edge, the view pages or scrolls. The wheel: a push grabs, a turn moves, a push drops.
- **The chain editor:** section 4b. Built.
- **The Clips grid:** a clip dropped in an empty slot moves there; onto another clip the two change places (a copy goes only into an empty slot); in another track's column it plays that track's sound, and the song's blocks that use it go with it. A row's number dragged moves the whole row to the line, the rows below shifting down, so nothing is written over; with SHIFT a copy goes in. Held still 0.4 s instead, it opens the row's scene card (section 4l). At the grid's edge the tracks page and the rows scroll. Built.
- **The Song view:** a block slides along its lane snapped to the bar, or up and down to another track's lane; the wheel moves the picked block a beat a detent; a picked block's end grip makes it longer (the clip repeats) or shorter. A section's name on the ruler drags the whole section, on every track, and the sections after shift so nothing is written over; with SHIFT a linked copy. Audio regions drag as they do already. Built, with these details for Jay to confirm: a block put on another track's lane takes its own copy of the clip (a linked block would name that track's slot); a block across a section's edge is cut there when a section moves, and joined again where its parts meet in step; a moved section takes its tempo changes and morph moves with it (Arrange 3.2); the Arrange page has no outline, so the wheel's push grabs the picked block, or a section picked by a tap on its name, in place of opening the record row; Delete and Make unique are undoable edits too; the hint line follows the pick. The picked block's start grip trims it from the left; held still first, a drag on the picked block slides what it plays instead of moving it (phase 6, section 4f). **Several blocks** (the Arrange refactor's 6.5, approved with the zoom chip): SHIFT + a tap on a block adds it to the pick, or takes it out; a drag across empty space on the lanes draws a box, and lifting picks every block it touches (with SHIFT: adds them); a tap on empty space picks nothing, on the lift. Each picked block has the pick's outline and no grips. A drag of any picked block moves them all, keeping their places to each other, each landing outlined, red where it would hit a block not picked or start before bar 1; SHIFT as it lifts copies them; all go or none, and a refusal says why. Split cuts the picked blocks at the playhead and picks the parts after; Duplicate puts copies of them all right after the last one ends and picks the copies; Delete deletes them all (Perform's hold applies); Make unique is faint (one block at a time). The wheel's push grabs them and a turn moves them a beat. The hint line: "Drag one: they all move (SHIFT as you lift: copies) · SHIFT + tap: add or take out a block · Split, Duplicate, Delete: all of them · wheel: a beat". With nothing picked it reads "Tap a block to pick it (SHIFT + tap: several) · drag across empty space: a box picks blocks · drag a block or a section's name: move it · tap the ruler: a marker". Each edit is one UNDO. Sample screens `arrange-song-picks`, `arrange-song-picks-drag`, `arrange-song-box`.
- **Markers and bars** (Arrange 3.3; Jay, 22 Nov 2026 at 06:55Z, "as drawn", https://claude.ai/artifact/8jRe7EaLAEMRVwS7MMs1TS): a tap on the ruler where no name is adds a marker on the nearest bar line, named with the next free letter, and picks it (a bar with a marker says so). A section picked by a tap on its name shows its name on the amber pill and a grip (two short bars, 28 px wide over the ruler's height) just left of its marker; the bottom row's last four buttons read + Bar (an empty bar before the section, everything from its marker on a bar later; presses in a row one undo), Rename (the text keyboard, "Name marker"), Remove marker (its bars join the section before) and Delete bars (the section's bars out, everything after closed up; Perform's hold). The grip drags along the ruler snapped to the bar with a "Verse from bar 6" tag and moves only the marker; it can't land on another. The hint line follows the pick. Each is an edit UNDO takes back, said in place of the hint. Built.
- **Pass lines** (the Arrange refactor's phase 4b, 4b.4, built; approved with the linked list's screens, 23 Nov 2026 01:10Z): a part of N passes has its name on the ruler ending "×N" ("Verse ×2"). A dashed line in a faint warning colour runs across the lanes at the start of each pass after the first, labelled "pass 2 of 2" on the ruler. The ruler's names are the linked list's parts (section 4k). Edit inside one pass and the part becomes the song's own: its passes then become plain bars, and the lines go.
- **The song list** (the Arrange refactor's phase 4b): a step dragged by its grip lands in the gap the line shows; on a list linked to the song everything under the part moves with it (section 4k).
- **The Looper:** a layer lifts out of the editor after a press and hold: still for 0.4 s (the row outlined in the accent), then moved 12 px; a finger that moves sooner swipes the rows instead (Jay, 17 Nov, after a 12 px move lifting the row kept swipes from scrolling). A layer dragged onto another loop moves there (with SHIFT a copy); onto an empty loop it becomes a loop of its own (since the Looper tab's deck, 17 Nov: the drop spots are the deck's loop channels, empty ones included, in place of the loop list and + New loop); onto To a pad just that layer goes (To a clip was drawn here too; Show in Clips took its place). A loop of another length refuses it and says why. Built, with these details for Jay to confirm: To a pad and To a clip are left out for now, since the loop's own bounce to a pad or a clip isn't in the app yet (their rows are faint); the loops that can take the layer are lit, empty ones too; the loop that took the layer is picked afterwards, so the editor shows where it went; a move from a loop's only layer leaves it there, so it acts as a copy (the looper keeps a loop's last layer); a busy or recording loop, or a full one, says why as a loop of another length does; the layer fades in, in time with the song, as soon as it is copied (a 64th of the loop's length later: about 60 ms for 2 bars at 120 BPM), and UNDO in either loop takes a move back in both; the hint line adds "swipe: scroll" and "hold a layer, drag: to a loop (SHIFT: copy)", and its first item is cut to "Tap: pick" so STOP's still fits.
- **Left out:** moving tracks (every per-track table would shift) and reordering layers within a loop.

## 4i. The Sample tab's frame

The sampler review's decision 5 (Jay, 16 Nov: the Sample tab in the shared frame, https://claude.ai/artifact/Jx5jTNTJxN6iQCv8jmjdaj): its Q-Links are where they are on every other page.
- **The frame:** on the page and on Auto-sample over it, the pad bar and the Send to pad, Chop to pads and Stems buttons (460 px; Stems since the stems plan's step 3) across the top; the pad bar has no Wave | Play switch since the one Sample page's step 6b (no sub-tabs, as drawn), so its sound card runs on to the three buttons. **On another kit** (sampler step 15e; the canvas "Pads and kits: step 15e", https://claude.ai/artifact/VDrYTMxR5ACoeJpUjUYZbH, board 4, approved by Jay 23 Nov 2026 16:11Z): while the page shows a kit other than the selected track's (a hit on a kit the pads play on their own), the pad bar's line starts with the kit's name in the text colour, and the pad pressed when the bank plays another bank of the kit ("Pad C1 · Snare 3" over "Big Kit, from pad B1 · Source 91ac…02 · ..."), and a raised 160 px chip, **Track 1's kit**, sits at the bar's right before the buttons: a tap shows the track's own kit again at its pad hit last. Edit pad's bar has the same, before its ‹ Pads. **Kit layers** (sampler step 15e's follow-up; the canvas "Kit layers and routes", https://claude.ai/artifact/BY2XQ1e7HhCQWNDGjNTzwu, board 1, approved by Jay 24 Nov 2026 14:41Z, "Build as drawn, with your picks"): after a hit that also played a kit layer, the line under the title holds a 22 px chip for each sound the hit played, the pads' own kit first ("Big Kit C1", "Lo-Fi A1 · layer"; the one shown filled in the accent), and the kit note moves beside the title; a tap shows that sound's kit and pad, and a layer picked stays for later hits while the pads play it. The bar keeps its height. Under them the page, with the shared Q-Link column (128 px) at the right, as the chain editor has it; the hint line under them. The strip of Q-Link cards along the bottom is gone, and the page gets its room.
- **The cards** are drawn as the column's: Q1 to Q4 in the knob's colour with the page's name (on Edit pad, section 4e, the section's), the job, and the ring with the value in it (its unit or offset under). A tap gives the wheel to the card where the page has a wheel focus; a drag up or down a card turns its knob, as on the Play tab; a faint card says why on the hint line.
- **The wheel's card** (the row picked on Auto-sample) is 56 px, under the page's list. On Wave the zoom is a tag in the overview's corner instead, with every tool (the card's place went to the Keep card, Jay's device notes of 20 Nov 2026). Edit pad (section 4e) has none: its panel outlines the control picked, and it puts the scenes in the panel instead. On Auto-sample the Q-Links turn nothing yet, so its cards are blank.
- **The panel under the wave** (Jay, 17 Nov 18:28Z and 18:33Z; mockups approved 18:47Z, https://claude.ai/artifact/15Vh6vDPt5GTF5EW845Fyr): the settings that sat in a list between the wave and the Q-Links become a 242 px band of labelled sections under the wave, holding knobs, choice buttons, ‹ › selectors, − + steppers, switches and buttons in the Synth tab's style. The wave then runs the page's width up to the Q-Link column, which stays on the right. A finger on a control gives it the wheel and - / +; the wheel picks the controls in the panel's order (section by section, top to bottom, left to right), and the one picked is outlined. A finger resting on a control with a value (not a button, not a faint one) puts it under the wheel until it lifts, as in section 6's hold and turn.
  - **Built: Wave.** The **recorder row** (64 px; the one Sample page's step 2a) across the top of the page's left column, so the Q-Link column stays where it is: REC (210 px; lit red while a take records, outlined with "Waits" while it waits for its start) with a second line under it (step 2c): where the next take goes, or how long the one recording has run, or what it waits for ("next take stays here", "next take onto A6", "0:05.1 · stays here", "for -30 dB · onto A6"), and, in the warning colour, why REC can't take ("Line in off: Looper tab, MENU"; a longer reason is cut there and the hint line says it in full). Then three cards that step with a tap on their left half (back) or right half (on), Source with its level as two bars (250 px), Starts (196 px: Now, On input with the threshold, Next bar) and Ends (166 px: On STOP, On silence, After 1 bar to After 32 bars, one list since step 2f, part 4, when the Rec page's Length went); the Source steps through Line in, Master, the page's pad and Auto-sample, then the tracks, the delay's return and the pad channels (step 2e, below); and, off the row since Jay's device notes of 20 Nov 2026 ("the capture ... button is at the top of the page, separate from the waveform display"), the **Keep** card (340 px) just over the wave's right end, beside the overview in the zoom card's place (step 2c; the mockup's "Keep last 4 bars", the one Sample page's decision 4: Sample Recall in one tap): a turn-back clock icon, the title saying what a tap keeps of the master Sample Recall holds ("Keep last 4 bars" while the music plays, fewer early on; "Keep last 10 s" stopped) a line naming the bars and saying where they go ("bars 11 to 14, as a new take", "bars 11 to 14, onto A6", the Looper's Capture's words for bars; "what just played, ..." stopped), and at its right a little wave (100 px) of exactly what a tap keeps, drawn as it plays (accent, faint when it can't keep). A tap keeps it as a new take where Onto says, as REC's take would (one undo step: "Recall take", or "Recall to A6"); a hold (0.6 s) opens Sample Recall over the page on the pad picked instead (below), keeping nothing. Faint, with the reason on its line, before the master is kept, before anything has played, while a take is ready, counting in or recording ("finish the take first": Sample Recall's rule, section 6 and REC arm rule 6, for the tap and the hold alike; the hold is refused with Sample Recall's words, the tap with "Finish the take first: Keep keeps what just played once it stops"), or with Next empty and no empty pad. A tap on REC is a pad take (rec-arm.md, A pad take), kept where Onto says when it stops (step 2b: Stay here, the default, keeps it on the page; Next empty or This pad put it on the pad the first line names), that goes at once instead of waiting for PLAY (Starts Next bar still waits for the music's next bar line, so stopped it waits for PLAY): the REC chip names it ("● REC → Take 4 · Line in", the name the take will get; "● REC → Pad A6 · Line in" when it goes onto a pad), REC here or on the MPC, or STOP, ends it and keeps what recorded with the music playing on, UNDO throws it away, nothing moves the arm until it ends, and REC then records what it did before; the hint line says so ("REC again or STOP: stop and keep the take · UNDO: throw it away · the music plays on · Q-Links 1-4: Gain, Threshold, Pre-roll, Monitor"). While another take counts in or records it is refused, said under REC ("Finish the take first: Drums records"). While REC is on the panel is one section, **Input** (Source, Channels and Onto: Stay here, Next empty or This pad, where the take goes when it stops, and Tail: Cut, Ring out or Loop, step 2f; then Gain, Threshold, Pre-roll and Silence for as knobs and Monitor's switch, step 2d; Source faint, as a take keeps its source; Tail faint unless Ends is After N bars, Silence for unless Ends is On silence, the tail rings out or loops, or the Source is the pad; each says why; the take running hears Silence for and Tail until it is past where they apply, then they are for the next take), and the Q-Links turn Gain, the Threshold, the Pre-roll and Monitor's level (the mockup's Recording board). Gain (-24 to +24 dB) turns the line input on its way into the take, so the Source card's bars and the threshold hear it and what is heard doesn't change; Monitor is the line input's one monitor, the Looper's "Monitor the input", and Q-Link 4 its level, the Mix tab's Input 1 fader (its card says "monitor off" under the level while it is off): how loud the input is in the mix while Monitor is on, and so in Sample Recall, Keep and a resample of the master; the looper and audio tracks record the input before it. While the source is a point in the mix both are faint ("Gain and Monitor are the line input's: a point in the mix records as you hear it"). **Sources that play** (step 2e; the mockup's decision 5, Flatten and Auto-sample become sources): with the page's pad as the Source ("Flatten A5"; "Flatten a pad" while a take shows) the Starts and Ends cards become Velocity and Held for (the Flatten velocity and hold), the Source card leaves out its channels and its bars show the pad's channel, and REC plays the pad once and records it until it goes quiet, kept where Onto says (Stay here: a take on the page, one undo step). Its line says "flattens A5 · stays here", then "plays A5 · stays here" while it waits for the sound, then the time. REC again stops it, keeping what recorded once the sound has begun (before, nothing). It is a pad take through the arm, as REC's take is: refused while another take counts in or records, REC on the MPC or STOP stop it the same way, UNDO throws it away, and the REC chip names it ("● REC → Break 92 flat · Flatten A5", the name the take will get). A held empty pad + REC records an input, so with the pad as the Source it is refused ("A held pad records an input: set the Sample page's Source to Line in or a point in the mix", on the hint line of the page showing). A Threshold or Pre-roll turned while REC's take waits for its sound reaches that take. Gain, Threshold, Pre-roll and Monitor are faint ("Flatten starts on the pad's first sound"), and REC says why it can't ("Flatten plays a pad, not a take", "The pads play the synth", "Flatten plays bank A's pads", "A5 has no sound to flatten"). With Auto-sample as the Source the two cards read "Plays · Note by note" and "Makes · An instrument", faint ("Auto-sample sets how it plays: REC opens it"), REC's line says "opens Auto-sample", and REC opens the Auto-sample page over the Wave page: its pad bar lights Wave, and its second button says "Wave" and comes back. The region's sections and the markers' Q-Links come back when the take ends. **A take on the page** (step 2b; decision 1 of the mockup: a fresh take stays on the page until it is put somewhere): a take that stays shows on the page with no pad: the pad bar's swatch in the REC colour and the title "Take 3 · new" (the subtitle ends "take 2 of 3" when there are more); the wave, the markers, the panel, the Sound section and Normalize work on it as on a pad, a tap on the wave plays it, and Send to pad and Chop to pads are both lit (Chop to pads picks the Slice tool on the take; step 3b). Send to pad then a hit puts it on that pad and the page shows the pad; SHIFT + wheel steps through the page's takes, then the pads with a sound (SHIFT + - / + still zooms). A take on the page is part of its kit: the project's save keeps it (and autosave), and opening another project or New project asks first, as for any unsaved change, so a take is never lost without the musician being asked; the page keeps 32 ("The page keeps 32 takes: put some on pads first"). The hint line: "Tap the wave: hear the take · Send to pad, then hit a pad: put it there · SHIFT + wheel: another sound · Q-Links 1-4: Start, End, loop · …". **The take drawn as it records** (step 2f, part 3: the Rec page's half-minute input view goes; the mockup's Recording board): once a take's sound starts, the wave and the overview are the take's. What has come in is drawn bright from the left of its span (its length when Ends is After N bars, else four bars, or 10 s with the transport stopped; twice that each time the take reaches the right edge), with the bar lines while the transport rolls and a line at the head; there are no marks (nor the Grain tool's window), a tap or drag on the wave says why on the hint line ("The wave is the take as it records: REC again or STOP keeps it, then trim it here"), and the wheel does nothing until it ends. It redraws every 0.1 s. The pad bar's swatch is the REC colour and its title "Take 4 · recording" ("Pad A6 · recording" for a held pad's take), with "0:05.1 so far · stays here" under it. When the take ends, the wave is the page's sound's again: the take itself when it stays on the page. Sample screen `sample-recording`. Under the row, the **tools** (360 px wide, 56 px, left of the overview in its row; the one Sample page's tools strip): Trim, Slice, Warp and Grain as one switch, the tool in use raised. The tool decides what a finger on the wave moves, which marks are bright, the panel's first section and the Q-Links. Every tool's marks stay on the wave, but the tab strip over it holds only the picked tool's tabs: the other tools' marks are faint lines with no tab, which move when their tool is picked (Trim keeps START, LOOP and END); Trim is the page as described here; Slice, Warp and Grain are on the page too (steps 3 to 5, below). While REC is on the strip is faint and a tap says why ("The tools wait for the take: stop it first"); Input and its Q-Links win. Beside the tools, the overview (56 px, narrower by the tools; the zoom in a small tag at its top right, "1.0× · start") with the Keep card (340 px), and the wave under them keeping the column's full width, and the panel: Mode (the play modes; the loop, Off, Forward or Ping-pong; Crossfade), Gain and fades (Gain, Fade in, Fade out; Normalize to 0 dB) and Options (Reverse, Snap, Channels), with the pad's **Sound** section beside it (340 px; the one Sample page, Jay 18 Nov 11:46Z, mockup https://claude.ai/artifact/NSGLzNnVy5zTA3ykWmpGzU): Amp, Filter, Pitch, Movement and Grain as buttons along its top, and the open part's four controls under them, Edit pad's own (section 4e; Amp: Attack, Decay, Sustain, Release; Filter: its type, Cutoff, Resonance, Env depth; Pitch: Tune, Wow & flutter, Vel → pitch, Vintage; Movement: LFO to, LFO rate, LFO depth, Pitch env) and the cloud's (Grain, step 5: Position, Size, Density, Grain pitch of the layer shown, faint while it doesn't play grains, saying "set Mode to Granular"). Warp and its tempo are the Warp tool's alone, and the page shows one Tune, the pad's (Jay, 12 Nov: never two look-alike settings on one screen that do different things). A sliced pad's cuts (sampler R4) show on the wave as faint lines with no tab, the Slice tool's marks; they stay put (they move with the Slice tool).
  - **Built: the Warp tool** (the one Sample page's step 4, in place of the Warp sub-page, which goes with its picture and its four sections). With Warp picked on the tools strip the page keeps its frame, the recorder row, the overview and the wave; the wave is drawn as the layer plays (stretched between its pins, so a pinned hit sits on its beat line), with the bars and beats of its warp tempo counted from the region's start (a grid line between beats once they are 12 px apart) and the hits found as short ticks along its foot (faint with the other tools). Pins are numbered tabs in the wave's tab strip (with the other tools, faint lines with no tab). A tap near a hit pins it to the nearest grid line or picks its pin, and dragging a pin's tab moves it along the beats; Trim's marks are faint lines with no tab, as for every tool. The panel's Mode, Gain and fades and Options give way to the **Warp** section (the same 242 px band, up to the Sound section) in two parts: **Warp** (300 px: Warp as Off, Stretch and Repitch buttons; Warp tempo, typed or stepped, and Grid; then Process's two, **New sound's pitch** in semitones and **New sound** with the button **Make it**, named so the pitch can't pass for a live one beside Sound's Tune), its note saying what Warp does now, or with a pitch set what a tap makes ("new sound: 2 st up, tap Make it"); and **Pins** (450 px: Hits, Pin and Pin beat; Pin hits, Remove pin and Clear pins), its note the number of pins. A faint button says why on the hint line. The Q-Links are Pin, Pin beat, Warp tempo and Scroll, and the wheel zooms round the pin picked. The hint line: "Tap a hit: pin it to the grid · Q-Links 1-4: pin, its beat, warp tempo, scroll · …".
  - **Built: the Slice tool** (the one Sample page's step 3, in place of the Chop sub-page, which goes). With Slice picked on the tools strip, or by Chop to pads, the page keeps its frame, the recorder row, the overview and the wave. The slices are numbered tabs in the wave's tab strip, each cut in its slice's colour (the theme's series colours; with the other tools, faint lines with no tab), the picked one shaded; a **lane** along the wave's foot (30 px, the wave drawn above it) names the pad each slice lands on in its colour ("A6"; on 1 pad "A5 · 3"; on New kit the new kit's pads from A1), lit once that pad plays just that slice, the picked one outlined (a tap on the lane is a tap on the wave over it; none on Library); the first marker and END are the sound's Start and End, which stay put (a touch says to move them with Trim). A tap on a slice picks it and plays it once (on a take kept on the page too); dragging a cut's tab moves it. The panel's Mode, Gain and fades and Options give way to two sections (the same 242 px band, up to the Sound section): **Slice** (330 px, as drawn: Method as a ‹ › chip, Transient, Beat with a tempo, Grid, Silence, Pitch (sampler R4.7), Manual, Live, over the Snap to zero switch, beside the method's setting as a knob; the slice picked as a chip, "Slice 3 of 15"; Split, Merge and Clear), its note how many slices and how ("15 slices · by transient"); and **Put on** (420 px, as drawn: the bank's **pad map**, its 16 pads laid out as the MPC's (pad 1 bottom left), each slice's number on its pad in the slice's colour, the picked one outlined and pads with a sound raised, over a ‹ › chip that reads the 16 pads the map shows ("A1–A16", round from H to A); beside it Pads, 1 pad, Keys, New kit (sampler R4.6, on a new track), Audio track (step 6) and Library (step 6c: the pad's sound, its settings and each slice saved in the library, under Samples › Exports) as buttons two to a row, the Phrase switch, and the main button, lit, 44 px high, naming what it does: "Chop to A6–B4", "Slices on A5", "Phrase on A5", "Chop to new kit", "To audio track", "Save 15 slices"; grey while every slice is on its pad already and no phrase is asked for, saying so, since chopping again would only undo edits to those pads), its note which pads with a sound it would replace ("no pad replaced", "replaces A5") or why it can't go now. A tap on a pad of the map starts the slices there (on 1 pad, that pad takes them), as far on as they fit; tapped where they start already, the hint line says so. On New kit the map shows the new kit's pads from A1, faint; on Library it is faint and empty. **Audio track** puts the slices on the song instead of on pads: the sound the slices span goes on the first empty audio track (with no audio track in the project, one is added at the end of the track list, as + Track's Audio track would add it, not armed), else the first with nothing from that bar on, from the start of the bar the playhead is in, a region a slice one after another, at the sound's own speed (as the MPC's Audio Track from Samples does). With none of the 8 empty or free from that bar, nothing goes on. On it the map and its chip are faint and empty, First pad (Q-Link 4) is faint, the lane names no pad and Phrase is faint, each saying why (no pad; Phrase is for pads, and on an audio track the slices already play one after another). The pads and the page don't change. The hint line says where the slices went ("Break 92's 15 slices on audio 1 from bar 5"; with song mode off, "...from bar 5; it plays in song mode") or why not (no audio track free from that bar, the memory full). The map and PAD BANK: the map opens on the bank the slices start in, and its chip turns the map alone, never the bank the pads play, which stays PAD BANK's (and SHIFT + PAD BANK's). The two are kept apart because PAD BANK also takes the Sample tab to the same pad of the new bank (so the pad it edits is one the pads play); a chip that turned the pads' bank would take the page off the pad being chopped. PAD BANK moves the map with it, since the map follows the pad the page then shows. So the chip is named by the pads it shows ("B1–B16"), never "Bank B", and can't be mistaken for the pads' bank (Jay, 12 Nov: no two look-alike settings that do different things). A faint button says why on the hint line. The Q-Links are the method's setting, Slice, the slice's start (SHIFT: a frame a detent) and First pad (the mockup's per-slice fade on Q-Link 4 waits until slices have fades of their own), and the wheel zooms round the slice picked. Once the slice picked is on its pad (its lane cell lit), the **Sound section** beside the panel shows that pad, titled "Sound · slice 3 → A8" with the note "what the pad plays", and its knobs edit it, one undo step a turn; its Grain part is faint there ("A slice's grains are set on its own pad: hit the pad"). Picking another slice moves it; a cut moved (that slice's lane cell dark) puts it back on the page's sound. The wheel walks the panel in its order: Method, Snap to zero, the setting, the slice, Split, Merge, Clear, then the pad map (its - / + step the first pad), Map (the map's chip), Put on, Phrase and the main button. The hint line: "Hit a pad to chop it · tap a slice: hear it · Q-Links 1-4: setting, slice, its start, first pad · …"; with Live, "Start plays the sound: hit pads in time to drop cuts · …". **A take on the page**: its slices go on pads straight from the page (the panel's Chop to, or the bar's Chop to pads): with no pad of its own it starts them at the first empty pads from A1, and the take leaves the page with them (its sound now lives on those pads); one UNDO brings the take back and empties the pads. On Pads, Keys or 1 pad the page then shows the pad of the slice picked. New kit and Library work on a take too (New kit as for a pad; Library keeps the take on the page). Live plays a pad, so on a take Start is faint and says why; another tool, pad or page stops a live take. The Slice tool opens on a sliced pad's own slices.
  - **Put in a cell** (audio tracks phase 2, approved 22 Nov 23:20Z, section 4c): with **Audio track** picked in Put on, the Phrase row (pads only) becomes a two-way switch, **On the song | In a cell**. It starts on On the song, which does what To audio track did.
    - **Which cell.** On In a cell, the main button puts the sound in a cell: from Start to End, as one region, ignoring the slices. The cell is the first empty one on the armed track, if it is an audio track with an empty cell; else the first audio track with one; else a new audio track at the end.
    - **The button names the cell:** "Into Vocal, row 3", or "Into a new audio track".
    - **The section's note says how the clip plays:** "loops 2 bars at 92 BPM" when the tempo is known (the tempo finder's or the file's), the length rounded to the nearest beat; "plays once · 5.2 s" when it isn't.
    - The sound is written to a file of its own and becomes a take, as To audio track does. Making the clip is one UNDO step.
  - **Built: the Grain tool** (the one Sample page's step 5, in place of the Grain sub-page, which goes with its section chips, cloud picture, touch pad and 260 px band). With Grain picked on the tools strip the sound plays as grains (a sound in another mode becomes Granular, one undo step, from its loudest grain when its grain settings are untouched; Jay's device notes, 20 Nov 2026) and the page keeps its frame, the recorder row, the overview and the wave; the wave shows the **grain window** in the grain colour: a box from where the grains start, one grain long, inside the band the spray scatters their starts over (as played, while the pad sounds), with two tabs, **GRAIN** at its start (the tab left of its line) and **SIZE** at its end (the tab right of its line), so the two never cover each other however short the grains. Dragging GRAIN moves the position and SIZE the size, and a tap on the wave moves GRAIN there; Trim's marks (Start, End, the loop) and the slice lines are faint lines with no tab, as for every tool, so they never crowd GRAIN and SIZE. The playhead is where the grains read. While the pad plays, each grain playing is a mark on the wave where it reads, round the wave's middle in the grain colour, as tall and bright as its window is open (none while a take records). The panel's Mode, Gain and fades and Options give way to the **Grain** section (the same 242 px band, up to the Sound section): its parts as one switch along its top (**Grains**, **Shape**, **Filter**, **Keys**, **Mod**, **LFO**, **Env**, **Presets**; Mod added and Filter, LFO and Env shortened so eight fit, sampler step 17c, layout A as Jay picked it on 24 Nov 2026), and under it the part's controls on one line (Grains: Scan, Spray, Size spray, Pitch spray, Level spray, Timing, Reverse, Stereo and Level knobs, each name whole, on two lines where it is long, then Spray shape as a selector; Filter: Pass, Off, Low pass, Band pass and High pass as buttons, then Grain cutoff, Grain res, Filter spray; Keys: Size follows key, Scan follows key, Glide; Mod: Route 1 from and Route 1 to as selectors (150 px each) for the route outlined, then the four routes' depth knobs named by what they route ("Vel → size", "Press → density", "Key → scan", "Route 4" while off, faint), the outlined one ringed in the accent; a touch on a knob outlines its route, and the selectors then read "Route 3 from" and "Route 3 to"; LFO: Grain LFO to and Wave as selectors, Rate, Depth, Sync; Env: Grain env to as a selector, Depth, Grain attack, Grain decay, Grain sustain) or on two lines (Shape: Grain mode and Quality, Clean, Tape and Lo-fi as three buttons; then the grains' Shape, Pitch quantise and Tempo sync as selectors. Presets: the eight as two lines of four buttons, the one on lit). The grains' own filter, LFO, envelope and pitch say Grain in their names, as the Sound section beside them holds the pad's own filter, envelope, LFO and Pitch (Jay, 12 Nov: never two look-alike settings on one screen that do different things). A grain mode other than Cloud shows only its own controls. The section's note says how the pad plays while it doesn't play grains (after an undo: "Plays as Loop: tap Grain to play it as grains"; a tap on Grain makes it Granular again), else the open part's word (the Mod part's route outlined in words, "route 1: harder hits move the size up"; what the LFO, the envelope or the filter does, the preset's), else how the cloud plays ("18 grains playing", "thinned to fit 64"). Q-Links: Position, Size, Density, Grain pitch (with SHIFT: Spray, finer Size and Density, Pitch spray). The hint line: "Drag GRAIN to move where grains start, SIZE for their length · tap the wave: grains start there · hit the pad to hear the cloud · SHIFT + Q-Link 1 / 4: the sprays"; with Mod open, "Touch a route's knob, then pick its From and To · hit the pad to hear it · From Off: the route does nothing".
- **Sample Recall** (sampler R5 part 2; the sampler review's Recall mockup, `recall.png`): a page of its own over the page it was opened on, under the top bar, as the undo history is. It opens with a kit pad held + SHIFT + REC (section 6), or a hold on the Wave page's Keep card (step 2c). Its picture (the page's width up to the Q-Link column) has the title line ("SAMPLE RECALL · the master's last 2:00, always kept") with the REC chip's text for while it is open ("REC → Pad A6 · Master, effects on"; display only, the arm doesn't move), the two minutes as an overview with numbered bar lines and the bracket, the bracket's line ("Bars 12 to 15 · 10.43 s · lands warped at 92 BPM"), the bracket drawn large with its bar lines, and what keeping does. Under it a 150 px panel: From (230 px, the one Sample page's step 2f; Jay, 18 Nov 19:48Z: Skip back moves here as a second source: Master, or the recorder's Source by name, "Line in"), Length (300 px: 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 bars or Free), Lands on (200 px: the pad, "next free" or "replaces its sound"), Warp (130 px, faint on Free) and Audition and Keep (260 px, Keep the main button). It opens on Master. From the Source it reads what the sampler's Source has kept (the line input's last 1:15, as the Rec page's Skip back did): the overview spans that, the title line says "SAMPLE RECALL · Line in's last 1:15, kept while it is the Source" and the chip "REC → Pad A6 · Line in", and Keep and Audition take from it. The Source's choice is faint when there is nothing of it to read (the line input off: "Line in off: Looper tab, MENU"; the Source is the master: "The recorder's Source is the master: From Master has it"), and a tap on From says why on the hint line; if its buffer goes while it is read, it reads the master again and says so. Q-Links: Length, Ends at, Fade, Pad. The hint line says what PLAY, REC and STOP do. A change of page closes it.
- **The Stems page** (the stems plan's step 3; mockups in the stems research notes, `stems/mockups/`): a page of its own over the page it was opened on, as Sample Recall is, splitting the sound shown into four new sounds that add up to it, Vocals, Drums, Bass and Other (`mpfree-stems`, Spleeter's model). The pad bar's **Stems** button opens it for the pad or take the page shows and, lit while it is open, takes the page back to Wave; it is faint while the page shows no sound. Its picture (the page's width up to the Q-Link column): the title line (the sound, what it reads, its length, "four new sounds that add up to it", and at the right how long it will take), then the sound over four lanes, one a stem in its own series colour, the same on the pad map and its Q-Link (the theme's fourth, third, second and first track colours: in the dark theme Vocals purple, Drums yellow, Bass green, Other blue), each with its name card (its name, what it is doing, and a switch: a tap on the card keeps the stem or not), and the 11.9 s segments the split works in marked along the top. Under it the 242 px panel in three sections:
  - **Split** (300 px): Reads as two buttons (Start to End, the sound's markers, Akai's "Use trimmed sample", or Whole sound), then three lines of what is so, not controls (the wheel passes them): Takes ("about 31 s (2 cores, full band)"; the plan's 10.4 s a segment until a split on the MPC says how long one takes), Holds ("176 MB while it runs · 1.1 GB free") and Makes ("4 sounds · 17 MB"). The section's note says how many are kept ("4 of 4 kept"); all four are worked out together, so a stem switched off is just not kept, and the split takes as long.
  - **Put on** (470 px), as the Slice tool's: the bank's pad map with the stems' names on the pads they land on in their colours (on Layers the sound's own pad, outlined, "4 layers"), over its ‹ › chip ("A1–A16"); 4 pads (the default, named by how many are kept, "3 pads": the first run of empty pads after the sound's, or from a pad tapped on the map, which picks 4 pads from there), Layers (the sound's own pad plays the four as its layers 1 to 4, together, in place of the sound, Akai's "Assign to Pad"), Takes (four takes on the page), New kit (a new kit's pads A1 to A4, on a new track) and Library (under Samples › Stems) as buttons two to a row; and the main button naming what it does ("Split to A6–A9", "Split into A5's layers", "Split to 4 takes", "Split to new kit", "Save 4 stems"). Its note says which pads with a sound it would replace ("no pad replaced") or why it can't go (in the warning colour).
  - **Listen** (326 px): the four stems' levels as knobs in their colours (the same levels as the Q-Links), and under them Play all four, faint until there are stems to hear ("The stems play here once split.").
- **While it splits:** a progress line over the picture and "42 % · about 18 s left" at the title line's right; the lanes fill segment by segment as the stems come, each saying where it is ("2 of 3 done", "working…", "waits"); the main button is **Stop** (nothing lands); Split's settings are faint. The split is a job on the sampler's sound lane, a step at a time, so the screen and the pads go first. Leaving the page doesn't stop it: it lands when it's done, and the pad bar's Stems button says how far it has got ("Stems 42 %") on every Sample page; Stems shows it again, whatever pad is picked (one split at a time). Opening another project, New project or a kit file loaded stops it. STOP stops the music, not the split.
- **Once it lands:** the four stems drawn whole on their lanes, on their pads, as one undo step ("UNDO takes them back"); the main button faint, "On A6–A9", until something changes. A tap on a stem's wave plays it; Play all four plays them together at their levels.
- **Q-Links 1 to 4** are the four stems' levels, both as heard on the page and as they land (each pad's or layer's level), so there are no audition-only levels.
- **What can't start**, with its reason as Put on's note in the warning colour and in full on the hint line: no stems model on the stick ("No stems model in files/models/ on the stick yet"), too little memory ("Needs 220 MB, 120 MB free"). A damaged model file is found by the split's first step, which says the reader's reason on the hint line, and the page is ready again.
- **The hint line** says what the page's controls do ("Split to A6–A9: work out the stems · a pad on the map: start there · tap a stem: keep it or not · Q-Links 1-4: each stem's level · Stems: back to Wave"). A change of page closes it; the split runs on. There is no band choice on the page: the stems are full band (to 16.5 kHz, the stems plan's decision 2).

## 4j. The clip settings card

The sequencer review's "clip settings card" (Jay's go on the whole roadmap, 16 Nov 20:47Z: "get started actually implementing all of this"; drawn in mockup C, https://claude.ai/artifact/NLTZcfgFDo4A579VMJBLzC). Mockup C draws it in the side panel of Perform; until Perform is built (sequencer S4) it is a tab of Arrange's Record panel, in the same 250 px place.
- **The tabs:** the Record panel's title line is two tabs, **Record** (66 px) and **Clip · Verse B** (the rest: the focused clip's name, cut with "…" when long, its track being the armed one; "Clip" with no clip; the mockup's "Clip · Bass · Verse B" did not fit beside Record at 250 px). Until the scene card (section 4l, the Arrange refactor's 5.2) a third tab, **Scene**, was the scene editor. The lit one is underlined; a tap on another swaps the panel, on the grid, the Song view and the song list alike. The note editor keeps its Edit options.
- **The focused clip:** the armed track's playing clip, else its first, as Beats and the note editor show (the review's "one focused clip").
- **The rows:** Length (in sixteenths, as Beats' length), Speed (×1/8 to ×4), Swing (the track's, or 50 to 75 %), Quantise (off, 1/4 to 1/32 with triplets), Strength (10 to 100 %, "—" while Quantise is off), Follow (off, Next, Previous, First, Last, Any, Other, Again, Stop), After (in bars, "—" while Follow is off), Launch (sequencer S4: "project", then the clip's own, at once, 1/16, 1/8, 1/4, 1/2, 1, 2, 4 or 8 bars), Mode (Restart; Legato: launched over a playing clip, it plays on from the place that one had reached; or Once, sequencer S4: a one-shot, which plays one pass and its track stops where the pass ends, its cell saying where it stops, "stops 3.4", from its last bar; its Follow waits), Program (sequencer S5, the review's program change per clip: off, or 1 to 128, sent to the track's outside gear as the clip starts) and Bank (its bank select, "—" while Program is off: off, or the two numbers sent, CC 0 / CC 32, "0/0" to "127/127"; SHIFT + an arrow steps a whole CC 0) and Direction (sequencer S6, the mockup's play direction: Forward, Reverse, Ping pong or Random, the order the clip plays its 1/16 steps; "—" on a clip that isn't two whole steps or more, which plays forward, where a step puts it back to Forward and then says why; sample screen `clips-card-direction`) and Mutate (sequencer S6, the Torso T-1's mutate with restore: "original", then "variant" once mutated; › makes a new variation of the clip's notes, SHIFT + › a bolder one, ‹ restores the original; "—" on a clip with no notes; sample screen `clips-card-mutate`) and Melody (step sequencer refactor E1, design doc rev 16, Jay's go 22 Nov: the card "Recording fixes, then Convert", 10:09Z, and the shorter words, 11:50Z: "pads" when the clip follows the pads or "notes" when it uses notes, the design's "Follows pads" and "Uses notes" shortened to fit the value at 250 px, "—" on a loop's cell; › on a synth track's clip that follows the pads opens Convert to notes' preview, whose words take the hint line while the buttons under the rows are Cancel and, when it can convert, Convert; sample screen `clips-card-convert`). The wheel picks a row and – / + step it, as on the record settings. A loop cell takes only Follow, After and Launch.
- **Undo:** each change plays at once; one row turned is one take ("Settings 4 · Bass / 2"), which UNDO takes back.
- **SHIFT + an arrow** on Length doubles the clip, its notes repeated in the new half, or halves it (the notes past the end come back on UNDO); on Speed it doubles or halves the speed. These are the roadmap's double and speed buttons; the hint line under the grid says so while the card shows.
- **The Record tab's Quantise row** (the roadmap's record quantise, the MPC's Timing Correct while recording): a seventh row after Pressure, for the armed track: Off (the notes go in as played, the default) or 1/4 to 1/32 with triplets, the grid the notes it records start on (their lengths kept). "–" with no track armed. The clip's own Quantise on the Clip tab still moves them on playback.
- **The Record tab's Transpose and Follows rows** (sequencer S4, live transpose, Jay's card of 17 Nov 2026 21:43Z): a ninth and tenth row after Launch, for the armed track. Transpose: "as written", "+5 st", or "+3 in key" for a track moved by notes of its key, – / + a semitone or a note at a time within 24 either way. Follows: On or Off (a new track's), whether 16 Levels' Transpose moves the track with the armed one. Both "–" with no track armed or one that plays a kit, the Drums engine or loops.
- **The Record tab's Loop row while following another device's position** (sequencer S5, the review's "the leader owns the timeline"): with Sync's Position on, the transport's loop is off for as long as it follows. The row reads "1–4, off while following" (or "off while following" with no loop), – / + set the loop that comes back when following stops, and Arrange's transport bar shows that loop's reading greyed.
- **The Record tab's Launch row** (sequencer S4, the review's transport bar "Launch 1 bar", mockup K): an eighth row after Quantise, the project's launch quantise, the line a tapped clip, a stop or a scene lands on while the music plays: at once, 1/16, 1/8, 1/4, 1/2, 1 bar (the default), 2, 4 or 8 bars. Arrange's transport bar shows it as a reading, "Launch 1 bar", between the meter and Swing. A clip's own Launch on the Clip tab wins for that clip; a scene's cues all take the project's. A cue waiting when it changes keeps its line.
- **Save state and Reload** (sequencer S4, Elektron's temp save and reload; mockup C draws Save state on the card and Reload clip on the morph bar): two buttons side by side under the card's rows (44 px), the rows scrolling over them. Save state keeps the focused clip as it is now (its notes, locks, moves, length and settings), in place of a state kept before; it is faint on a loop's cell, whose layers have the looper's undo. Reload, lit once a state is kept, puts it back: while the clip plays and the transport rolls, on its launch line (the clip's own Launch or the project's), so the saved pattern starts on the beat; otherwise at once. A reload is a take ("Reload 3 · saved state") that UNDO takes back. While it waits it is outlined amber, and a tap takes it back; STOP lands it. **The morph bar** gets a fourth button after Save, **Reload clip** (104 px; the other three narrow to 96, 64 and 76 px), the same reload for the focused clip, faint until it has a saved state and amber while it waits. Saved states last while the project is open (not saved with it); deleting, moving or copying a clip, or moving a row, forgets the states of the slots it touches.
- **Mutate and Restore** (sequencer S6, the Torso T-1's mutate and the KeyStep's Mutate and Original; the card's thirteenth row, since its 250 px foot holds only two buttons): › on Mutate (or + with the row picked) makes a new variation of the focused clip's notes at once, from the clip as it was before its first Mutate, which the clip keeps and saves; SHIFT + › varies about half the notes in place of a quarter. A variation changes a note's velocity, its chance, its ratchet or, on a track that plays notes, its pitch to the next one the clip plays. ‹ (Restore) puts the original back, whatever was edited since. Each is a take ("Mutate 4 · variant 2", "Restore 5 · the original") that UNDO takes back, and the hint line names the variant. Refused while recording; on a loop's cell the row is "—" and says why.
- **An audio clip's card** (audio tracks phase 2, approved 22 Nov 23:20Z as drawn on board C, https://claude.ai/artifact/A7K7GE4ZAxyjNiJgQJbncu). The Clip tab follows an audio track armed by its own arm (its name on the grid, its arm dot on the Song view) until a note track is armed. Its rows:
  - Length: a loop's length in bars and beats; – / + steps a beat, SHIFT a bar (a shorter loop cuts its regions there, the frames cut off coming back when it grows again). A once clip shows its time, such as 3:17, and doesn't step.
  - Mode: Restart, Legato or Once.
  - Launch, Follow and After.
  - Gain: the clip's own, from off to +12 dB in 0.5 dB steps (SHIFT 0.1 dB), on top of each region's gain.
  - Name: a tap opens the naming keyboard, as on the Sample page.

  Speed, Swing, Quantise, Strength, Program, Bank, Direction and Mutate don't apply, so they aren't shown.
  - **Its foot**, where a note clip's Save state and Reload are, has two parts. Two lines say the tempo the clip was made at and what that does here: "Made at 120 BPM:" (amber) over "at 92 a 1.2 s gap each pass", "Made at 92 BPM," over "the project's tempo", or for a once clip "Plays to its end" over "at any tempo". Under them **Fit to tempo** (44 px, the panel's width) makes a fitted copy of the audio at the current tempo, keeping the pitch. It runs on a worker ("Fitting… 40 %", the button filling as it goes) and is one UNDO step. It is faint when the clip is already at the project's tempo, and for a once clip; a tap on it then says why on the hint line.
  - One row turned is one take ("Settings 4 · Vocal / 2"), as for note clips. The hint line under the grid says "Fit to tempo: a copy that fits 92 BPM, same pitch · UNDO goes back" in place of SHIFT's.
- **Continue** (composing tools, phase 1 step 4; the doc "Composing tools", https://claude.ai/artifact/HjU5wKNXdHtWffH9HeaEZb, "a row on the clip card"; sample screen `clips-card-continue`; approved as drawn, Jay's card, 22 Nov 2026 23:00Z): the card's fifteenth row, after Melody (fourteenth and after Mutate as drawn; Melody came first on main). It reads "+4 bars" on a clip of notes (the bars Continue adds as it opens), "no tune" on a clip of pad hits, "no notes" on an empty clip and "—" on a loop's cell. Either arrow (or – / + with the row picked) opens the note editor on the focused clip, the Sequencer showing it, with Continue open as its Edit options row opens it (the drawer, the tool bar and the roll as the Notes part says), asking at once; the other values say why in place of the hint. Nothing on the card itself changes.
- **Not built yet** from the mockup's rows: the clip's name (for note clips) and colour, step size, and the Copy clip button (COPY + a clip does it).

## 4k. The song list

The sequencer review's "song list for MPC thinkers" (Jay's go on the whole roadmap, 16 Nov 20:47Z; mockup D, https://claude.ai/artifact/NLTZcfgFDo4A579VMJBLzC): Arrange's third view beside the Clips grid and the Song view. Sample screen `arrange-list`.
- **Where:** in the grid's and the morph bar's place (the morph bar is not on this view), with the Record panel (or its Clip tab) and Takes beside it and the Q-Link column at the right, as on the grid. It is reached by the view chip on any Arrange title line (section 4f; phase 5, approved 23 Nov 2026 11:43Z), and in Perform by MAIN, which goes Clips, Song, List and round. Its own title line has the view chip at the left and **Loop the list: off / on** (132 px) at the right. (Until phase 5 it was **List** on the Song view's title line, and the list had Clips and Song buttons.)
- **The title:** "7 steps · 76 bars · 3:18" (the time at the tempo now; the lit chip names the view, so since phase 5 the title doesn't say "Song list"); nothing while it is empty, with a line under the add row saying to add the scenes in the order they play.
- **The rows** (50 px, 8 px apart), a step each, under the column names #, Scene, Times, Bars, Starts: the step's number, its scene's colour (its row's), its scene's name in a box with ‹ and › (a plain row is "Row 3"), its times in a box with – and + ("× 2", 1 to 99), its bars (the scene's length times its times; a scene's length is its follow's bars, else its longest clip, as Scenes → song has it) and the bar it starts on when the list plays from the top. A tap on ‹ or › (the box's outer thirds) steps the scene through the rows with a clip, going round; on – or + the times. A tap elsewhere on the row picks it (outlined in the accent colour) on the lift; a drag up or down scrolls a long list. **The grip** (the Arrange refactor's phase 4b, approved with the linked list's screens, 23 Nov 2026 01:10Z): two short bars at the row's left, before its number; a drag that starts there (on the bars or the number) moves the step: the row is outlined and an accent line with a dot at each end shows the gap it lands in, as dragging a section's name on the Song view's ruler does, and letting go there moves it (UNDO takes it back). Let go where it was, nothing moves; a tap there picks it. The step playing is filled green and outlined, with "pass 3 of 4" and a bar filling over the whole step; the step waiting for its line is outlined amber with "next".
- **The add row** (dashed, after the last step): "+ Add a scene · then: Stop" ("then: from the top" while the list goes round). A tap puts a step in after the one picked (at the end with none): the next row with a clip after the picked step's scene, once, and picks it. Faint at 128 steps.
- **The buttons** under the list (46 px): **List → song** (150 px) lays the list on the end of the song, each step its scene's clips for its bars with the scene's name as a section marker, as Scenes → song does; **Play from here** (150 px) plays the list from the picked step, its scene launched as a scene is, on the next launch line (at once while stopped); **Remove** (120 px) takes the picked step out, and in Perform needs the half-second hold (section 6's guard). Every edit of the list is a take ("List 5 · song list") that UNDO takes back.
- **While it plays:** each step's scene plays its times, and in its last bar the next step's scene is cued for the bar line where the step ends, as a follow action is; after the last step every track stops on that line, or with Loop the list on the first step plays again. The scenes' own follow actions wait while the list plays. A scene launched by hand, Stop all or Back to song ends the list (what plays plays on). NEXT names the next step ("Verse in 2 bars", "Stop all in 1 bar").
- A step's number held opens its scene's card over the list (section 4l; the Arrange refactor's 5.2). Until then the side panel's card of the picked step's scene was the Record panel's Scene tab.
- **Linked to the song** (the Arrange refactor's phase 4b; decision 8, "One song, two views", 23 Nov 2026 00:23Z; these screens approved as drawn 01:10Z, https://claude.ai/artifact/LCy1z8wtru2BMz2oypKQqg): the list is the song's parts, so the list and the Song view show the same thing, and editing either changes both. A new project's list is linked (from 4b.4). A project saved before keeps its own list until Link (4b.5). Sample screen `arrange-list-linked` (and from their steps `arrange-list-link`, `arrange-song-passes`).
  - **The title:** "Linked to the song · 7 parts · 50 bars · 2:10" (phase 5; before, "Song list · linked to the song · …").
  - **The rows** are the song's parts, each from its marker to the next. A row shows the marker's name in the Scene box; its scene's colour, or no swatch for a part that is the song's own; its times (× N: N passes, each its scene's length); its bars, and the bar of the song it starts on. The song's bars before its first marker are row 1, "Song", with faint words and no grip; a tap on its boxes says "Bars before the first marker: add a marker on the Song view's ruler to make them a part". With a marker on bar 1 there is no such row.
  - **‹ ›** puts another scene's clips in the part (the next row with a clip, going round), and the part takes that scene's name. **– / +** change its times: + adds a pass at its end and everything after moves later; – takes its last pass out and everything after closes up. A part that doesn't follow a scene (the song's own, or changed in the song) is × 1, and + repeats its whole length once more.
  - **The grip** moves the part, and everything under it in the song moves with it, as dragging its name on the Song view's ruler does. Nothing goes before the Song row.
  - **+ Add a scene** puts a part after the one picked: the next row with a clip, × 1. The parts after it move later.
  - **Changed in the song** (4b.3, built): once a part is edited inside on the Song view, it shows "● changed in the song" in the warning colour at the row's right (words only, with no badge on the Song view's ruler), where "pass 3 of 4" shows while it plays. The edits that count: a block moved, deleted, or made longer or shorter, and Rec song over it. It no longer follows its scene: a clip edited on the grid still plays there, but a clip added to the scene's row doesn't arrive. Its passes become plain bars (× 1). The hint line says it as it happens: "Drop is the song's own now: Back to the scene on the song list puts the scene's clips back".
  - **The buttons:** **Back to the scene** (150 px, 4b.3, built) takes List → song's place. It is lit when a changed part is picked, and puts the scene's clips back for the part's bars, as one UNDO. Otherwise it is faint and says why ("Drop follows its scene already", "Song has no scene"). **Play from here** (4b.4, built) plays the song from the part's first bar, with song mode on. **Remove** takes the part out with everything under it, and the song closes up (Perform's hold, as now).
  - **Loop the list** (4b.4, built) loops the song from its first part to the end of its last.
  - **While it plays** in song mode, the part under the playhead is lit with its pass ("pass 3 of 4"). Launching a scene over it, and Back to song, work as they do now. Every edit is a take that UNDO takes back whole, the song with it.
  - **Link** (84 px, left of Loop the list; 4b.5, built) shows only on a list that isn't linked. A tap opens a chooser under it with two choices; each is one UNDO, and once linked, Link goes. **Song from the list:** "The list's 7 steps become the song; its old bars go after them". **List from the song:** "The song's 5 marked parts become the list, each × 1". The hint line: "Link: the list and the song become one · Song from the list, or List from the song · UNDO takes it back". List → song stays on an unlinked list, and Song → scenes stays on the Song view, for copies instead of a link.
  - **The hint line** on a linked list: "Tap a part to pick it · ‹ ›: its scene · – / +: times · drag its grip: move it · + Add a scene: after the pick · Play from here: the song from there".

## 4l. The scene card

The sequencer review's scene settings (Jay's go on the whole roadmap, 16 Nov 20:47Z; mockup D's side card of a scene, "Rename" and "Mutes in this scene") came first as the Record panel's Scene tab. The Arrange refactor's 5.2 made them a card of their own, with Keep playing, the scene's tempo (5.2b), Capture (5.3) and three row tools (Jay's yes on the scene card's spec diff, "Yes, as drawn", 23 Nov 2026 16:06Z; spec diff https://claude.ai/artifact/UHiw9wAERHT7N9zGcQY31p). Sample screens `clips-scene-card` and `arrange-list-scene-card`.
- **Where** (the Arrange refactor's 5.2, which replaces the Scene tab): a row's number held 0.4 s on the Clips grid, or a step's number on the song list, opens the card for that row's scene over the grid or the list, centred, at most 786 × 494 px; what is under it dims. A tap off the card, Done, a change of view or the row deleted closes it. The Song view has none.
- **The scene:** the held row's; ‹ › beside the name go to the scene before or after (1 to 16), the card moving with them.
- **The title line:** "SCENE · ROW 2 OF 16" with "4 bars · playing" (or "· waiting") at its right; under it ‹ (48 px), the name in a 300 px box with "Rename" at its right ("Row 2" for a plain row), ›. A tap on the name opens the text keyboard (24 characters at most; it offers "Scene 2" to type over), Enter names it, Cancel leaves it.
- **The rows,** in two columns of 44 px rows, a 96 px name and a value box with ‹ and › (40 px) at its ends: Follow, After, Chance, Or and Tempo on the left; Mutes, Program, Bank and Sent to on the right. Follow (off, Next, Previous, First, Last, Any, Other, Again, Stop: what plays when the scene has played its bars; turned on, it starts after the scene's longest clip in whole bars), After (1 to 999 bars, "—" while Follow is off), Chance (0 to 100 % in steps of 10), Or (Stay, then the follow actions: what plays the rest of the time, "—" at 100 %), Mutes ("as they are": the scene leaves the mutes alone; › takes the tracks muted now, "none", "Keys, Bass" or "Keys +2"; ‹ back to as they are), and from sequencer S5 Program (the program change the scene sends the armed track's gear as it starts: off, 1 to 128; "—" with no track armed), Bank (off, then 0/0 to 127/127 as on the clip card, SHIFT + an arrow a whole first number; "—" until Program is set) and Sent to (the tracks the scene sends one, named as on Mutes: "none", "Keys", "Keys, Bass", "Keys +2"; its arrows only say why; sample screen `clips-scene-program`). **Tempo** (5.2b): "as it is", or 30 to 300 BPM to a tenth ("95.0 BPM"); a tap on the value sets the tempo now, or goes back to as it is; ‹ › step 1 BPM, SHIFT 0.1. The tempo comes in on the line the scene's clips start on. A tap on a row's name lights it (accent outline) for the wheel and – / +; a setting at its end, or Or at 100 %, says why on the hint line. A tap on Mutes' value gives the scene the mutes as they are now (the tracks muted now are muted when it starts, the others play).
- **Empty cells** (Keep playing, 5.2): a line "EMPTY CELLS · as the scene starts, a track with no clip in the row stops, or keeps playing", then a 50 px box for each track but the audio tracks, eight across, ‹ › (30 px) at the ends when there are more: the track's colour bar and name, then the row's clip name (dim), Stop, or Keep (filled faintly and outlined in the good colour). A tap on an empty cell's box turns it over; a clip's box does nothing.
- **The foot** (44 px): **Capture**, **Copy row**, **Insert row** and **Delete row** (112 px each, Delete row's word in the bad colour), and **Done** (120 px, accent) at the right. Capture puts the clips playing now as copies in the first empty row under the playing scene; Copy row and Insert row put the new row under this one. Faint when it can't: Capture with nothing playing, Copy row and Insert row with the grid's last row in use. Delete row needs Perform's half-second hold (section 6); in Make it takes one tap and UNDO brings it back.
- **The hint line** while it is open: "‹ ›: change a setting · a setting's name: the wheel turns it · Tempo or Mutes: tap it to take what plays now · Empty cells: tap a track, Stop or Keep · Done or a tap off the card: close".
- **Undo:** each change is a take ("Scene 7 · row 2"); one row turned again is one take, so the wheel through many values is one UNDO; Capture, Copy row, Insert row and Delete row are one take each. While the scene plays, a new name shows at once, new mutes wait for its next start, and a follow change counts from when it started (an After already past moves on at the next bar line).
- **Not built yet:** the scene's colour, Open the scene (its clips on the grid already are), and taking a name off (a new name replaces it).

## 4m. The Sequencer tab

The first step of the Play, Pads and Sequencer redesign (approved by Jay 18 Nov 00:30Z, "Beautiful! Ok, let's build it!"; design doc https://claude.ai/artifact/KHBG7mNbDFU4uZCFqNp9dW, mockups https://claude.ai/artifact/9UD77LP6xGPnMuHPMY9vhr). The tab is after Play (Jay's order, 18 Nov 07:28Z; it was after Pads). It shows the selected track's clip (the one the clip chip picks, else the one playing, else its first) in one of two views, and STEP SEQ opens it. Sample screens `sequencer-steps`, `sequencer-steps-synth`, `sequencer-notes`, `sequencer-notes-list` and `sequencer-notes-tracker`.
- **Steps** is what Play's Beats view was, in Play's frame (section 4): the transport card, the pattern, the Your timing strip, the throws or the step inspector, the master bar and the Q-Link column. The transport card's switch reads **Steps | Notes**, Steps lit. Its wheel moves the outline, as on Play; its Q-Links, macro banks, REC chip and NOTE REPEAT's strip are Play's. The tab arms the notes.
- **The clip chip** (step sequencer refactor C2; the owner's go on C2 and C4, 22 Nov 2026 02:24Z): on Steps, after the Arm pill, the pattern's name is a chip with a ▾ (the XY chip's look), the track's name after it. A tap opens the **Clips** list over the page (as the Lanes list): **What plays** first, then each of the selected track's clips with its slot, "plays", "starts next" and "editing"; a card with Back. A row tapped (or the wheel and its push) makes the Sequencer edit that clip without launching it, and the heading's details then read "Pattern 1 plays". What plays, another track selected or the clip taken away goes back to the clip that plays. Sample screen `sequencer-edit-clips`.
- **The step size chip** (step sequencer refactor D1; the owner's go on phase D, 22 Nov 2026 04:15Z): on Steps, left of the length (**– 16 steps +**), the clip's step size with a ▾ in a raised chip ("1/16 ▾"). A tap opens the **Step size** list over the page (as the Clips list): 1/4, 1/8, 1/8T, 1/16, 1/16T and 1/32, each row with the steps the clip has at that size and " · now" on its own; a card with Back. A row tapped (or the wheel and its push) cuts the clip into steps of that size: the clip keeps its length in time and no note moves (16 sixteenths are 8 eighths), the size is kept with the clip as one UNDO, and STEP SEQ's pages follow it. With no clip the chip is greyed and a tap says "tap a step to start a pattern first". The heading's details drop the size while the chip shows ("1 bar"). Sample screen `sequencer-step-size`.
- **The length a step at a time** (step sequencer refactor D2; the owner's go on phase D, 22 Nov 2026 04:15Z): a tap on the length ("16 steps", between the head's – and +) puts the outline on it, grabbed; the wheel or the – and + buttons then add or take one step (down to one, up to 128), and SHIFT + – or + doubles or halves it as the head's + and – do. The push or a tap elsewhere lets go. The head's – and + keep halving and doubling. Each step is one UNDO. No screen layout changes.
- **Velocity and Chance lanes** (step sequencer refactor D3; the owner's go on phase D, 22 Nov 2026 04:15Z): on a drum track's Steps, the Lanes list (+ Lane) starts with the pad hit last's **Velocity** and **Chance**, each "Kick · the pad hit last · drawn with a finger" with the sound's colour for its dot; the list's card is titled "Add a lane" and gains the line "Velocity and Chance: drag a finger across the hits." ("Velocity and Chance lanes: on a kit's sounds." on a synth's scale rows, where the list has none). A tap gives that sound a lane under it, in the lock lanes' place (after the sound's lock lanes): its name in a dashed box in the theme's outline with the hook from its sound, over the sound's name, and the × in place of the mute. Each step with a note shows a bar in the sound's colour as high as its velocity (1 to 127) or chance (1 to 100 %), the value over it in small text (the top 14 px of the row are kept for it), a faint line along the foot; a step switched off draws faint, a picked step outlined.
