A finger dragged along the lane sets the steps it crosses to the height under it and the bars follow the finger; on the lift the stroke is one UNDO; a second finger calls it off. The wheel with a finger on a step sets it 5 a detent (1 with SHIFT). The × takes the lane away and the steps keep their values (in Perform it is held, as a lock lane's). Synth tracks don't offer them (their scale rows share their lanes). Sample screen `sequencer-value-lanes`.
- **A ratchet's hits in the inspector** (step sequencer refactor D4; the owner's go on phase D, 22 Nov 2026 04:15Z): the step inspector's Ratchet chip draws a bar per hit at its right, first to last, each as tall as the hit plays after the ramp (accent bars on a faint baseline, 5 px wide, 3 px apart); a step of one hit draws none. Nothing moves. Sample screen `sequencer-step-ratchet`.
- **Steps picked together** (step sequencer refactor C4; the owner's go, 22 Nov 2026 02:24Z): on Steps, SHIFT + a tap on a lit step picks it at once (no hold), and further SHIFT + taps on its lane add steps or take one out; the picked steps are outlined as held step pads are, the inspector's heading reads "Steps 1, 3, 5 · Hat · picked", and the inspector and the Q-Links set them all as one undo. SHIFT + a tap on an empty step, or on a lock lane's, keeps its job (a note; a lock), and so does SHIFT + an empty step pad on STEP SEQ (held with no note). A plain tap still puts a step in or takes it out (a picked one leaves the picks), a hold picks one step alone, and the inspector's ×, another page or another clip ends the picks. SHIFT's hint line on Steps starts "tap steps: pick several". No screen layout changes.
- **Notes** is the note editor (the piano roll, the list and the tracker; [note-editor.md](note-editor.md)) on the clip Steps shows: a tap on Notes opens it, as Arrange's Edit row does (which shows the Sequencer). The editor's first button, **‹ Steps**, closes it. It keeps Arrange's transport bar and the Q-Link column. **The Edit options drawer** (piano roll plan 1A.1; the owner's go on the plan's call 2, 22 Nov 2026 05:10Z): the notes take the page's width, and the Edit options and the takes are a 250 px drawer at the right, opened by the editor's **Edit** button (its last, lit while open; it took TC's place, and again closes it), the MPC's TC or the wheel's push. **With it closed** (piano roll plan 1C.1; the owner's go on call 2, 22 Nov 2026 05:10Z) - / + nudge the picked notes a snap (with none picked they open it), SHIFT + - / + zoom the roll in time, and the wheel moves the **cursor**, a 2 px line across the roll in the theme's second accent with a mark on the ruler (on the tracker its step outlined; on the list the wheel opens the drawer). In the drawer the wheel picks an Edit option and its push opens it; its first row is a **Correct picked** (**Correct all**) button, and TC pressed with it open corrects. SHIFT + wheel scrolls the roll's rows, or the list. **The roll** (1A.1): 40 px rows to begin with (24 to 96, the plan's call 7); a 32 px **overview** over the ruler shows the whole clip with the window outlined, **Fit** at its left and **Follow** at its right; two fingers zoom round the point between them (across: time, up and down: pitch, a slant: both, picked by the first move) and scroll, and one finger on the ruler or the keys scrolls. The snap button's first setting is **Auto**, which follows the zoom and names its grid ("Auto · 1/16"). **Draw** (piano roll plan 1B.1; the owner's go on call 2, 22 Nov 2026 05:10Z): the third button is one Draw switch, lit while on, in place of the Select, Draw and Erase cycle; with it on the roll has the step feel (a tap puts a note in or takes a short one out, a drag draws), with it off a tap picks; ERASE held + a tap erases. **Select** (piano roll plan 1B.3; the owner's go on call 2, 22 Nov 2026 05:10Z): the fifth button, in place of All/None, opens the Select menu over the roll, two columns of 40 px rows 210 px wide above the button: All, None, Invert, Add to pick (a switch, lit with the button while on) and Fit to picked, then the filters (Same pitch, Pitch range, Time range, Quiet notes, Loud notes, With a condition, With repeats, Overlapping, Off the scale); an item does its job and closes the menu (Add to pick keeps it open), and a touch anywhere else closes it. The tracker's and the list's rows are 40 px. **The note inspector** (piano roll plan 1C.1; the owner's go on call 2, 22 Nov 2026 05:10Z): while notes are picked, the Q-Link column has a 44 px header in the theme's second accent, "Note edit" over the count ("3 notes"), and its four cards, in the same colour, are **Position**, **Length**, **Semitones** and **Velocity**, each with its value or range ("80–104"); a turn from the first knob touched to the last let go is one UNDO. Q-LINK's macro banks go over it, as on Play. **The lane under the roll** (piano roll plan 2A.1): 64 px to begin with, its chip at the top of the key column beside it naming the lane (**Vel**, **Chance**, **Repeats**, **Pressure** or a lock lane's setting; a tap steps to the next) and a ▾ under the chip that folds the lane to a 26 px strip (the chip, with a caret up, opens it again); a short grip on its top edge drags it from 48 to 200 px, keeping the roll four rows. A bar in the track's colour stands at each place where notes start, the value over it; a finger drawn along the lane sets each bar it passes to the height under it, shown as it goes, one UNDO on the lift, a second finger calling it off; SHIFT held as the finger goes down on Repeats draws the ramp from a middle line. **Pressure** (piano roll plan 3A.1; the owner's go on the plan, 22 Nov 2026 05:10Z) shows the picked notes' pressure as a line in the track's colour over a light fill along them, in 4 px columns; a finger drawn along it redraws the line under it, one UNDO on the lift; with nothing picked it says "Pick notes to draw their pressure". **Lock lanes** (piano roll plan 3A.2; the owner's go on the plan, 22 Nov 2026 05:10Z) follow Pressure, one for each of the Steps' lock lanes on the clip, the chip naming the setting ("Cutoff"): a bar in the track's colour at each place a lock sits for the notes in view (a step and a row, as the Steps keep them), as high as the lock among the setting's values (from a middle line for one either side of 0), faint at what the step plays where it has none, outlined while a note there is picked; a stroke locks each bar it passes, one UNDO, and ERASE + a tap takes a bar's lock off. A line in the lane's top right, 11 px in the dim text colour, says the picked bar's value, or the one under the finger, and "shared by 3 notes" when notes share the lock, or "not locked"; with no notes in view that play the setting the lane says "No notes in view use this setting". **Marks on notes** (3A.2): a note with a lock where it plays has a 6 px square 3 px in from its top right corner, and one with a condition or a chance under 100 % a 6 px ring 9 px left of that, in the ink that reads on the note (on a note at least 20 px wide and 12 px tall); on a note 48 px wide or more a tap in its top right 20 px opens the lane its mark is on and picks the note. Which lane, its height and the fold stay for every clip. **The clip's scale** (piano roll plan 2B.1; the owner's go on the plan's call 10, 22 Nov 2026 05:10Z): the roll's 34 px head band has three chips at its right, the legend moving left of them: **Scale** (200 px, "Scale: D Dorian", lit in the track's colour while the clip has one; none on a clip of pad hits, plan 4.2), **Fold** (150 px: All rows, Rows with notes, Scale rows; lit while not All) and, while Fold hides notes, "2 notes hidden" (140 px, in the warning colour; a tap shows every row). The Scale chip opens the **Scale menu** over the roll, from the key column to the roll's right edge: the 12 roots in a row of 34 px buttons, then the scales six or more to a row (Off, the pads' scale, the built-in scales, the owner's), the clip's lit; a touch anywhere else closes it. With a scale, its rows are lit in the track's colour, root rows more, with a mark in the keys, and a note off the scale has a warning-coloured dot; a bold line marks where Fold skips rows. Drags, Q3 (its card then **Scale steps**) and drawn notes keep to the scale; SHIFT gives semitones. The Edit options end with **Fit to scale**. **Chords** (piano roll plan 2B.2; the owner's go on the plan, 22 Nov 2026 05:10Z): a **Chord** chip (190 px) left of Scale (none on a clip of pad hits), "Chord: Off", "Chord: Triad" or with no scale "Chord: Minor triad", lit in the track's colour while on, opens the **Chord menu** over the roll: one row of 40 px buttons, Major and Minor first while the clip has no scale, then Off, Triad, Seventh, Sus2, Sus4, Power, Octave and Pads (while the pads have a scale), the ones in use lit; a shape closes it, a touch elsewhere closes it. A note drawn then puts in the chord, previewed whole while drawn and picked after. The note inspector's header names the picks' chord after the count ("3 notes · Dm7"). **Tools** (piano roll plan 2B.3; the owner's go on the plan, 22 Nov 2026 05:10Z): the Edit options' last rows are **Strum**, **Invert**, **Spread**, **Chop**, **Legato** and **Humanize**; one opens its tool on the picks, the roll showing the result, and while it is open a **tool bar** takes the buttons' place: **Cancel** on the first button, the tool's own button over the next two (Chop's **Reset pressure**, lit while on; Humanize's **Again**), the tool's name in bold over a line saying what it shows, and **Done**, lit in the accent, on the last; the Q-Link column's cards are the tool's settings under "Chop · 2 notes", and the notes, their handles and the lane do nothing until Done or Cancel (the view still moves). **Continue** (composing tools, phase 1: the doc "Composing tools", https://claude.ai/artifact/HjU5wKNXdHtWffH9HeaEZb, Jay's "Yes, Continue first" of 22 Nov 2026 11:15Z; sample screen `sequencer-notes-continue`; approved as drawn, Jay's card, 22 Nov 2026 21:26Z): the Edit options' last row is **Continue**, which opens on the whole clip (not on a clip of pad hits, nor on one with no notes playing). Its tool bar has **Cancel** (the clip as it was, from the next bar line), **Try again** over the next two buttons and **Done** (the try kept as one take, one UNDO). While it is open the drawer is titled **Continue** and its rows are Continue's settings: Style, Character (S5, below), Bars (+1 to +16 bars), Ending (Home, Open, Loop), Rhythm, Shape, Chords and Accents (yours or free), each step asking for another try. The takes area under them is **Tries**: the last 8, newest first ("Try 2 · Minimal" over "+4 bars", the one playing lit), with **‹** and **›** in Undo's and Redo's places and no Capture. The Q-Link cards are **Similarity**, **Variation**, **Activity** ("As yours" at its middle) and **Range** (Narrow, Medium, Wide) under "Continue · +4 bars". The roll shows the clip as it plays with the try: a 2 px line in the track's colour where the new bars start, the new notes faint after it (the track's colour at 30 %, their edges at 60 %), each new bar's job ("loop", "vary", "land home") after its number on the ruler where the bar is 72 px wide or more, and in the lane's place **Chords**: a box for each chord, solid where you played it, dashed where Continue suggests one. The tool bar's line says what is heard ("Try 2 of 2 plays in place of the clip: Done keeps it", "Making the new bars", "Try 3 of 3 comes in at bar 5"). **Picks and locks** (composing tools, phase 1 step 3; sample screen `sequencer-notes-continue-picks`; approved as drawn, Jay's card, 22 Nov 2026 22:38Z): while Continue is open a tap on a new bar's place on the ruler picks it, or lets it go, lighting the ruler under its number and job (the background mixed 35 % toward the accent, 2 px in from the bar lines) and washing the roll over the bar (the accent at 10 %); a tap on a new note picks it, or lets it go, outlined as a picked note is; bars or notes, never both, and your own bars and notes pick nothing. The tool bar's middle button names what it tries: **Try again**, **Try bar 6 again**, **Try 2 bars again**, **Try 3 notes again**. The drawer's button (Correct's) is **Lock bar 6** or **Lock 2 bars** with bars picked, **Unlock bar 4** when they all are locked, **Lock picked bars** with none; a locked bar has a 10 px lock and "locked" in place of its job on the ruler, also where the bar is too narrow for jobs. A try made of part of the one before says what it changed in the Tries list ("bar 6", "2 bars", "3 notes") where a whole try says "+4 bars". The hint line's middle part is "Tap new bars or notes to pick them". **Character and aimed tries** (composing tools S5, boards A and B, https://claude.ai/artifact/754EQJS9tzBxYmaMHEMZuA, pictures in mpfree-claude `composing-tools/project-files/s5-s6-boards/`; approved as drawn, Jay's card, 24 Nov 2026 14:52Z; sample screens `sequencer-notes-continue-character` and `sequencer-notes-continue-aim`): the drawer's second row is **Character** (As style, Restrained, Yearning, Playful, Tense, Urgent; "As style" since "As the style" is wider than a row's value); while it is picked and not As style, the tool bar's title reads "Continue · Yearning" and its line what that does. The tool bar has a **▾** (46 px, outlined) right of Try again: it opens a menu over the roll's foot, from the roll's left, "TRY AGAIN, AIMED" over a 46 px row for each of Busier, Calmer, Land it, Less bright and Brighter with what it does in the dim text; a row makes that try and closes the menu, a touch anywhere else closes it. With new notes picked the ▾ is faint. An aimed try is named for its aim in Tries ("Try 3 · calmer"). **Pads write and Sound** (piano roll plan 1C.2; the owner's go on the plan, 22 Nov 2026 05:10Z; their place approved as drawn, Jay's card, 22 Nov 2026 19:50Z: the plan put them in the heading, which has no room for them beside Scale, Fold and Chord while the drawer is open): on the roll two chips on the overview row, left of **Follow**, the overview taking the rest: **Pads write** (104 px, lit in the REC colour while on; off for every clip opened) and **Sound** (156 px: "Sound: On", "Sound: Stopped only", "Sound: Off", tinted toward the warning colour while not On; it stays for every clip). On the list and the tracker they sit at the right end of the heading, the legend moving left of them. With Pads write on a pad writes its notes at the cursor, a snap long, and the cursor steps a snap when the last held pad lets go; with it off a pad on a clip of pad hits picks that pad's notes. A note picked, drawn or moved is heard through the track's instrument for its length, up to a second. **Show** (piano roll plan 4.1; the owner's go on the plan, 22 Nov 2026 05:10Z; its place approved as drawn, Jay's card, 22 Nov 2026 19:50Z, as Pads write's): on the roll a third chip on the overview row, left of **Pads write** (104 px: "Show", or "Show: 2" lit while other clips show), the overview taking the rest; not on the list or the tracker, which have no time line to put other clips on. A tap opens the **Show menu** over the roll, under the overview row: **None** and a line saying what shows, then the other clips of the same kind three to a row, each named with its track and **Plays with** or **Reference**, lit in its track's colour while it shows, **More** when they don't all fit. The clips shown (three at most) are drawn faintly under the clip's own notes, the track's colour at 30 % on the background, with a label for each at the roll's top right. **Drum clips** (piano roll plan 4.2; the owner's go on the plan, 22 Nov 2026 05:10Z, which hides Scale and Chord there): a clip of pad hits has a row for each of the kit's 128 pads in place of the keys, A1 at the bottom, each named in the key column by its place and the kit's name for it ("A1 Kick", read from the left; the key column is 112 px on pad rows, not 64, for the names, approved as drawn, Jay's card, 22 Nov 2026 19:50Z), a line under each bank's first pad as under each C; the head band has Fold alone (no Scale or Chord chip). PAD BANK scrolls the roll to the bank's first pad, the zoom kept, as the pads go to the bank. The editor stays open on other tabs and shows again when the Sequencer does; Arrange shows its grid meanwhile. **The clip's end on the ruler** (piano roll plan 1A.2; Jay's yes, 23 Nov 2026 23:28Z): the ruler carries a grip at the clip's end: drag it to change the clip's length by bars or steps, one take.
- **Play has one view,** All (the looper redesign's step 5), so MAIN on Play has nothing to step through.
- **Lock lanes** (step 5, board 3), on a drum track's Steps: a lane for every setting the clip locks, and for each added with **+ Lane** (on the pattern's head, left of the page's ‹), under its sound; the track's (volume, pan, sends) after all the sounds. A lane's name sits in a dashed purple box with a hook from its sound, over "lock · Kick" ("lock · track FX", "lock · track mix"); its × stands where a sound's speaker is and takes the lane off with its locks (Perform's guard holds it). Each locked step is a purple bar as high as its value, the value over it. A tap on a lane's step locks it at the setting's own value or takes the lock off; a drag up or down turns it (SHIFT fine), and so does the wheel while a finger holds it; ERASE + a tap takes one lock. With more rows than fit the rows swipe, with Play's rail at the right. + Lane opens the **Lanes** list over the page (as the list of kits, section 2): the settings of the pad hit last (tune, decay, filter, level, and slice on a sliced pad), then the track's volume, pan and sends A to D, with a card and its Back. Sample screens `sequencer-lock-lanes` and `sequencer-lanes-list`. Board 3 drew only lanes added by hand; every locked setting getting a lane is the build's choice, so a lock made in the inspector or recorded live shows.
- **Synth and gear lanes** (step 5b, board 4): on a synth track's scale rows the lock lanes go under the notes, as many as leave three notes, the rest a swipe away; + Lane lists the synth's first Q-Link set (and each synth setting already locked), then the track's. A track that plays outside gear (a Tracks out in the I/O view's MIDI section) has only the gear's controls: the CCs its device profile names, else the ones most gear shares (Mod wheel, Volume, Pan, Cutoff on CC 74 ...; never the bank select, data entry or RPN numbers), then Bend and Pressure, each row "Mono synth · CC 43". The lane's name is the control's, over "CC 43" ("pitch bend", "pressure"). The Lanes card gets a second button, **Another CC**, which opens the number pad (0 to 119) for a CC the list doesn't name; the card is 440 px wide so both buttons fit. On the Lanes list the wheel moves the row picked and its push adds that lane. Sample screens `sequencer-gear-lanes` and `sequencer-gear-lanes-list`.
- **An LFO on a gear lane** (LFO to CC, the MIDI powerhouse plan): on a track that plays outside gear, a tap on a CC, Bend or Pressure lane's name opens that control's **mod rows** over the pattern, drawn as the chain editor's (section 4b: a 440 px panel, 44 px rows). It shows each LFO or follower that moves the control, with its depth bar (drag it) and its ×, and **+ LFO**, which adds LFO 1 at 25 %. A touch anywhere else closes them. While something moves the control, a pink **~** sits right of the lane's name, in a different look from the pad-type tag under it. On a locked step the LFO sweeps round the lock's value. A sound's lanes (drum and synth tracks) keep their name as a label, with only the × answering. The gear gets the control only when its value changes (7-bit for a CC), and on the 5-pin jacks no sooner than 4 ms after the last. A value that comes too soon waits, and the newest goes. Sample screen `sequencer-gear-lane-lfo`.
- **Still to come** in the redesign: pad types and the Perform set (step 6). Play's rows came in step 3, a step switched off but kept with the inspector's On | Off, ramp and Tie / Slide in step 3b, lock lanes in step 5 and synth and gear lanes in step 5b.

## 4n. The conversion report

The Arrange refactor's 4.3 (Jay's go, "Yes, as drawn", 22 Nov 2026 12:48Z; spec diff https://claude.ai/artifact/MEABy6P2zMw2bHowtrwMJb). After Scenes → song, List → song or Song → scenes. Sample screens `arrange-report`, `arrange-report-list`, `arrange-report-exact` and `arrange-report-scrolls`.
- **Exact:** an exact one opens nothing: what it did shows in place of the hint, as ERASE's line does ("Song → scenes: rows 9 to 11, exact. UNDO takes it back."). So does why one couldn't be made ("Song → scenes: the song is empty").
- **With differences:** the report card opens over the Song card (the Song view or the song list), 786 px wide and as tall as its rows need (at most 498 px), in the middle of it, with the Song card dimmed behind it. The transport bar, the right panel, the morph bar and the Q-Links stay as they are.
- **Its title line** has the conversion in capitals ("SONG → SCENES") and, at its right, what it made and how many differences there are ("rows 9 to 11 · 4 differences"). Under it is one line in plain words: "Launch row 9 and the rows play the song, except:", or "The song plays the rows, except:" or "The song plays the list, except:".
- **The differences** come in song order under the column names Track, Bar and What's different, one row each (48 px, 6 px apart). Each row has the track's colour as a bar at its left and its name in that colour ("Song", in the text colour, for one that is the whole song's), the bar in the mono font ("bar 5"), and what is different in the words the log uses. Five rows show. More scroll with a drag or the wheel, with "1 to 5 of 9" under them. The rows don't answer a tap.
- **Its buttons:** at the card's bottom right, UNDO (154 px) takes the whole conversion back, as UNDO does, and closes the card. Done (150 px, the accent) closes it and keeps the result; it is not called Keep because the morph bar under the song has a Keep. To their left: "UNDO takes the whole conversion back". The MPC's UNDO does what the card's does.
- **Closing it:** a tap outside the card, MENU or a page change closes it and keeps the result; that tap does nothing else. A closed report doesn't come back (the log in Dev keeps its lines). The hint line while it is open: "Done: close it and keep the rows · UNDO, here or on the MPC: take it all back · a tap outside or a page change keeps it" ("keep the song" after Scenes → song or List → song); while the rows scroll, "Done: close it and keep the rows · UNDO: take it all back · drag the list or turn the wheel: the rest · a tap outside keeps it".

## 4o. The XY page, Setup and the waiting banner

The XY controller plan (`mpfree-claude` `xy-controller/plan.md`): Jay's approval of the XY mockups, 23 Nov 2026 10:34Z ("Approve, start phase 2"; canvas https://claude.ai/artifact/X2A8nsHiwCf6S6PuZQDq4n, pictures in mpfree-claude `xy-controller/mockups/`). Ten boards; names and values in them are examples. Built in two steps: the XY plan's phase 2 builds your layouts, Setup, the banner, the XY chips and knob marks (section 4b), Learn's XY row (section 4d), and Hold, Let go and Setup under today's full-screen pad (section 6); phase 4 builds the rest of the XY page (the layouts column, the line saying where moves go, Loop a move, Lock, the edge lists and its Q-Links). Phase 3 (recording) fills the line and the recording and playback looks.

- **Your layouts** (board 1, section 4): a layout says what across and up move and how the pad behaves. Each direction has a name (typed, as section 1's names; 16 characters) and up to 8 settings. A setting sits on one direction only: picking it for the other moves it there, and the hint line says so. A ready-made layout changed in Setup becomes a new layout of yours ("Wash 2"), and the ready-made one stays as it was.
- **The XY page** (board 2; phase 4), in the view under the transport bar, beside the master bar and the Q-Links:
  - **the layouts column** at the left (164 px): YOUR LAYOUTS, a 52 px row each (its name, and what it plays: "Bass · 4 settings", "follows the track", "Master · Hold"; the one playing lit, with the accent bar at its left), **+ New layout** (dashed), then READY-MADE with the two last used and **All layouts ▾**, which opens the XY pad's list. A tap switches layouts: the old one lets go of everything it holds before the new one takes anything.
  - **the line above the pad** (36 px), saying where moves will go before you touch the pad (boards 7 and 7b): "REC → Bass clip: Cutoff, Drive, Send A · SONG → Room mix (Master)", each part after a tick in its colour; with Rec song off the song's part reads, faint, "Not recorded: Room mix (Master) · Rec song is off"; a synth two tracks play says "(shared with Track 3)" after its setting; with only Rec song on it reads "REC SONG · SONG → Cutoff, Drive, Send A, Room mix: all of it goes into the song".
  - **the pad**, as big as the view allows, with the four quarters' lines. Its edges are named by chips, "↑ UP · Wet" at the top left and "ACROSS → Bite" at the bottom left, each with a faint line of its settings and their values now ("Send A (Bass) 28 % · Room mix (Master) 31 %"). A tap on a chip opens the list of what that direction moves (board 9: "UP · WET MOVES", a row each with its colour, its name and where it is, its value now, and Set or Add) over the pad; a tap anywhere else closes it. The dot shows only a real place: a finger, Hold, a glide back or a looped move. While nothing holds the pad there is no dot, and each setting's value (a recorded one, while moves play) is a mark on its edge in its colour (board 8). While REC records, the dot's trail is red (board 7).
  - **under the pad** a row of 44 px buttons: **Hold** (lit while on), **Let go: Go back** or **Let go: Stay** (a tap switches it), **Loop a move**, **Lock: off** (a tap steps off, across, up: the pad moves only that way) and **Setup**. While a loop plays (board 9), Loop a move, Lock and Setup give way to **Loop: 2 bars** (lit; the length is picked, 1, 2 or 4 bars, before it starts), **Print motion** and **Stop**, and the line above the pad reads "LOOP · 2 BARS  A loop never records itself. Print motion turns it into moves: Bass clip + song"; the loop's path is drawn in the second accent, with a ring riding it and where it is ("bar 1, beat 3") beside the ring.
  - **the Q-Links**: Q1 across and Q2 up (fine moves), Q3 the glide back, Q4 the XY FX's mix on an XY FX layout, else free.
  - **the hint line**: "Drag: play the layout · a layout: switch · UP or ACROSS: its settings · Q1, Q2: fine moves · SHIFT + NEXT SEQ: back"; while a loop plays, "Touch the pad: you take over, let go: the loop carries on · Print motion → Bass clip + song, one UNDO step · Stop: end the loop".
  - The pads keep playing, so one hand drums while the other plays the pad.
- **Setup** (board 3) opens from **Setup** under the pad and takes the pad's place; Done gives the pad back. On the XY page the layouts column stays beside it; on today's full-screen pad (phase 2) it takes the whole view.
  - **Its head:** SETUP, the layout's name (typed; 24 characters), then **Save to browser**, **Delete** and **Done** (the accent) at the right. Delete takes the layout out of this song (Perform's hold applies); if it was playing, the pad lets go of everything and plays Filter + echo. A ready-made layout shows neither Save to browser nor Delete.
  - **Each direction:** "ACROSS →" or "UP ↑", its name (typed), "2 of 8", and **+ across** or **+ up** at the right (greyed at 8 of 8), which raises the banner (below).
  - **A row per setting** (48 px, 6 px apart): a dot in its colour (its track's, or the master's), its name and where it is ("Bass · Poly · Filter", "Bass strip · Drive, effect 2", "Master · Room, effect 1"), its from value, a range bar with two handles (from and to, each dragged; the bar in its colour), its to value, "runs backwards" under the bar when from is above to, its curve (a chip with ▾ opening Even, Slow start, Fast start, S-shaped), **Set | Add**, and × to take it off. A tap picks a row (outlined in the accent), and Q1 From, Q2 To and Q3 Curve turn it; Q4 is the glide back. Add is greyed on a synth's or a pad's own settings, and a tap on it says why on the hint line, until the mod engine reaches them. The rows scroll with a drag or the wheel when they don't fit.
  - **A setting that went away** (an effect taken out, a track removed, an effect switched to a mode without it) stays struck out: a faint dot, its old name and place ("Track 7 · Poly"), the reason in warn ("Track 7 was removed.") and "It does nothing until you take it off or pick another.", with **Take off**. It is never re-bound to whatever took its place.
  - **At the foot, a card:** When you let go: **Go back | Stay**; Glide back: – 120 ms + (0 to 2 s, the wheel and Q4 too); A touch: **Jumps | Moves from here**; Follow the selected track: **Off | On**; then two faint lines: "Stay: Set settings stay where you lift (one UNDO step; never while recording). Add settings always glide back. Hold keeps everything." and "Add is greyed on a synth's or a pad's own settings until the mod engine reaches them."
  - **The hint line:** "Tap a setting: Q1 to Q3 set its range and curve · + across, + up: then SHIFT + touch settings on any page · Done: back to the pad".
- **The waiting banner** (board 4): after **+ across** or **+ up**, a 48 px banner in the hint line's place (section 1), outlined in the second accent, stays on every page until Done or Cancel: an **XY · ACROSS** (or XY · UP) chip in the second accent, "Bite on Squelch waits: hold SHIFT and touch settings on any page", and under it, faint, what just happened ("Just added: Cutoff (Bass) moves across, 0 to 100 % · 2 on across · Done keeps them, Cancel puts Squelch back"); **Cancel** and **Done** (the second accent) at its right. Each SHIFT + touch on a setting (on the Synth tab, the chain editor, the mixer or the Pads tab; the setting MIDI Learn would take) joins that direction at once, over its whole range, Even, Set, so SHIFT + NEXT SEQ to the pad tries it and back for more. One already on the other direction moves, and the line says so; one the pad can't move (a switch), or a direction already at 8, is refused and the line says why. Done keeps them; Cancel puts the layout back exactly as it was. While the banner is up, SHIFT + touch picks for it and not for MIDI Learn; the rest of the time SHIFT + touch is MIDI Learn, as before.
- **Hold:** while lit, letting go leaves the pad in charge of its settings until Hold is off, which then does the layout's Go back or Stay. A lost touch, a controller unplugged, a project opened, the layout switched or deleted lets go of everything, even with Hold on; STOP while stopped turns Hold off.

## 4p. Sets: the Show view and the Set editor

**Approved by Jay on 22 Nov 2026** (08:11Z, "Approve as drawn" on the live shows thread's screens, https://claude.ai/artifact/NBvvDrBnjzCp5pNGyREaYB; the plan approved 06:21Z, https://claude.ai/artifact/PBWnfRy2bfrJwhgS1eVjf6). Stage B1 of the live shows plan ([design](live-shows.md)). A set is a list of songs in order, each one a project in Projects. It's kept in the library as `Sets/<name>.set` and finds each song by its lasting id, so a song that's renamed or moved stays in the set. Sample screens: `arrange-show`, `browser-sets`, `browser-set-song`, `browser-sets-check`, `browser-sets-soundcheck`, `mixer-io-learn-show`.

**Stage B2 approved by Jay on 23 Nov 2026** (19:25Z, "Approve as drawn" on the live shows thread's B2 screens, https://claude.ai/artifact/Mm8JcDSNuHuSGWEj33N1u4; the mockup's source is mpfree-claude `live-shows/mockups/b2-set-check.html`): the set check as a full preflight, Soundcheck and Prepare show media, in the lines marked B2 below. The check view is built (B2a, with its sample screen `browser-sets-check`), and so is Soundcheck (B2b, with `browser-sets-soundcheck`). Prepare show media (B2c) follows in its own pull request; until it lands, the set's card has seven buttons (Play set, Edit set, Check set, Soundcheck, Rename, Copy, Delete) and no Show media row.

- **Where:** the Show view is Arrange's fourth view, shown only while a set is open. In Perform, MAIN goes to it from any page, and on Arrange MAIN goes Show, Clips, Song, List and round. While a set is open, the Clips grid's, the Song view's and the song list's title lines gain **Show** (72 px, left of their other buttons, Perform's Capture scene among them). Where the Clips grid shows Show, Back to song and the pager together and the view chip leaves no room for all three (the Clips view beside the Record panel, more than eight tracks), Back to song narrows to 100 px and the pager's label to 92 px (it reads 17–24 of 24), so Show clears the chip; built that way when Arrange's view chip joined the title line, a detail for Jay to confirm. Play set opens it and switches to Perform. With no set open, nothing changes.
- **Its frame:** the top bar, Arrange's transport bar, then the view in the place of the grid and the Record panel (1116 px), the Q-Link column at the right as on the grid, and the hint line. There's no Record panel and no Takes.
- **The set column** (280 px, at the left): the set's name as its title, "12 songs · 54 min" under it, then a 48 px row for each song (6 px apart). Each row has the song's number and name and, at its right, what it is now: NOW (filled green and outlined: the song playing), NEXT with its change's state (outlined amber), "played" (faint), or a dot for its last check (amber: Ready with limits; red: Not ready). A long set scrolls to keep NOW and NEXT in view. Tapping a song arms it. Tapping NOW or NEXT does nothing, so a double tap never goes. **Back to the set order** (46 px, at the column's foot) arms the song after NOW in the set's order. It's faint while that song is already the one armed.
- **The title line:** "NOW · SONG 4 OF 12", and Clips, Song and List at the right (72 px each).
- **The song band:** the song's name (40 px) and, at its right, its state as a 160 × 44 px pill. The states are PLAYING (green), LOADED (blue, with "press PLAY" under it, or the count-in while it counts), FINISHING with the seconds left (amber), SAVING TAKES (purple), OPENING (blue) and NOTHING PLAYING (red, after an open fails). Under the name: the tempo, the part and the time left in the song list ("92 BPM · part 4 of 7 · 1:52 left"), and "autosave waiting" in amber while autosave is held back.
- **The parts:** a strip of tiles (108 × 76 px, 6 px apart), one for each step of the song's song list. Each tile has its scene's name, "× 4 · 16 bars" and its scene's colour as a stripe. NOW is filled green with "pass 3 of 4" and a bar filling across the step, NEXT is outlined amber, and steps already played are faint. The strip scrolls to keep NOW in view. A song with no song list shows one dashed tile: "No parts: a song list on Arrange gives this song its parts". The tiles are for showing only. Part controls come with E2.
- **The next-song card** (404 px, under the parts at the left): "NEXT SONG", the song's number and name (28 px), its state as a large word with what that means, a "Go:" line saying what Go will do, and two 56 px buttons: **Go** (green fill, 180 px) and **Cancel next** (180 px). The states in B1 are: not armed ("NEXT IN THE SET" over the next song in order, faint, with "Press Next song to arm it", and both buttons faint); COLD LOAD, with the pause ("about 4 s" from the last soundcheck, or "pause not measured yet"); FINISHING (Go reads Go now); "saving takes, then opening"; OPENING, with how far it has got; BLOCKED, in red, with why ("the file samples/strings.wav is missing"), and Go faint; LAST SONG, while the set's last song plays. D1 adds PREPARING with how far it has got, GOES WHEN READY and READY. Once a change has started, its presses are ignored, except Go during FINISHING. If an open fails after the song before has gone, the card turns red: it says what failed and that every take and change is kept, and **Try again** and **Reopen** (with the song's name) take the place of Go and Cancel next.
- **The notes card** (right of the next-song card): "NOTES · " and the song's name, then its notes from the set at 20 px, as many lines as fit. Anything past that ends with "…".
- **Your keyboards play** (under the next-song card): a 28 px line for each keyboard the rig knows, with its name and what it plays ("Rhodes · track 3"). A keyboard that isn't connected reads "not connected", dim, or red when this song needs it. With patches (live shows C1, approved with the patch screens 23 Nov 2026 10:32Z), a connected keyboard's line reads its name and the patch its keys reach ("KeyLab 61 · Chorus split", "Keys as routed" while none is picked), with a 10 px strip of that patch's zones at its right (at most 190 px, a third of the line): only the zones that reach that keyboard, so a zone set to one keyboard shows on that keyboard's line alone. Without patches it reads "connected".
- **Click** (under the notes card): Click on or off, a 120 × 44 px switch, the same setting as SHIFT + PLAY START, with the song's count-in beside it. E1 adds the stem groups' mutes beside it.
- **The MPC's controls:** the wheel and – / + arm the song after or before the one armed (starting from NOW when none is armed). While a song is armed, the wheel's push is Go. PLAY starts a LOADED song, and STOP stops, as everywhere. TRACK MUTE, the pads and the Q-Links work as they do on Arrange.
- **MENU** on the Show view: Open the Set editor, Check the set, and Close the set (the song playing stays open as a plain project, and the Show view goes).
- **Learned controls** (the Learn section's Show card, 4d) act from any page while a set is open. A press counts once: it acts on the press only, a second press within 50 ms is the same press, and presses while a change is under way are ignored. With no set open they do nothing, and the hint line says so.
- **The Sets place** (in the browser, after Projects; the places column's rows close up to fit it, section 3): a row for each set, with its name, how many songs (and, once songs' lengths are read, how long) and its last check as a chip at its right (READY, LIMITS, NOT READY or NOT CHECKED: its worst song; a set that can't be read says so in red). The list's title line has **New set**, which asks for a name on the browser's keyboard and opens the empty set. The list has no Search or order chips: sets are few, in the natural order of their names.
- **A set picked** (changed by B2): the Selected column's rows are Songs, Checked (the time, then "7 ready, 1 with limits, 1 not ready"), Soundcheck (the time and "all 9 songs", or "8 of 9 songs"; "not yet") and Show media ("on the stick · 2.4 GB"; "not prepared: songs play from where they are"; "older than a song" in amber), then the box of what isn't ready (each song that isn't ready and each with limits, with why: "5 Glass harbour: the file samples/strings.wav is missing."), then eight buttons two across: **Play set** (accent fill), **Edit set**, **Check set**, **Soundcheck**, **Prepare show media**, **Rename**, **Copy**, **Delete**. Check every file moves to the check view. A second tap or the wheel's push is Edit set. Delete asks first: the set goes to the Trash and its songs stay in Projects. Copy puts a copy beside it ("Friday 2").
- **Editing a set:** the list's title line reads "‹ " and the set's name (‹ goes back to Sets), with **Add songs** and **Check set** at the right. Then there's a 50 px row for each song: its number, its name, how it starts and ends ("Stopped · Let it finish") and its check chip. Add songs shows Projects with a + at each row's right (the title reads "‹ Add songs to Friday", with **Done**; the Selected column lists the set's songs and says where + puts the next). A tap on + puts that song after the one picked (or at the end), the wheel's push or + adds the project picked, and a song can be in a set more than once; – or Done goes back. – / + move the picked song up or down. ERASE is Remove from set. The set saves itself after each change, written to a new file and renamed into place.
- **A song picked in a set:** the Selected column shows "SONG 4 OF 12", the song's name, "Projects", its tempo and its check line ("92 BPM · Ready, checked …"), and why it isn't ready, if it isn't, then the rows Starts (Stopped, Playing, Count-in then play), Ends (Let it finish, Fade, Cut), Fade (1 to 30 s, or "—" unless Ends is Fade) and Needs (the rig's keyboards and learned controls this song needs; a tap lists them in the Selected column, each with whether it is connected, and a tap on one goes Needed, Nice to have, not needed; Done goes back). A value's ‹ and › halves step it; a tap on it, or the wheel's push (Starts, Ends, Fade, then none), gives it to the wheel, outlined. Then **Notes**, a three-line box that opens the keyboard when tapped, with "Tap to type · 132 of 240 characters" under it, then **Move up**, **Move down** (faint at the ends), **Open in Make** (opens the song as a plain project, outside the set) and **Remove from set** (a second tap removes: it reads "Tap again to remove").
- **Check set** runs the check on every song: every part reads, every file is there and unchanged, the keyboards it needs are connected, and (B2) its plugins, outputs, memory, the stick's room and its streams. Each song keeps its quick check's result with the time in the set, so the chips show after a restart. A song that was renamed or moved takes its new name.
- **The check view** (new, B2): Check set (the set's card, a set's songs' title line, and Check the set in the Show view's MENU, which opens the browser on it while the set plays on) runs the check and opens it in the list column. Its title line reads "‹ Check · " and the set's name, with **Check every file** (150 px) at its right. Under it a line: the set's chip, its counts, when it was checked (quick, or every file) and when it was soundchecked. Then a 58 px row for each song: its number, its name and its chip (READY, LIMITS, NOT READY), and under the name its numbers: "opens in 3.1 s · 540 MB · CPU 71 % in Chorus 2" as Soundcheck measured them, or "about 5 s · about 460 MB · not soundchecked yet". The picked row is outlined as in the set's songs.
- **A song picked in the check view** (new, B2): the Selected column shows "SONG 5 OF 9", the name and its chip, its tempo and when it was checked, then a line for each check with a mark (✓ green, ! amber, × red, i blue for a note) and what it found: Files (every sound, take and stem there and unchanged, and the show media copies if prepared), Parts (every part reads), Plugins (each plugin and preset installed), Keyboards (the keyboards and controls its Needs lists, connected), Outputs (its outputs and click routing exist on this MPC), Memory (its peak with the song before it, that song's takes still writing, and the song after it, against what's free; a pair over it is a note saying that change is always cold), Stick (room for takes and recovery copies), Streams (its streams fit the stream cache) and Soundcheck (what it measured, or "not soundchecked yet"). Then **Soundcheck this song** and **Open in Make**. A missing file, plugin or output, or a song too big on its own, is always NOT READY. A keyboard not connected, a glitch in soundcheck, or a file changed since its show media copy is LIMITS.
- **Soundcheck** (new, B2): asks first, in the Selected column: what it does and about how long ("opens each of the 9 songs in turn and plays its busiest part, then its change into the next: about 6 minutes"), Sound: **Out loud** (default) or **Muted** (measures the same; nothing leaves the outputs), a line when the song open now has changes ("Night drive has changes: they're saved first"), then **Start soundcheck** (accent) and **Cancel**. While it runs, the check view's title button reads **Stop soundcheck** (red text), its line "SOUNDCHECK · song 3 of 9 · about 5 min left", done songs show their numbers, the song being checked is outlined in the accent with a SOUNDCHECK chip, and the rest read "waiting", faint. The Selected column shows its three steps, each with a mark (✓ done, ● now, ○ to come): opened cold (time, memory at its peak); its busiest part (the step's name and bars, the bar playing, CPU as a bar with now and worst, the longest disk wait, misses and xruns); the change into the next song (its ending, then the cold open, timed). Under them an amber line while sound goes out ("Playing out loud on Main out"), then **Stop soundcheck** and **Skip this song**. The busiest part is the song list step with the most streams, plugins and scene changes at once (the first of any tie), played for up to 16 bars; with no song list, the song's first 16 bars. STOP stops it too. At the end the Selected column says how it went ("9 songs in 6 min 10 s · the busiest: Slow signal, CPU 82 % in Outro · no misses") with **Done**, and the song that was open before opens again. What it measured replaces the estimates in the check and the COLD LOAD pause on the Show view.
- **Prepare show media** (new, B2): asks first, in the Selected column: what it copies by kind (audio tracks' takes, stems, sounds over a minute long) with counts and sizes, the stick's room now and after, that each copy is read back and checked, and that the songs stay where they are; then **Start copying** (accent) and **Cancel**. While it copies: a bar, the file, "1.1 of 2.4 GB · about 1 min left" and **Cancel** (the copies so far are deleted). Each copy is read back and checked by size and hash; one that fails twice stops it with the file named and "the stick may be failing", and the set keeps playing from the originals. When it's done: "13 files, 2.4 GB, each checked. The set plays from the stick." with **Done**; the card's Show media row says so, and the check view adds **Use the originals** (the copies are deleted). A file changed since its copy was made is an amber Files line: the show plays the copy until media is prepared again.
- **While a set plays** (B2), Soundcheck and Prepare show media are faint, and a tap says "Close the set or stop it first". Check set still runs: the quick check reads only the songs' manifests.
- **Changed on stage:** after a set, a song that changed on stage shows "changed on stage" in purple in the set's list, and its card offers **Keep the changes** (they go into the song, and Earlier saves keeps the version from before) and **Throw them away**.
- **Hint lines:** the Sets place: "Dial: move · push: edit the set · Play set: plays it in Perform · Check set: every song's checks · BROWSE closes"; the check view (B2): "Dial: move · push: the song's checks · Check every file: reads every file in full · ‹ or –: back to the set · BROWSE closes"; while Soundcheck runs (B2): "Soundcheck plays each song's busiest part · STOP or Stop soundcheck: stop it · what it measured is kept"; while media copies (B2): "Copying the set's long audio to the stick · Cancel: stop and delete the copies"; a set's songs: "Dial: move · - / +: move the picked song · push or tap a value: change it · Add songs: from Projects · BROWSE closes"; adding songs: "Dial: move · push or +: add the song picked · -: done · a tap on +: add that song · BROWSE closes".

## 4q. Patches: what the keys play

**Approved by Jay on 23 Nov 2026** (10:32Z, "Approve as drawn" on the live shows thread's patch screens, https://claude.ai/artifact/J6ktaoNyjNYRqLUi2id6YY; the mockup's source is mpfree-claude `live-shows/mockups/patches-c1.html`). From the live shows plan (approved 22 Nov 2026 06:21Z, decisions 2 to 5), stage C1 ([design](live-shows.md)). A patch is a named keyboard setup kept in the song: up to 8 zones, each a key range and a velocity range playing one of the song's instruments. A song with no patches plays its keys as before. Sample screens: `play-keys`, `play-patches`, `play-patch-edit`. Its second half (Next and Previous patch on Learn's Play card, Program change in: Picks a patch, and the patch on the Show view's keyboards card) is in 4d and 4p.

- **KEYS PLAY** (Play's transport bar, while the song has at least one patch): "KEYS PLAY" over the patch's number and name ("3 · Chorus split") and a 6 px strip of its zones across C1 to C7, each in its instrument's colour; 160 to 200 px, as wide as its name, between the REC chip and PADS PLAY, in the place of the bar and step dots (which stay on the Sequencer tab and Arrange). With no patch picked it reads "Keys as routed". A tap opens the patch list, and again closes it. While a pick waits for your hands it reads "Chorus split → Lead" with "when your hands are up" under it, outlined amber; a missing instrument in the patch playing puts a red dot before its name. A song opens on its patch 1.
- **The patch list,** in place of Play's view, as the REC chip's target list and the XY pad's list are: "KEYS PLAY" and "5 patches in this song" as its title, **New patch** (140 × 44) and **Close** (112 × 44) at the right. Then tiles two across (about 500 × 96, 10 px apart): Keys as routed first, then each patch with its number, its name (20 px), its zones over a keyboard strip and a line naming their instruments, and **Edit** (84 × 44) at its right. The one playing is outlined in the accent and marked PLAYING. A patch with a missing instrument says so on its line in red, naming the instrument by the words its zone keeps ("Track 9 is missing: C5 and up play nothing"). A tap on a tile switches at once and closes the list; Close, the chip or a view closes it with nothing changed. A long list scrolls (a finger that moves 12 px scrolls, so a tile acts when the finger lifts). Edit opens the editor and picks that patch, so the keys play what you edit.
- **Play's MENU** ends with **Patches** (Open), which opens the patch list: a song with no patches has no KEYS PLAY, so it makes its first patch there ("No patches yet: New patch makes one" as the list's title). *Proposed 23 Nov 2026, as the drawings didn't say; Jay chose it on 23 Nov 2026 19:24Z ("Patches on Play's MENU").*
- **New patch** asks for a layout: Whole keyboard, Bass + keys (split at C3), Two layered, Three-way split (up to B2, C3 to B4, C5 up). Its zones start on the instrument the keys play now, and the editor opens on the new patch, named "Patch 6" until renamed, with the keys playing it.
- **The patch editor,** in place of Play's view.
  - **Its head:** ‹ Patches (back to the list), the patch's name (tap to type, 32 characters), "patch 3 · program change 3", and **Copy** (puts "Chorus split copy" after it), **Delete** (asks first: "Delete?" on the button and the hint line, and the second tap deletes) and **Close**.
  - **Zones** (a panel the view's width): a lane per zone (24 px, 6 px apart, up to 8; closer when many), its bar over the keys it takes in its instrument's colour, the instrument's name in it, and a crossed pedal at its right end when Pedals is off; the picked lane outlined; a zone whose instrument is missing hatched in red. A last lane, dashed, reads "+ Zone: press Learn low, then play a key" while fewer than 8. At the panel's right, VEL: each lane's velocity range as a bar from 1 to 127. Under the lanes the keyboard, C1 to C7 (43 white keys), with the keys held lit in the accent; the zones a held key reaches light too, and its velocity shows as a tick on their VEL bars. Under the keyboard, what came in last ("KeyLab 61 · E4 G4 at 96 → Grand, Strings"). Drag a bar's ends to move them; a zone's ends snap to keys.
  - **The picked zone's card** (under the zones, at the left): "ZONE 3 · STRINGS", **Learn low**, **Learn high** (the next key pressed sets the end; a new zone comes with Learn low lit) and **Remove**. Rows: Plays (the song's instruments, as Mix names them), Keyboard (Any keyboard, or one port, all its channels or one; an MPE keyboard by its port), Low key, High key, Transpose (±48), Low velocity, High velocity (which velocities the zone takes), Velocity (how it plays them: As played, Soft, Hard or Fixed 100, the curves of the routes' Velocity block), Pedals (on: sustain, sostenuto and soft reach it) and Wheels and pressure (on: bend, mod wheel, aftertouch and MPE reach it).
  - **This patch** (under the zones, at the right): Keys outside the zones (Nothing, the default; Play an instrument, with which; As routed: the armed track and Routing, as before), and Cut the old sound (off by default: held notes and pedalled chords ring on the patch they started on; on: they stop when this patch comes in).
  - **Q-Links** in the editor ("Q-Links · Patch"): the picked zone's Low key, High key, Transpose and Low velocity.
  - **Wheel:** pick a row; **– / +:** change it; **push:** give the row to the wheel. **ERASE + a lane** removes that zone (section 6). **UNDO:** each change is one take ("Patch 3 · zone 3 · Low key"); turns of one row in a row are one take.
- **How a switch happens:** a tap on a tile, or – / + on the list, switches at once. Next patch and Previous patch (learned on the Play card, 4d) and a program change (Sync's Program change in: Picks a patch) switch at once, or when every key and pedal is up if that control's Switches says so. Next and Previous patch step from the patch a waiting switch goes to (else the one playing), stop at the first and the last, and never step to Keys as routed (from there Next patch picks patch 1); where there is nowhere to go the hint line says why ("Next patch: patch 5 is the song's last"). A switch never cuts a held note or a pedal: each ends where it started.
- **Hint lines:** the list: "Tap a patch: the keys play it · Edit: its zones · wheel: move · push: pick · – / +: the patch before or after · Close: back"; the editor: "Press keys: see what they play · drag a zone's ends · wheel: pick a row · – / +: change it · Learn: the next key sets the end · UNDO: the last change".

## 4r. Add a part and the row's chords

Composing tools S2.6, boards A to D (approved by Joshi 23 Nov 2026 06:39Z, "Yes, as drawn"; boards https://claude.ai/artifact/FW7kVf6T7ADU9UcXDjqnWf, agreed with the Arrange thread first). The design is [compose.md](compose.md), S2.6.

- **Add a part** on the Clips grid's title line, before + Track (the colour legend moves to its left); with two of the pager, Back to song and the Show button showing it steps aside, as there is no room, and it steps aside too where Arrange's buttons would lose their place to it (Perform's Capture scene, the bank chip), so they sit as they do without it. Faint while Rec song is on (a try would be recorded into the song), the hint saying so. A tap opens its panel on the scene pick (or the playing row, which it picks): the row outlined in the accent colour, its number as it is. The panel takes the side area, from under the transport bar to the foot, until Back, Keep or ×: ADD A PART · ROW N; Heard (a line for each part it reads: a dot in the track's colour, the name, what it is); the part as four tiles (Bass, Chords, Melody, Drums); Role, Style, Bars and Track as ‹ › rows, the role's words under Role; Sound as a reading of the track's instrument (a ‹ › row for New track); a status line (the try, or why it can't, in the warning colour); Try again, ‹ Try N, Back and Keep. The part plays in its cell while you choose (dashed, the track's colour, "try N", the loop ring). Keep is one take, "a new part". The Q-Links are Similarity, Variation, Activity and Range.
- **The row's chords**: a strip one row high under the scene pick's row while Add a part or the Chords tab is open (the rows under it move down one; taps, drops and row gaps skip it, a drop on it does nothing, it hides during a clip or row drag, and the row and its strip are kept in view; in Perform's big rows it keeps Make's height). "chords" in the number column, the row's bars across the grid's width with small bar numbers, a chip per chord as long as it lasts: heard solid, suggested dashed, yours in the accent colour; the green play line. With no notes in the row it reads "no chords yet".
- A tap on a chip opens **the chord picker** under it: BAR N with what was heard and what's picked; twelve roots; twelve kinds; Split, Join next, As heard and Use (the chord's name). × closes with nothing changed. A part being tried plays the new chord at once. With Add a part open the chord goes with Keep; from the Chords tab Use is one take, "row N's chords".
- **Clips that no longer fit**: a kept clip with notes off the row's chords shows a dashed outline and "doesn't fit" in the warning colour in its cell, and is never rewritten. **The Chords tab**, a third tab of the Record panel (Record, Clip with the clip's name, Chords; the scene card took the drawn Scene tab's place), shows the row (the scene pick, ‹ › to another), what changed, and those clips ticked with how many notes would move; and Fit to the new chords moves the ticked ones (one take, "the new chords"). Drums and audio are never marked or moved. The Clip tab keeps the clip's name, cut with "…" when it is long.
- **The Sequencer's empty clip**: the note editor's empty grid shows a card with Add a part ›, which opens Arrange's Clips with the panel on the clip's row and track. The empty clip makes way for the part at Keep, and UNDO puts it back.
- Not in this step: the Song view shows no chords yet (a Chords lane under Tempo could come later with the Arrange thread), and chord lengths change by Split and Join only.

## 5. Prefs (SHIFT + MENU)

- **Main rows, in order:**
  1. Pad velocity curve
  2. Latch keeps pads (Replace, the default, Toggle or Add)
  3. Sustain pedal latches (Off, the default, or On: CC 64 holds the arp as Latch does, and lets it go). Both rows are in the arp options too, after Modifier (Jay, 23 Nov 2026 16:08Z, "Approve and merge" on the arp controller tricks card; canvas https://claude.ai/artifact/THaamfTJUJ1T1xWKE8Mppi)
  4. Quick advance (Off, the default, or On: while a set plays, Next song arms and a second press goes, while stopped too; On, one press goes while the transport is stopped. Live shows B1, approved with the Show view, 22 Nov 2026 08:11Z; kept across restarts)
  5. Audio buffer
  6. Find the buttons again
  7. Show developer tools
  8. Sound cores (Shared, Core 3, Cores 3 and 2, Cores 3, 2 and 1 (a test Jay asked for on 12 Nov): where the sound runs from the next start; asked for by the profiling thread, agreed by the app thread on 12 Nov; Jay's go 12 Nov 07:10Z, merged in PR #200, train 14)
  9. Map controls
  10. Line input
  11. Send MIDI and CV out (Off, the default, or "On (sends MIDI)": the MIDI Out jack and the CV jacks send only while it is on, and it is kept across restarts; Jay's pick on the MIDI thread's card, 16 Nov 23:16Z, in place of the `--midi-out` and `--cv-out` start flags. It sits after the Line input latency row)
  12. System info
  13. System status (opens System status, section 2a, as the status chip does)
  14. At start (New project, the default, or Last project: what opens when MPFree starts, once the sound is going; Jay's pick on the project thread's settings card, 13 Nov 14:09Z)
  15. Export (Fast, quiet, the default, or Listen: how an export plays; a song that needs outside gear listens anyway; Jay, 14 Nov 01:32Z, on the missing-features thread)
  16. Export file (24-bit, the default, 16-bit with dither, or 32-bit float) and 17. Export rate (44.1 kHz, the default, or 48 kHz): the format each export starts from; the Export panel's File row changes it for one export (Jay's pick "Both", 14 Nov 07:51Z, on the missing-features thread)
  18. Backup and Akai kits (opens its own rows; Jay's pick "A Prefs row before Touch screen", this thread's card, 17 Nov 18:12Z, with the first start's backup and Akai's kits made optional, his pick "Backup and kits both optional", 18:06Z. Built in the USB creator thread's pull request; the app thread, which owns this spec, is told there.)
  19. Touch screen (opens its own rows)
- **The Backup and Akai kits rows** ("Prefs · Backup and Akai kits"):
  1. Back
  2. Backup of your MPC: "Made 17 Nov 2026", "Not made yet" or "Left out". Push or + asks for a backup at the next start ("At the next start", or "New one at the next start" when one is there); again, or -, takes it back.
  3. Akai's kits and samples: "In BROWSE" once they are in, else "Not in yet" or "Left out". Push or + brings them in at the next start; again, or -, takes it back. Without a backup on the stick they are read straight from the MPC through the read-only window.

  Both rows say "Not on this stick" on a stick the USB creator didn't make. A change shows a line saying it happens at the next start with the stick in, and that the power button's Restart starts it now; nothing runs before then.
- **The Touch screen rows** ("Prefs · Touch screen", approved in the touch thread at 08:13Z, #69):
  1. Back
  2. Screen pad velocity
  3. Calibrate touch
  4. Touch lab
- **Touch screen stays the last main row, and Touch lab its last row,** because the runbook names them by place.
- **Prefs always opens on its main rows.** On System info, the Backup and Akai kits rows or the Touch screen rows, MENU (or Back) goes back to them, on the row that opened them.
- **System info** shows the program's version and commit, the OS, the kernel and the hardware.
- **Line input** is Off at every start.
- **Appearance** (the theme; owner's pick "Yes, both" on the theme thread's card, 22 Nov 2026 07:17Z):
  - **Keep this theme?** Changing Appearance switches the screen at once, and the panel on the right ("About this setting") then asks "Keep Console?" with "Back to Default in 15 s" counting down, and two buttons, **Keep** and **Go back**. Keep, or the data wheel's push on the Appearance row, keeps it. Go back, closing Prefs, or 15 seconds without either, puts the theme from before back. Stepping again starts the 15 seconds again, still going back to the theme from before the first step; stepping back to that one ends the question. While it asks, the panel asks on every row, and the old theme is the one kept for the next start.
  - **Theme problems.** When Appearance is on a theme file, the panel lists that file's problems under the row's help: lines it couldn't use (each uses the base theme's value), keys it doesn't know, lines it ignored, and colours that are hard to read. It also names the theme files that were left out, and why. Only a theme no one could read (text or dim text under 3:1 on the page, a panel or a button) is left out; a file with a mistake in a line is offered.

## 6. The buttons everywhere

- **MENU:** the page's options. On System info, the Backup and Akai kits rows or the Touch screen rows, back to Prefs. On the Tunings page, back to the Synth options' Tuning row (section 3). **‹ Back** at the left of the options' and Prefs' title line (Jay's device notes, 20 Nov 2026: a finger can leave them without MENU; `OptionList::back`, a fingertip's height) does what MENU does there, and SHIFT + MENU in Prefs' main rows; the Synth tab's gear opens its options (section 4g, "The gear"). *Proposed on the "Synth page: pick and play" canvas, https://claude.ai/artifact/WxERuieihAA7FsGwGuS8UA; waits for Jay's approval with the pull request.*
- **SHIFT + MENU:** Prefs.
- **The power button** (Jay, 15 Nov: "work like it does on the MPC"; the card approved as drawn 22:24Z, screens power-ask, power-unsaved and power-going): a short press (up within 1.5 s) opens the **power card** over any page, a page of its own like the number pad: "Shut down the MPC?", a line on the project (unsaved changes in warn, or saved, or no data folder), a line on what Restart starts, and Cancel · Restart · Shut down along its foot, 72 px tall, the last in the accent with the outline on it. Over unsaved work, Shut down or Restart asks "Save … first?" with Cancel · Don't save · Save and shut down (or restart); the save is SHIFT + BROWSE's, and the card waits for it, then says "Shutting down…" or "Restarting…" until the app stops. The wheel and – / + move the outline, the push chooses, and another short press is Cancel. Holding the button is the hard power-off, never a press. Engine: [power button](../hardware/power-button.md).
- **STOP:** the transport, then silence.
- **SHIFT + STOP:** as on the MPC: stop, silence everything and go back to bar 1 (Jay, 20:40Z, control map decision 2).
- **The Console:** hold SHIFT, hold ERASE, then press TAP TEMPO (and the same again to go back). Jay wants a chord nothing else uses, since only developers open it (22:12Z); the hardware control map thread picked this one. The press is no tempo tap, and nothing fires on the way into the chord. The Dev tab's Console page opens it too. It works without developer tools, but has no strip then.
- **ERASE held + a tap deletes what the tap lands on**, where UNDO brings it back (Jay after live test 3, 13 Nov 13:27Z): a clip on Arrange (the takes' undo; a clip the song uses is refused, since song edits have no undo yet), a pattern lane's name on Play (that sound's steps, one take), a step on Play's rows or the Sequencer's Steps (the step and its locks, one take: a plain tap only switches a step off and keeps it, the Play, Pads and Sequencer redesign's step 3b), and a layer on the Looper tab (the looper's undo; the last layer stays), or there a ring or a loop's name (the whole loop, which UNDO brings back; step 3a of the Looper tab), and a zone's lane in the patch editor (that zone, one take; live shows C1, section 4q). An empty slot says why nothing happened. While ERASE is held the hint line lists what it deletes; those taps only delete (no mute, launch or new clip), and taps elsewhere work as usual. Song blocks, effects, macros and chop slices wait until they have an undo. **Live erase** (sequencer roadmap S1, the MPC's way): with ERASE held while the transport records, a pad pressed doesn't sound or record; its notes on the armed track come out as the playhead reaches them, on every pass, until the pad or ERASE is let go, as part of the take UNDO takes back. With STEP SEQ on, ERASE + a step pad stays STEP SEQ's. The FX lock mode with a step held comes later.
- **ERASE held + a pad** (Joshi's go on the unused buttons proposals, 19 Nov 06:35Z, proposals 9 and 14): while recording, live erase as before (sequencer S1); **while only playing**, the same, with the kit on the pads, each hold of ERASE one take UNDO brings back ("erase 1" in the takes list); **stopped**, every note of that kit pad comes out of the armed track's clip at once, one edit take, as ERASE + a lane's name. The pad plays nothing. STEP SEQ, TRACK MUTE, 16 LEVEL and the Perform set keep their own ERASE + pad.
- **COPY held + a tap on the Clips grid** (sequencer S3, the roadmap's "copy and paste"; COPY's first job is STEP SEQ's steps, above): a clip tapped is copied (a loop cell says it copies with SHIFT + a drag), and an empty slot tapped, on any track and any page, gets a copy of the clip copied last, as a take UNDO takes away. A slot holding a clip, a Loop track's cell, or nothing copied yet says why. The copy is kept until another is made, so it can go into several slots. While COPY is held on the grid the hint line says what a tap copies or pastes.
- **COPY held + a tap in the note editor** (piano roll plan 1B.4; the owner's go on the plan, 22 Nov 2026 05:10Z): a tap on a note copies the picked notes, or that one; a tap on empty roll, or an empty line of the tracker, pastes the copy there with its first note at the tap, in this clip or another track's, with the notes' pressure and the locks that track has the setting of (the rest left out and said), as one take UNDO takes back. The copy is kept until another is made. While COPY is held over the editor the hint line says what a tap copies or pastes.
- **COPY held + pads** (Joshi's go on the unused buttons proposals, 19 Nov 06:35Z, proposal 3; the MPC's Copy and Delete pad): while the pads play the kit (no STEP SEQ, TRACK MUTE, 16 LEVEL, Perform set or synth on them), the first pad hit is the one copied and each pad hit after it takes a copy of its sound and settings, in the bank PAD BANK shows; **SHIFT + COPY** held empties each pad hit. Each pad is one undo step, as the Sample tab's pad copy is, and the pads don't play while COPY has them. The hint line says so while COPY is held.
- **FULL LEVEL** (Joshi's go on the unused buttons proposals, 19 Nov 06:35Z, proposal 8; the MPC's hold FULL LEVEL): a press (on its release) turns Full level on and off, and SHIFT + FULL LEVEL Half level, as before. **Held, with a pad hit**, it sets the level every hit plays at to that hit's level and turns it on in Half level's place; the hit itself plays. The chip then reads "Level 92" (Half level at the default 64), and SHIFT + FULL LEVEL turns it off and on. The level is kept in the app settings (`half-level`, storage spec). While FULL LEVEL is held the hint line says what a hit does.
- **TC** (Joshi's go on the unused buttons proposals, 19 Nov 06:35Z, proposal 1; the MPC's Timing Correct): opens the Sequencer's note editor on Timing Correct's settings; pressed again there, it corrects the notes, as the editor's TC button does. **SHIFT + TC** turns quantise while recording on and off for the armed track: off remembers the grid it had, and on gives it back (1/16 the first time).
- **NOTE REPEAT:** repeat. Press it twice, or SHIFT + it, to Latch.
- **NOTE REPEAT + MENU:** the Arp panel.
- **TAP TEMPO held** (Joshi's go on the unused buttons proposals, 19 Nov 06:35Z, proposal 11): the data wheel turns the tempo, 1 BPM a detent, 0.1 with SHIFT (the song's tempo point at the playhead while song mode plays its points, as the tempo chip's number pad); held 0.6 s alone, the tempo's number pad opens as it is let go. A tap is a tap as before. The hint line says so while it is held. SHIFT + TAP TEMPO alone stays empty: it starts the Console's chord.
- **SHIFT + a kit pad** (Joshi's go on the unused buttons proposals, 19 Nov 06:35Z, proposal 17): picks that pad, as a hit would (the Sample and Pads tabs, the drum synth's page, the Play page's Q-Links, STEP SEQ's next sound), and plays and lights nothing; only while the pads play the kit. SHIFT + ERASE stays reserved.
- **TRACK MIX:** the Mix tab on Track Mix; SHIFT + TRACK MIX on Pad Mix, as the FX thread proposed; on the Mix tab, either goes back to the page before it (section 3a). The guide names the button "Track Mix / Pad Mix"; the pad player hasn't seen it yet, and round 7's mapper lists it.
- **REC, OVERDUB, UNDO:** **REC and OVERDUB record what is armed, on every page** (Jay's sequencer decision 1, 16 Nov 2026, replacing "the screen picks" of 13 Nov; [rec-arm.md](rec-arm.md)): the notes of the track the pads play (into its clip) or the picked loop. The Sequencer tab arms the notes, the Looper tab arms the loop, and picking a track arms its notes (Play leaves the arm, since it shows both); a tap on an Arm pill or a row of the chip's target list arms what it names, and SHIFT + that tap arms both, each recording as it would alone; SHIFT + a tap on a track's row in the list records that track's notes too, in the same take (several tracks, the MPC's SHIFT + a track's arm). **An empty kit pad held + REC** (sampler R5.7, Jay's card of 18 Nov 08:52Z, one take only; rec-arm.md, "A pad take") arms the Sample tab's recorder onto that pad for one take, over the arm: a pad playing the kit (not under STEP SEQ, TRACK MUTE, 16 Levels or the loop keys) with no sound (one with a sound held is plain REC). The REC chip reads "REC → Pad A5 · Line in" (the Rec tab's source), "● REC → Pad A5 · Line in" while it records, and the header shows it on pages without a transport bar. It starts on the Rec tab's threshold, else once the music plays. REC again or STOP ends it and keeps what recorded (STOP while it records leaves the music playing), UNDO while it records throws it away, and REC then records what it did before: the target and both never change for it. While it waits it holds like a pin, a tap on a pill or a row calls it off and arms what was tapped, SHIFT + a tap is refused, and arming it lets go of a notes ready or a loop waiting for PLAY; it is refused while a take counts in or records. Nothing moves the arm while a take records (a tap says "Finish the take first"), pinned only a tap moves it, and a ready goes with it. With the notes armed, REC from stopped makes them ready (the REC chip outlined in red, the hint line saying what PLAY records) and PLAY or PLAY START starts the take after the count-in; REC again or STOP lets go; OVERDUB readies a take that adds. While playing, REC punches in replacing and OVERDUB adding; while recording, REC punches out, and OVERDUB switches a replacing take to adding or punches an adding one out, never turning adding into replacing (Jay's device run, 20 Nov 2026: OVERDUB pressed again to stop adding erased notes). With a loop armed they are the looper's Record and Overdub; with an empty Loop cell armed on the Clips grid (section 4f), REC records a new loop into it and OVERDUB layers the loop that Loop track plays. **While a take records, UNDO throws it away** (sequencer decision 1): the notes punch out and their take so far goes, the music playing on (during the count-in, it stops); a loop's first pass or overdub is undone. Otherwise **UNDO follows the page you're on** (Jay, 13 Nov 13:53Z): the Looper tab undoes the loop; the Sequencer tab, Arrange and the Arp and pattern screens undo the takes; Play undoes whichever of the two changed last; the Sample and Pads tabs undo their edits; the Synth tab undoes knob turns and the mixer strip changes (a run of turns of one control within 1.5 s is one step; `mpfree-app/src/undo.rs`); other pages, for now the chain editor and the macros panel too, say "Nothing to undo on this page". The line in place of the hint says what was undone ("Undid take 3 · Drums / 1", "Undo: layer 3 fades out"). SHIFT + UNDO redoes, the same way. **Held for 0.6 s, UNDO opens that history as a list** (Jay after live test 3; the app thread's 13 Nov 13:30Z proposal, point 5): the press still undoes one step at once, and the list opens over the page under the top bar (the `Undo history` page, `mpfree_ui::history::HistoryPanel`). Its rows are Start, then each step oldest first, then the steps REDO would bring back, faint; the row that is now is filled and tagged "now". A tap on a row goes back (or forward) to just after that step, as many steps as it takes; the wheel and – / + move an outline through the rows and the wheel's push goes to the outlined row. The list stays open and follows its history (UNDO and SHIFT + UNDO still step it); Close or a change of page closes it. Every row and Close is 64 px tall. Steps are named as the page names them: the loop's as the Looper editor's Undo history does, takes as "Edit 3 · Drums / 1", the kit's edits by pad ("Pad A3", "Chop to 8 pads from A5"), knob and fader turns with where they went ("Filter Cutoff to 1.2 kHz", "Drums Volume to -6.0 dB"; the app keeps those and sends them on `FromUi::UndoHistory`). While the list or the number pad is open the app gives the wheel, its push and – / + to it on every page (`FromUi::Overlay`). UNDO's light is bright while the page showing has something to undo and dim when not (blinking while recording, as Akai's); the screen works it out and tells the app (`FromUi::UndoReady`). The idle light is inferred, not confirmed: in an earlier capture Akai's app took UNDO's light from dim to bright during Q-Link edits, which fits lighting it when there is something to undo.
- **All four PAD BANK buttons held** (Joshi's go on the unused buttons proposals, 19 Nov 06:35Z, proposal 10; the MPC's screenshot): a picture of the screen, saved by the store in `files/screens/` (storage spec); the fourth press does only that, the first three their own jobs. The hint line says where it went.
- **PAD BANK A–D:** banks. SHIFT, or a second press, gives E–H. While controller mode has the pads (section 4d) they pick the bank of notes the pads send. While STEP SEQ has the pads they are its pages, while TRACK MUTE has them its banks of tracks, and while the Perform set has them (section 4a) its pages, A the loop keys, clip windows and scenes pages among them (a scenes page names its bank, so PAD BANK still turns the set's pages there; section 4e). With a kit on the pads they are the pages of the armed track's kit (sampler R3, Jay's decision 1, 16 Nov 2026): A is its pads A1–A16, B is B1–B16, up to H, 113–128; the bank stays when another track is armed or another kit picked, and the PAD BANK of the bank on the pads is lit. They no longer change kits (as they did from Jay's 13 Nov 14:26Z pick until then): the list of kits picks the armed track's kit, and SHIFT + wheel on Play steps through that list. While the synth has the pads, PAD BANK picks its pad layouts and SHIFT + wheel its octave, so the list of kits (what the pads play on Play, or the Pads tab's Kit) is the way back to a kit.
- **SHIFT + REC:** Capture, on every page; on the Looper tab, the looper's Capture (the last bars played become a loop; step 4a of section 4a).
- **Hold a kit pad + SHIFT + REC:** Sample Recall onto that pad, on every page (sampler decision 3; the overlay in section 4i). A kit pad means a pad playing the kit now: with STEP SEQ, TRACK MUTE or 16 LEVEL on, the pads as the loop keys, or the synth on the pads, SHIFT + REC stays Capture, as it is with no pad held (the looper's on the Looper tab). Recall is refused while a take is ready, counting in or recording ("Finish the take first", as REC arm rule 6). While the overlay is open, PLAY auditions the bracket, REC keeps it and STOP closes it (the transport plays on); SHIFT + REC is still Capture, and the wheel, - / + and Q-Links 1 to 4 go to it (`FromUi::Overlay`, `FromUi::Recall`).
- **SHIFT + PLAY START:** the metronome on or off.
- **SHIFT + PLAY** (Joshi's go on the unused buttons proposals, 19 Nov 06:35Z, proposal 13; the MPC's play from the last start): stopped, plays from where PLAY last started the transport (PLAY START: bar 1); playing, the playhead jumps back there. Before PLAY has started anything it is PLAY. While a take is ready it is PLAY, as before.
- **SHIFT + OVERDUB** (Joshi's go on the unused buttons proposals, 19 Nov 06:35Z, proposal 15; the MPC's Count In): the count-in off, or back to the bars it had (1 bar the first time), on every page; the hint line says which.
- **PLAY while the transport plays:** FILL while it is held (notes with the FILL and !FILL trig conditions follow it); pressed twice, FILL stays latched until PLAY is pressed again or STOP (Jay, 20:54Z, control map decision 9). Stopped, PLAY plays. **Following another device's Start and Stop** (Sync's Tempo from with Start and stop on; sequencer S5, the review's "Following another sequencer"), PLAY and PLAY START wait for the leader while it is stopped: the pattern they would start is made ready, PLAY START goes to bar 1, and the line in place of the hint says "Waiting for clock: PLAY starts when MIDI In starts". A take made ready with REC records from the leader's Start, on its first pulse and with no count-in (PLAY keeps it ready and says "Waiting for clock: Bass records when MIDI In starts"); STOP lets go of it.
- **The mode buttons** go to the page with the same job (Jay, 20:40Z; the control map doc, https://claude.ai/artifact/CzSwDDjX1Ngc7LkhXP4KqD):
  - MAIN: a press (on its release) goes to Play (one view since the looper redesign's step 5, so on Play it stays). In Perform (section 2's chip; sequencer decision 5), Arrange's Clips grid; on Arrange, the next view: Clips, the Song view, the song list (section 4k) and round.
