While a set is open the Show view comes first (section 4p): in Perform MAIN goes to it, and on Arrange goes Show, Clips, Song, List. **SHIFT + MAIN** switches between Make and Perform. **In Perform the arm is pinned** (the REC chip's Pin the arm turns on, and off again in Make): only a tap on an Arm pill moves it. **MAIN held** (Joshi's go on the unused buttons proposals, 19 Nov 06:35Z, proposal 4; the MPC's track select): the pads pick a track of 16, a pad arming its track as a tap on its Arm pill would, and the page stays when MAIN is let go. PAD BANK picks which 16 (A: tracks 1-16, B: 17-32, up to H, as TRACK MUTE's banks), lit for the bank shown. The pads light green for a track with a clip launched, dim green for the other tracks, white for the track picked and dark past the last track; a dark pad says on the hint line that there is no such track. While MAIN is held the hint line says what a pad does.
  - STEP SEQ (sequencer decision 2, Jay's card "Pads become steps, as designed", 16 Nov 2026 20:00Z; the design is [step-pads.md](step-pads.md)): **the lit mode button says what the pads are.** A press (on its release) makes the pads the steps of one sound over whatever page shows, and opens the Sequencer tab on its Steps (closing the note editor if it shows); the same press again gives them back. The sound is the pad played last; **held**, STEP SEQ gives the pads back to the kit (PAD BANK picks the kit's bank), and the pad tapped is the sound when it is let go. Pad 1 (bottom left) is the page's first step. A tap on an empty step puts a note there at the pad's velocity (127 with FULL LEVEL), heard once while stopped; a lit step comes off when the pad is let go. With no clip, the first tap on page 1 makes a one-bar clip and launches it, as a tap on Beats does. **PAD BANK** A–D are pages 1–4, SHIFT or a second press 5–8; **SHIFT + STEP SEQ** turns Follow on, so the page turns with the playhead (a page picked by hand turns it off). **ERASE + a step pad** clears that step, and **ERASE + a PAD BANK** that page of the sound's steps, locks included; UNDO brings them back. One pad mode at a time: STEP SEQ, TRACK MUTE, 16 LEVEL and the loop keys (section 4a) each turn the others off. **The lights:** STEP SEQ lit while the pads are steps; a step with a note in the sound's colour, brighter the harder; purple with locks, dim purple for locks with no note; dim for the rest of a note held over; white for the playhead; a faint glow on an empty first step of each beat; dark past the clip's end. The PAD BANK of the page shown is lit and the others dim (only the two values Akai's app was seen sending for them, so pages past the clip's end are dim, not dark), and the page playing blinks when it is another of the four. **Holding a step pad** gives it the Q-Links on Play and Arrange, over a macro bank: its Step bank (velocity, length, nudge, chance; the cards say "Step 5"), and Q-LINK while it is held steps to its Trig bank (condition, ratchet, nudge in ticks, a fourth card empty for the ratchet ramp in S6), then its Sound bank (a kit pad's Tune, Decay, Filter and Level, or the synth's first Q-Link set: a turn is a lock on the steps held), and back; a whole turn is one undo, and a lit step turned while held stays on its release. Steps held together are set together, to the first one's value. **Hold a step and tap a later one** before turning anything: the note lasts to the end of that step (tapped inside the note, it ends there); a lit step, an earlier one, or any step after a turn joins the steps held instead. The held steps are outlined on Beats, the step inspector shows the first one in the throws' place (section 4), and the hint line says what a hold does. **COPY** (its first job) held: a step pad picks the step to copy and each one tapped after it takes a copy, locks included; a PAD BANK picks a page of the sound's steps and the next PAD BANK takes the copy. **Locks** (S1's third part, 17 Nov): **SHIFT + an empty step** holds it with no note, for a lock with no note; steps held with no note always show the Sound bank, and SHIFT held while the pads are steps puts this on the hint line. **ERASE pressed while steps are held** takes their locks off and leaves their notes; UNDO brings them back. With steps held, **a knob turned on the Synth tab** (for the synth the steps' track plays) is a lock on them, not a change to the sound, and the tab shows the first step's locks until they are let go. A pad's sound and the synth play their locks: a kit pad's level, pan and filter move the sound still ringing, its tune and envelope the next note. A knob turned on another page with a step held (the mixer, an effect) does what it does there until a later part makes it a lock. **On the synth** (S1's sixth part, 17 Nov; the design's "Synth tracks"), the pads are the steps of the whole line, chords included: a step lights for any note on it, and a new step plays **the pen**, the pad or pads picked last. **STEP SEQ held** gives the pads to the synth: a pad played picks the pen, pads held together a chord; with none picked yet, the pen is the pad played last. The chip names the synth and the pen ("Pads: steps · Poly · C3 + G3", as the design's "Pads: steps · Bass · C2") and the hint line says how to pick it. A lit step comes off whole, ties, ERASE, COPY, the Q-Links and locks take every note of a step, and while stopped a step put on sounds the pen once. **A line step held** (S1's seventh part, the design's "Synth tracks") has the Step bank **Note**, velocity, length and chance, the nudge on Trig (in ticks): a Note detent moves its notes a pad along the layout, a chord keeping its shape, and the card says "Note" or "Chord" with the lowest note. **A keyboard's keys** (MIDI IN or USB) played while line steps are held go on them, the keys held together as a chord, in place of what they held, as one undo; a key no pad plays says so on the hint line. Picked on Beats, the inspector's head names the step's notes ("Step 1 · E3 + G3").
  - NEXT SEQ: Arrange. **SHIFT + NEXT SEQ** (Joshi's go on the unused buttons proposals, 19 Nov 06:35Z, proposal 5; the MPC's XYFX): the XY page (section 4o), from any page; again, back to Play's view. Until the XY page is built (the XY plan's phase 4) it is the XY pad full screen on Play (the page `Play · XY`: the pad in the whole view under the transport bar, beside the master bar and the Q-Links, its knob twice the size), with the XY page's Hold, Let go and Setup under the pad from the XY plan's phase 2. Its chip still opens the XY pad's list, which comes back to the full screen; a view picked or another page closes it. Sample screen `play-xy-full`.
  - SAMPLER: Sample. SHIFT + SAMPLER: Looper; pressed again on the Looper tab, the pads as the Perform set at the page shown last (page A: the loop keys) and back (section 4a).
  - SAMPLE EDIT: Sample, on its Wave editor. **SHIFT + SAMPLE EDIT** (Joshi's go on the unused buttons proposals, 19 Nov 06:35Z, proposal 6; the MPC's Q-Link Edit): the macros panel on Macros 1-4 over the page (section 4b); on it, back to the page it opened over.
  - PROG EDIT: the sound the pads play: the Synth tab while the synth has them, otherwise the Pads tab on Pad; pressed on the Pads tab, Edit pad on the pad picked (again: back to the pads). **SHIFT + PROG EDIT** (Joshi's go on the unused buttons proposals, 19 Nov 06:35Z, proposal 7; the MPC's MIDI Control): the Mix tab's I/O view on its Controller section (section 4d), as the tempo chip opens Sync.
  - 16 LEVEL (Jay, 13 Nov 13:13Z card "Button plus a 16 Levels panel"; 13:23Z: square cells, in the colour the pads are lit): 16 Levels on for the pad hit last, with the 16 Levels panel on Play; again, off. The panel takes the view under the transport bar, as the Arp panel does: a heading with the pad they play, Pick pad, Close (the panel goes, 16 Levels stays) and Off; 16 square cells as the pads (pad 1 bottom left), each with its value and step bar, in the kit's pad colour with the held pad bright; and the kinds (Velocity, Tune, Cutoff, Decay, Start) in a column beside them, picked by a tap or the wheel. The Q-Links and the master bar stay as on Play. The button is lit while 16 Levels is on, and a "16 Levels · tune" status chip (warn, after Full level) shows it on every page. **Transpose** (sequencer S4, Jay's card "16 Levels gets a Transpose kind", 17 Nov 2026 21:43Z; [make-perform.md](make-perform.md)) is the column's sixth kind, "-8 to +7": the pads move the armed track and the tracks that follow it, latched, pad 9 as written ("as written" under its value, as Tune's "as recorded"), each other pad a semitone either side, or a note of the key while the synth's pads play In key or chords ("+5" rather than "+5 st"). The heading names the track, those that follow and where it is ("Bass, with Keys · +5 st", "Bass · -3 in C Minor"), or dim why the pads can't move it ("Arm the track to transpose", "Drums plays a kit: arm a track that plays notes"); the cell it is on is bright (none past ±8), Pick pad is faint, and the chip reads "16 Levels · transpose". The MPC's pads light the step it is on amber, pad 9 dim white and the rest dim blue. With the synth on the pads, 16 LEVEL opens Transpose at once; on a kit it is picked from the column. Q-Link 2 turns it in Tune's place.
  - SHIFT + 16 LEVEL: the synth pads' Chromatic | In key switch.
  - Q-LINK: while controller mode is on (section 4d), the controller's next four Q-Links on every page (1-4, 5-8, 9-12, 13-16); otherwise the Q-Link column's next bank on Play, the chain editor and the macros panel (section 4b), and on the Looper tab (Loops 1-4, Loops 5-8, the picked loop; section 4a); while STEP SEQ's step pads are held, their Step, Trig or Sound bank; with a step picked on the Sequencer's Steps, its Step or Trig bank (the step inspector shows which, section 4). **SHIFT + Q-LINK** steps back a bank wherever Q-LINK steps on (Joshi's go on the unused buttons proposals, 19 Nov 06:35Z, proposal 2), the controller's Q-Links included.
- **TRACK MUTE** (control map decision 5): the pads become mute keys over whatever page shows, and the page stays. Pads 1-16 mute and unmute 16 tracks at once (their clips keep their place), and while the pads are mute keys the PAD BANK buttons pick which 16 (A: tracks 1-16, B: 17-32, up to H: 113-128; section 4f) and are lit for that bank; a pad is lit green while its track plays a clip, dim green with nothing launched, red while muted, and dark with no track. **SHIFT + TRACK MUTE** does the same for the selected track's pads (the speakers on Play), on any kit and on the bank PAD BANK shows: lit in the kit's colour while a pad plays, red while muted, dark without a sound. A pad mute is the track's alone (the instrument redesign's step 10, as Jay chose on his card of 22 Nov 2026): another track playing the same kit keeps the pad. On a track that plays notes the pads stay lit and a press says to mute the track. The same press again leaves, and the other one switches. The hint line says what the pads are, and the button is lit while they are mute keys. A pad held when TRACK MUTE is pressed still lets go as a note. **When a track's mute lands** (sequencer decision 5, 16 Nov; [make-perform.md](make-perform.md)): in Make at once; in Perform it waits for the launch line (the Record tab's Launch row), and SHIFT + the pad gives the other timing. Taps that wait join **the mute queue** and all land together on its line; a tap on a track waiting takes its change back. With Launch at once, or the transport stopped, a tap acts at once, and STOP lands whatever waits. A queue keeps its line when Launch changes. A waiting pad blinks amber (twice a second). **Solo:** hold TRACK MUTE and tap a pad: that track plays and the others go silent, the soloed pad lit blue and the tracks silent by solo red (Akai's app was seen sending no dim red); more taps solo more tracks, and a soloed pad tapped again lets go of its solo. A quick press of TRACK MUTE still turns the mode on or off; held with a pad tapped, the mode is left as it was before the press. A solo waits or lands just as a mute does. The kit's pads (SHIFT + TRACK MUTE) take the same timing and join the same queue (a pad's change lands just before the line, within about 3 ms), and WAITING names them ("Pad 3 at 9.1"); held TRACK MUTE over them solos a pad of the kit (lit blue, the pads silent by it red), and letting go leaves the pads the kit's.
- **Built in, never mapped by the musician** (Jay, 16 Nov 04:04Z: "ALL of the buttons should always be mapped by default"): ERASE, Q-LINK, the mode buttons and the data dial's push have measured notes since the creator's fifth stick's Map controls pass, so every button works from the start. Map controls stays, for developers, to learn a button again.
- **The data dial's push** (Jay, 12 Nov 08:11Z): in Prefs and the options menus, it opens the picked row (its value box outlined), the wheel changes the value, and a second push sets it and gives the wheel back to the rows. On a row that opens something (System info, Backup and Akai kits, Touch screen, Back, Find the buttons again, Map controls, Calibrate touch, Touch lab) it opens it, as + does.
- **The data wheel on a page with rows** (Jay, 13 Nov 06:27Z): on Sample (every sub-page with a settings list), the Pads tab, Synth, Arrange and the Sequencer's Notes, the wheel moves the highlight through the rows (on Synth, the tiles) and - / + step the highlighted one, as in the menus. A push opens the highlighted row for the wheel (its value box outlined, the Synth tile raised); the wheel then changes it, and a second push gives the wheel back to the rows. Changing page closes it. SHIFT keeps each page's second wheel job: SHIFT + wheel is the next take or pad with a sound on Sample (the page's takes first, then the pads: the one Sample page's step 2b) and the next pad with a sound on Pads, the Synth page on Synth and scrolling on Arrange; SHIFT + - / + zooms the wave on Wave, Warp and Chop. Pads' Browse view keeps the wheel it had; Play, the Looper tab, the mixer and the chain editor have the outline (next).
- **The data wheel's outline** (Jay, 13 Nov 14:26Z: the wheel as the main way round the screen besides touch). On Play, the Sequencer's Steps, the Looper tab, the mixer, the chain editor and the macros panel, a tap puts an outline on the control it lands on, and the wheel moves the outline from control to control: through the panel in reading order (rows top to bottom, each left to right), then on into the next panel (panels by their top, then their left), stopping at the first and the last. Controls drawn as unavailable are skipped.
  - **The push** does what a tap does on a button, a row, a pad or a step. On a control that is held (a throw) it holds it down until the next push. On a value (a mixer fader, pan or send, a Q-Link card, the master level, an effect's knob, a slider) it grabs it: the outline thickens in the second accent colour, the wheel turns the value by the same steps as its Q-Link, and the next push lets it go. Values the wheel can't turn yet (the XY pad, waveform markers, the Looper tab's trims, the macros panel's knobs) say "drag it with a finger". On the picked effect in the chain editor the push picks it up (the outline thickens), a turn or – / + moves it a place along the chain per detent with the outline on it, and the next push puts it down (Jay, 14 Nov 12:03Z; section 4b).
  - **– / +** step an outlined value without a push, and move the outline on anything else. **SHIFT + – / +** (Joshi's go on the unused buttons proposals, 19 Nov 06:35Z, proposal 16) step it in the finer steps SHIFT + wheel gives a held control (`Nav::Fine`, `Ui::focus_step_fine`).
  - **SHIFT + the push** (Joshi's go, proposal 12): the outlined value back to its default: a mixer fader to 0 dB, a pan to the middle, a send off, the master level to 0 dB, a loop's level to 0 dB and an effect's knob, fader or slider to its setting's own default (`Widget::reset`, `Ui::focus_reset`, `Effect::Reset`). The hint line says it went back, or that it was at its default already or has none (Q-Link cards, the macros panel's knobs and the Synth tab's controls have none yet). With a finger resting on a control, SHIFT + the push puts that control back instead (`Ui::held_reset`).
  - **When it starts:** on Play and the Looper tab the first turn shows the outline on the first control; on the mixer, the chain editor and the macros panel the wheel keeps its job there (the selected strip's volume, Q-Link 1) until a tap or a push puts the outline on a control. Changing page, or a tap where there is no control, takes it away. SHIFT + wheel and NOTE REPEAT keep their jobs.
  - **In the effect picker** the wheel turns the tiles, SHIFT + wheel the groups, and a push opens presets and then adds, whether or not the outline is on the chain editor (the FX thread's picker, #315). With the picker shut, the push is the outline's.
  - The pages with rows (Sample, Pads, Synth, Arrange), the menus, the Arp panel, the workspace and the Dev tab keep their own wheel for now.
  - How it is built: the screen keeps the outline, since it knows each widget's controls (`Widget::targets`, from the control audit) and which of them the wheel can turn (`Widget::steps` and `Widget::step`). It tells the app whether it shows one (`FromUi::Outline`), and the app sends the wheel, its push and – / + to it as `Effect::Nav` ([UI architecture](ui-architecture.md#the-wheels-outline)).
- **Hold and turn** (Jay, 17 Nov 18:28Z: "if I am holding my finger on a UI element on the screen, then I want to be able to use the data wheel to control that element"; the proposal approved as written at 18:38Z, https://claude.ai/artifact/93L3BnFbtiRgBWXqAq4LhC). On every page, while a finger rests on a control with a value, the wheel and – / + turn it, by the same step as the push-and-turn (its Q-Link's step); there is no wait. It shows the grabbed outline (5 px, second accent) where the finger went down, and the hint line reads its name and value ("… · the wheel turns it while you hold"). When the finger lifts, the wheel, its push and – / + go back to the page's own job (the outline, the row highlight, the zoom), the outline staying where the finger put it.
  - **What holds:** a drag the widget's wheel turns (`Widget::steps`: faders, pans, sends, Q-Link and macro cards, the master level, effect knobs and sliders, an EQ dot, which turns its Q as before); a whole settings row (`OptionList`, the Sample tab's `SettingList`, not a button row) as its arrows step it; a Synth tab control or section switch, turned as the wheel turns the selected tile; a marker's tab on the Wave page, moved as its Q-Link moves it (a screen pixel a detent, snapped the way it turns). A note on the note editor's roll, turning its velocity (or the picks', as Q4 turns them); SHIFT + the push puts it back to 100, the velocity a drawn note gets (Jay's yes on both, 23 Nov 2026 23:28Z). Buttons, tabs, pads, steps, clips and rows that only pick keep the page's wheel. Sequencer steps are the sequencer thread's to give a held job.
  - **Edge cases:** a touch the wheel didn't turn is what it always was; once turned, the lift is a cancel, not a tap (a typed value's number pad now opens on the lift for that reason, and a macro card's half-second long press is ended by the first turn). A value dragged keeps the wheel and both move it; a row dragged more than 12 px is a scroll and lets go. The wheel follows the newest finger still on a control; lifting it gives it back to an older one. SHIFT + wheel while holding is finer where the control has a finer step (the mixer's SHIFT steps, a tenth of an effect knob's step, a thousandth of a slider, a marker's single frame unsnapped); SHIFT pressed before the touch is still MIDI Learn. The push does nothing while held; SHIFT + the push puts the held control back to its default, and SHIFT + – / + step it finer (the button combos of 19 Nov). A list or pad over the page keeps the wheel; a change of page drops every hold. On the Sample and Pads tabs a hold is one undo step (their edit runs end on the lift); the Synth tab and the mixer join turns of one control within 1.5 s, as before.
  - How it is built: `Widget::held` names the control under a finger as it comes down (before the widget sees the touch, which may scroll it) and `Widget::held_step` turns it, `fine` with SHIFT; `mpfree_ui::Ui` keeps one hold per finger and turns the newest. The screen tells the app at once (`FromUi::Held`), and the app sends the wheel and – / + as `Effect::Held` ahead of every page's own wheel job.
- **The transport lights** (Jay, 13 Nov 06:37Z, card "Capture Akai's lights first"): PLAY, REC and OVERDUB light only once a read-only capture under Akai's 3.6.0 shows the message its software sends for them; until then the app sends nothing for them and says so once in the Console. **The capture ran in live test 3 (13 Nov 09:33Z):** PLAY is lit while playing, REC and OVERDUB blink while armed (with PLAY) and while recording, all with values 3 and 0 (controller protocol page, "Akai's transport lights"). Wiring them is the next build. The MPC One's STOP button has **no LED** (Jay, 13 Nov), so the STOP light the app sends shows nothing on this model.
- **BROWSE** (controls decision 13; Jay after live test 3): opens the browser over the page and goes back to it (section 3, "Not tabs"), as the folder icon does. While it shows, the wheel moves through the list, its push opens or loads, - goes up a folder and + into one, PLAY hears the sound picked (it doesn't start the transport), Q-Link 1 scrolls, Q-Link 3 is the preview level and Q-Link 4 the places, SHIFT + wheel moves through the places, and ERASE deletes the item picked (into the Trash, asking first). **SHIFT + BROWSE** saves the project on any page. BROWSE has no measured note yet, so it works once Map controls has found it.
- **Still to come from the control map:** COPY anywhere but the pads, STEP SEQ's steps, the Clips grid and the note editor. All 17 of the 19 Nov proposals Joshi approved are built.

## 7. How changes happen

- **Each thread owns the inside of its own tab or panel.** A change there needs no layout approval.
- **Anything in the shared layout is a proposal.** Send it to the owner. Jay approves it, this file is updated, and then it lands.
- **Previews draw the real bar.** Every crate's preview pictures use the canonical tab list, `mpfree_ui::widgets::APP_TABS` (and `Header::app`), not a list of their own.

## Proposed, waiting for Jay (not part of the spec yet)

- **Pressure ring on the screen's pad tiles** (synth thread, 08:53Z, poly aftertouch design part 7): a thin ring showing each pad's shaped pressure.
- **Scales on the synth pads** (arp thread; design https://claude.ai/artifact/DceFbX4jtupvJUT4759ZBZ). **Approved by Jay 09:55Z:** the pads are chromatic by default (the scale's notes lit once one is picked), and one simple, direct action switches them to In key and back (Jay likes how the Hydrasynth does it). In key fills the rows in sequence by default, with "a fourth apart" as the other choice. While the synth is on the pads, PAD BANK A to H hold a pad layout per bank (approved for now, to discuss later). Chord pads: triads on the bottom two rows, sevenths on the top two. **Jay 12:22Z (card):** the switch is a Chromatic | In key chip in the pads panel header, on the Play page and on the Synth tab; one tap switches the bank playing, and the status chip reads "Poly · A minor · in key". SHIFT + PAD BANK stays banks E to H.
- **SHIFT + REC = Capture on every page** (time thread, 09:35Z, as the sequencer plan had it; patch shift-rec-capture-17e5961.patch). Without it, Capture works from the Takes panel's button on Arrange (#80).
- **A new Play page** (Jay, 09:34Z and 09:40Z): the big pads go. Play is the home for finger drumming, live looping and making beats, with a small pad map, the four Q-Links in a column beside the knobs, and a thin master bar. Mockups A to E (09:52Z, /mnt/project-files/hello-mpc/play-mockups/). **Jay 10:05Z:** the all-in-one layout (D) is the default home view, and a one-tap switch goes to the other views (Beats, Loop, Drum) and back; a speaker icon beside each instrument (and loop layer) mutes it, instead of a Mutes panel, and the space goes to XY or effects. Round 2 mockups F to I (10:08Z) show that; not built yet. **Jay 10:16Z, the direction for now:** the Looper tab stays and becomes the loop's editor and setup (mockup J-looper-tab, 10:14Z), while Play's looper strip is for performing the loop. The tab has a list of loops with a memory gauge; one lane per layer with level, pan, solo, mute and delete; half length, double, reverse, nudge, trim, seam fade, merging layers and the undo history; recording and input setup; and sending a loop to a pad, a clip, a save or resampling. Several loops, mixing per layer and the edit tools are new engine work for the time thread. With it: the Clips tab becomes **Arrange** (clip launching, takes, Capture; scenes and the song later), and the graphical pad editor becomes the first, half-screen sub-page of Sample (Pads, Wave, Chop, Play, Grain, FX).
- **The mixer's hardware controls** (FX thread, 10:09Z; panel in #83, `mpfree-mixer-ui`): while the mixer overlay is open, the data wheel sets the selected strip's volume, Q-Links 1-4 its volume, pan, send A and send B, and pads 1-16 select strips on the page. The panel sizes to whatever area the layout gives it: 10 strips and the master at full width, about 7 if the new Play page's right column stays up. **The app thread proposes:** while the mixer is open, that column shows only the Q-Links (labelled from the selected strip), so there is no second master meter.
- **Per-pad pressure calibration** (synth thread, 08:53Z): a Touch-lab-style page, opened from a Prefs row, that stores a scale per pad. It needs saved settings (storage spec).
- **Effect pages, the Perform FX page and the DJ knob** (FX thread, 14:06Z; code in PR #116, crate `mpfree-fx-ui`; previews in /mnt/project-files/fx/fx-ui/):
  - an effect's page (title, drawings, tiles, presets, its own Q-Link strip) opens over the current page under the top bar, with Back, as the mixer does: from a tap on the selected insert's name in the chain editor, and from a mixer return or master strip's insert row;
  - the Perform FX page (16 pads with lights, the XY pad with Hold, the selected pad's settings) opens the same way from the Perform FX insert on the master; its pads and XY view can also sit in a Play view;
  - the DJ knob is Q-Link 1 on that page (it stops at the middle on the way through), and can be a Play Q-Link too.
  - **The app thread proposes:** the pages use the 168 px Q-Link column instead of a strip of their own, so the frame stays the same everywhere; Play's throws and XY pad are on the Perform engine since round 10, with no layout change.
- **Continue on Steps** (composing tools, step 4; boards A to C, https://claude.ai/artifact/PcySMnxMavNq78EY9LA8j2, pictures in mpfree-claude `composing-tools/project-files/s4-boards/`; approved by Jay "Yes, as drawn" 24 Nov 2026 14:50Z; sample screens `sequencer-steps-continue` and `sequencer-steps-continue-bar`): on a kit track's clip the Steps head has **Continue** (108 px) between the track's name and + Lane. While it is open the head shows, in place of the size, the length and + Lane, the bar the grid shows and what it does in bold ("Bar 5 · land home · fill") left of ‹ 5/5 ›, and the grid's new steps are drawn faint; the **bar strip** takes the timing strip's place (its height): "BARS" over "tap one: page to it", then a tile per bar (its number and job over a small picture of its hits, a row per sound in the grid's colours, the new bars faint, the bar shown outlined in the accent); the **Continue panel** takes the step editor's place (196 px): "CONTINUE · DRUMS" over Style, Bars and Ending (40 px rows with ‹ value ›, the wheel's row marked), "TRIES" over the last 8 (36 px rows, the one heard lit), and "Continue" over the line saying what is heard, then Try again and ‹ Try 2 over Cancel and Done (46 px, Done lit). The Q-Link cards are Similarity, Variation, Activity and Range ("drums: none").

Jay asked for these in the app thread (message of 13 Nov 11:46Z). The ones that change the shared layout are his decision already; each needs this file updated in the same pull request that builds it. Details, causes and file references: mpfree-claude `handoffs/2026-11-13-app-thread.md`.

- **A Mix/FX tab** in the top bar for the mixer and effects, and a **dramatic overhaul of adding effects** to pads, tracks and audio channels. (**+ FX** on the mixer is built; see below. **The Mix tab** is built: section 3a, #353.)
- **BROWSE opens the top-level browser** (projects, samples and the rest), as the browser plan's overlay under the top bar; projects load from it and **save**. A **New project** button starts from a fresh, empty project. **Built** by the project thread ("Add project New, Save and Load", 13 Nov): see section 3 ("Not tabs") and section 6 (BROWSE).
- **Touch targets big enough to hit, or reachable with the wheel and – / +**, everywhere. The rule is in section 1, and the mutes and + FX are built (below); the wheel's outline reaches every control on Play, the Looper tab, the mixer and the chain editor (section 6). The FX page's buttons and the pages with rows still need a pass.
- **More steps and more tracks** in the sequencer, with a clear way to add them (32, 64, 128 steps; more than 8 tracks). (**The steps** are built: the pattern's head in section 4. **More tracks** are built, up to 128: section 4f, #332.)

The screen and the mixer already hold one throw per finger (tests `two_fingers_hold_two_throws_at_once` and `two_throws_held_at_once_each_hold_their_pad`), so what's left is the panel: the device check `touch` (step 3) checks that it reports two fingers.
